## PR — Model-measurement harness, Steps 2+3 (this branch, `conductor/model-measurement-harness-steps-2-3-item-m`, EXECUTED — shipping)

**Superseded/retained note:** Step 1 (per-subagent token attribution) shipped as #129 with its own Spec-walk (see history.md 2026-08-26). This block replaces the original QUEUED draft for Steps 2+3 with what was actually built; the original draft's bullets are preserved below each corresponding item for traceability.

**Restated request:** Build the ability to *measure* a delegated model vs a baseline, without routing any subagent off Opus. Conservative + measurement-first per user direction: Opus stays default, **Sonnet is the only challenger, no Haiku**, nothing routes on faith. Generalizes PR P's auditor-only, measurement-first discipline to the whole subagent fleet.

**Mode:** feature (dev-tooling, not shipped). **Depends on:** Step 1's `tools/model-measure/model_measure.py` (sidecar attribution, reused not duplicated) and `plugins/flow/evals/run_evals.py` (`check_required`, `render_context`, `load_ground_truth`, reused not duplicated).

**Verified before drafting:** `git grep -n "^model:" plugins/flow/agents/*.md` → zero matches (no agent carries `model:` frontmatter today, so nothing to preserve a diff against). `plugins/flow/evals/ground_truth.yaml` has 11 cases, all `auditor`/`plan-critic` fixtures — no `lens-*` fixtures exist.

**Spec-walk:**

*Step 2 — Offline A/B eval* (original draft: *"run the reviewer fixtures (auditor, plan-critic, lens-*) through Opus vs Sonnet and report finding-overlap % + FP-rate + token cost per agent... no swap is applied by the eval itself"*):
- [x] `tools/model-measure/ab_eval.py` scores two saved raw outputs (`<case_id>.opus.txt` / `<case_id>.sonnet.txt`) against `ground_truth.yaml` by importing `run_evals.check_required` — no forked copy of the check logic. *Verified:* `run_ab_eval_evals.py::test_score_case_integrates_ground_truth_and_overlap`.
- [x] Finding-overlap % computed from parsed `ISSUE` header lines (category, case-insensitive Dice coefficient), not string equality on the whole output — handles both auditor's severity-less headers (`ISSUE · Unverified assumption`) and plan-critic's severity-carrying headers (`ISSUE · BLOCKER · Scope drift`). *Verified:* `test_parse_issues_*` (4 cases) + `test_finding_overlap_*` (4 cases, incl. the exact-fraction case: 2 shared of 4 total → 0.5).
- [x] FP-rate computed per model from issues matching no fixture `category` check; returns `None` (not `0.0`) when a fixture declares no category check, rather than fabricating a score with nothing to measure against. *Verified:* `test_fp_rate_*` (4 cases).
- [x] Token cost per model read from `model_measure`'s existing sidecar attribution (`token_cost_by_model`, re-bucketed by model instead of by agent type), not re-implemented. *Verified:* `test_token_cost_by_model_reuses_sidecar_attribution` + `-missing_transcript_no_crash`.
- [x] The live-invocation recipe (render context via `run_evals.render_context`, spawn the fixture's reviewer agent once per model via an Agent-tool `model` override, save raw output to the expected path) is documented in `ab_eval.py`'s module docstring — precise enough to execute without guessing paths or prompts. No code claims to execute it automatically.
- [x] `run_ab_eval_evals.py` (25 checks) runs entirely offline against synthetic saved outputs — zero live model calls, zero network — and is wired into `ci.yml`'s existing `model-measure` job. *Verified:* full local run, all green; CI job updated.
- [x] **No agent `model:` frontmatter changes.** *Verified:* same `git grep` as Step 1, re-run at ship — still empty.
- [x] Lens agents are explicitly out of scope for Step 2 (no fixtures exist to score them against) — recorded as a `roadmap.md` follow-up, not silently dropped.

*Step 3 — Randomized/shadow sampler* (original draft: *"logs (agent, model, tokens, output) per real invocation so paired/aggregate samples accumulate over normal use; single-assignment on read-heavy agents first... a dry-run shows the log line shape + that Opus stays the default assignment"*):
- [x] `tools/model-measure/shadow_sampler.py` provides `recommend_model()` (weighted random Opus/Sonnet pick, default 10% Sonnet, injectable `rng`), `record_sample()` (logs one JSONL line, attributing tokens for that one invocation via `model_measure`'s sidecar lookup — not re-derived), and `aggregate()` (per `(agent_type, model)` sample count + mean output tokens). *Verified:* `run_shadow_sampler_evals.py` (12 checks): fixed-value + statistical checks on `recommend_model`, sidecar-reuse + unmatched-id checks on `record_sample`, multi-bucket + malformed-line checks on `aggregate`.
- [x] `--dry-run` fabricates one end-to-end sample against a synthetic transcript with zero live invocation and zero network/API call, proving the log-line shape. *Verified:* `test_dry_run_cli_no_live_call` + `test_no_network_imports` (greps the file for networking-module imports).
- [x] **This is a recommend/log utility a developer opts into for their own real work — not an interception of any shipped skill's dispatch, and it never swaps a real flow-skill invocation.** Nothing under `plugins/flow/` references it. *Verified:* `test_not_referenced_by_shipped_plugin` (`git grep -l shadow_sampler` restricted to `plugins/flow/` is empty).
- [x] The sample log lives under `tools/model-measure/` and is already covered by the repo's blanket `tools/` gitignore — no new ignore rule, no risk of committing session content. *Verified:* `test_log_path_covered_by_existing_gitignore` (`git check-ignore -q`).
- [x] **No agent `model:` frontmatter changes; Opus stays the default by construction (10% `sonnet_rate`), not by discipline.** *Verified:* same `git grep` as above; `test_recommend_model_default_rate_favors_opus_statistically` (2000-draw statistical check, tolerant of RNG implementation).

*Belt-and-suspenders — risk (1) closure on Step 1's harness:*
- [x] Added `test_concurrent_same_type_invocations_do_not_cross_contaminate` plus a targeted single-invocation lookup (`find_sidecar_invocation`, added during `/simplify`'s efficiency-fix pass) to `run_model_measure_evals.py` (19 checks now): two same-type sidecar spawns with different models sum and attribute correctly, proving sidecar attribution is disambiguated by file (`tool_use_id`), not by conversation position or timing — the property both Step 2 and Step 3 depend on. *Verified:* full local run, all green.
- [x] `/flow:staff-review` (4 lenses) ran post-`/simplify`; fixed 2 correctness bugs it caught in the `/simplify`-added code itself (`aggregate()` silently counting a failed lookup as a zero-token sample; a dropped malformed-meta warning) plus 2 CLI-ergonomics NITs (`--json` dropping `warnings`; a silent no-op on an empty `--session-file`), and applied 2 cheap `roadmap-concrete` findings (an `ab_eval.py` aggregate summary line; a `shadow_sampler.py --aggregate` flag) since both wired already-computed/already-tested logic with no new data path. `run_ab_eval_evals.py` → 25 checks, `run_shadow_sampler_evals.py` → 16 checks. *Verified:* full local run, all green; see history.md for the per-lens breakdown.

**Open risks (surfaced, not silently resolved):**
1. **Per-invocation transcript usage attributable to the spawning subagent vs the parent** — substantially proven for the sidecar (primary) format by Step 1's own evals plus the new concurrent-spawn case above; the only remaining ambiguity (legacy inline format, concurrent overlapping spawns) already degrades honestly to `unattributed` rather than guessing.
2. **Non-fixtured agents (docs, lenses on live diffs) have no mechanical quality comparison** — confirmed no `lens-*` fixtures exist; out of scope for this PR, `roadmap.md` follow-up added.
3. **Shadow sampler sample rate may be sparse** (single-assignment, opt-in) — shipping single-assignment first per the plan's own explicit guidance; revisit if real use shows samples are too sparse to be useful.

**Deliberately deferred (human decision at the plan gate):** the first live A/B run (spawning real Opus + Sonnet subagents against the fixtures) is real cost exposure and was explicitly NOT bundled into this PR — the human approved the harness + synthetic self-tests only, deferring the first live run to a separate, later, explicitly-confirmed step.

**Files touched:** `tools/model-measure/ab_eval.py` (new), `tools/model-measure/run_ab_eval_evals.py` (new), `tools/model-measure/shadow_sampler.py` (new), `tools/model-measure/run_shadow_sampler_evals.py` (new), `tools/model-measure/run_model_measure_evals.py` (new concurrent-spawn case), `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (two more `run:` steps under `model-measure`), `dev-docs/plan.md` (this entry), `dev-docs/roadmap.md` (item M Steps 2/3 marked shipped + follow-ups), `dev-docs/history.md` (ship-time entry). **No plugin artifacts changed** — no version bump, no manifest edit, no CHANGELOG entry, same non-shipped status as Step 1. **Zero agent `model:` changes.**

## PR — SPIKE: agentic design-guidance investigation (this branch, `conductor/spike-designmd-investigation-vercel-agentic-design-guidance`, EXECUTED — shipping)

**Mode:** spike (research — the doc is the deliverable; no code was written to answer the question).
`platform: library` ⇒ `/flow:verify-build` self-skips. Docs-only diff ⇒ mechanical preflight self-skips; `/simplify` + `/flow:staff-review` skipped per spike mode.

**Research question.** What, if anything, should flow learn from Vercel's `design.md`? Extended mid-spike by user direction to: what are leading AI/product companies publishing on how agents produce high-quality **design/craft** output, and how that improves from human feedback — the axis flow's existing research corpus (orchestration, context, reflection, spec-driven loops) does not cover?

**What was built.** Nothing shipped as code. `dev-docs/research/2026-09-design-md-investigation.md` (~420 lines): primary sources fetched live and quoted, the four consumer repos read at HEAD via `gh api` with two token drift-checks, a flow-level-mechanism vs repo-level-content split argued from that evidence, and a recommendation with explicit non-goals.

**What we learned (short form; full doc for the argument).**
- Vercel published **three** artifacts, not one. `design.md` is a rewrite compensating for an agent that *cannot read a codebase* — every flow consumer can, so the relevant post is the other one (`product-design`, in-repo skill + linters + review loop).
- The only large measured result in the survey is about **loading, not authoring**: an available skill went uninvoked 56% of the time and scored *at* baseline (53%/53%), while passive always-present context hit 100%. Measured on framework APIs, not design — extension to design guidance is flagged as inference, with the cheap experiment named.
- **Drift-checked the fleet:** `ripe` and `health-tracker` zero drift, `music-app` drift-proof by construction, `portfolio` (not a flow consumer) has one confirmed doc↔code divergence. **The design docs are working; nothing enforces them; the evidence does not show that anything needs to.**
- Two of Vercel's strongest moves (falsifiable axioms, named anti-patterns) were **already independently invented** in `ripe` and `music-app` — and never written anywhere the next repo could inherit them. Supplying the *shape* is flow's job; supplying the *content* never is.
- Three verified defects in flow's own `designLanguagePath` plumbing (no template, absent from doctor's slot loop, single-string slot hides ~80% of `health-tracker`'s design corpus).

**Recommendation: proceed — but small.** Next real PR is S1+S2+S3 (§5a): add `designLanguagePath` to `/flow:doctor`'s slot-existence loop **and** correct the frontmatter's false "all 33 slots" guarantee (7 of 33 are actually checked); ship `template/base/core-docs/design-language.md` carrying five shape rules and no taste. Explicit **NO** to six things (§5c), each with a deletion criterion (FB-0088) and what would flip it. Routed to `roadmap.md` § Next + § Exploration by this doc's §6; **not yet added to the roadmap** — that is the follow-up PR's job, not the spike's.

**Disposability.** No code to dispose of. The research doc is **kept** as a point-in-time record with a `Status:` line per repo convention and is indexed in `dev-docs/README.md`. It gates the S1–S3 PR; if that PR never happens, the doc still stands as the recorded reason flow did *not* build design machinery.

**Carried in the same PR (doc-currency fixes, both independently confirmed by the human before fixing).**
- Stale `uiSurface: false` claim in `CLAUDE.md:115` and `dev-docs/design-language.md:3` — `flow.config.json` has said `true` since v1.24.0. The design-language doc's *scope* claim (it governs the verify-build HTML report specifically) was correct and was **preserved**; only the `uiSurface` assertion changed, plus a pointer to the two sibling browser surfaces that remain deliberately out of its scope.
- Stale "anthropic.com is egress-blocked" caveat in `dev-docs/research/anthropic-canon-alignment-2026-08.md` (lines 7 + 76). Re-verified live: `200`, title *"Building Effective AI Agents \ Anthropic"*. The caveat is corrected **without** claiming its quotes are now verified — they aren't; what changed is that re-verification is cheap and unblocked, which is stated so a future pass can do it.

**Deliberately NOT done.**
- **No version bump.** The diff touches zero plugin artifacts, and the last three merges to `main` (#125, #136, #138) all sit at v1.36.0. Flagged to the human rather than decided silently — a one-line change if they want it.
- **No `feedback.md` entry.** `/flow:ship-spike` § 4 forbids it: spike conversations are too sparse on user direction to distill reliably. FB-0097 remains the high-water mark, unconsumed.
- **No roadmap edits.** §6 *proposes* routing; the human decides what lands.
- **Did not re-verify `anthropic-canon-alignment-2026-08.md`'s quotes.** Separate, larger job, explicitly out of scope.

## PR — Phase 00: fix two shipped-but-never-loading flow features (this branch, EXECUTED — shipping)

**Restated request:** Two flow features have been advertised since early releases and have never mechanically fired for any consumer. (A) `plugins/flow/rules/{general,plan-discipline,documentation,exploration}.md` — `rules/` is not a Claude Code plugin component (confirmed live below); the four "auto-loading rules" have never loaded from the plugin. (B) `plugins/flow/hooks/default-hooks.json` matches no auto-discovery filename and `plugin.json` declares no `hooks` field, so `claude plugin details` always reports `Hooks (0)`. Fix both, reconcile the drift they let go unnoticed, and add a doctor check that verifies the loader's report rather than disk presence — so this class of bug can't recur silently. This is Phase 00 of the service-agnostic roadmap, explicitly standalone and NOT part of any Codex/Cursor porting work.

**Evidence (live-verified this session, CLI v2.1.237 — one minor version past the handoff's v2.1.141):**
- `grep -ac` over the installed `claude` binary confirms `paths`, `user-invocable`, and `disable-model-invocation` are real SKILL.md frontmatter keys, and confirms the literal string `"The standard hooks/hooks.json is loaded automatically, so manifest.hooks should only reference additional hook files"` and the `rulesDir` call sites (`Managed`/`User`/`.claude/rules` **Project** only — no plugin-root call site).
- Scratch-copied `plugins/flow/` to `/tmp/flow-scratch`, added a skill with `paths: ["**/*"]` + `user-invocable: false` → `claude --plugin-dir /tmp/flow-scratch plugin details flow` skill count went **17 → 18** and the new skill's name appeared in the loader's own list. Confirms 00a's mechanism works, not just that the docs claim it does.
- Same scratch copy: added `"hooks": "./hooks/default-hooks.json"` to `plugin.json` → `Hooks (0)` became `Hooks (1)  PreToolUse` in the loader report. Confirms the declare-a-hooks-field fix works mechanically (used only to validate the option — see Decision 00b below for why it's **not** the recommended path).
- `claude plugin details flow@flow` (no `--plugin-dir`, reading this sandbox's real installed copy) round-trips cleanly against a real marketplace install, confirming the same command is usable both as a standalone plugin-dir smoke test (CI / this repo) and as a doctor check against a consumer's real install.

**Mode:** feature (docs + a small skill-conversion + a doctor-check addition). No gate-machinery code touched (ship/manifest-triage/skip-audit/verify-build/pr-coherence untouched — confirmed by scope, not just intent).

**Scope (in):**

1. **00a — Convert the 4 rules to path-activated skills.** New `plugins/flow/skills/{general,plan-discipline,documentation,exploration}/SKILL.md`, each carrying its source file's existing `paths:` frontmatter (already present in `plugins/flow/rules/*.md` — written in anticipation of exactly this move) plus `user-invocable: false` and a short `description:`. Delete `plugins/flow/rules/` once content moves (a dead, never-loaded directory left behind would violate the no-dead-artifacts bar and re-confuse the next reader exactly as it confused this one). Update the 4 internal `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/rules/plan-discipline.md` / `.../documentation.md` cross-references found by `git grep -n "CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/rules"` (2 self-references inside the moved files, plus `ship/SKILL.md:518` and `doctor/SKILL.md:597`) to the new `skills/<name>/SKILL.md` paths. Run the full eval suite after (`run_plugin_desc_evals.py` globs `SKILLS_DIR.glob("*/SKILL.md")` and the composition lint counts skills dynamically — both should pick up the 4 new files with no hardcoded-count breakage, but this gets verified, not assumed, per item 7 below).
2. **00b — Hooks filename/declaration: ESCALATED, recommendation below.** No plugin.json/filename change (see Decision 00b). Fix the one live doc that blurs the opt-in posture: `plugins/flow/docs/workflow.md:23` lists "default hooks" among unconditionally-bundled components (rules, agents, schema) with no opt-in qualifier — reword to name `hooks/default-hooks.json` as an opt-in recipe consumers merge into their own `.claude/settings.json`, matching `docs/automation-boundaries.md:19`'s already-correct "The plugin registers no hooks by default."
3. **00c — Reconcile `.claude/rules/{general,documentation}.md` vs plugin counterparts: ESCALATED, recommendation below.** Not a literal merge-to-one-file (the two serve different audiences — see Decision 00c) — a one-time content sync of the genuinely-duplicated sections plus an explicit stated sync-obligation so future drift is a decision, not an accident.
4. **00d — Consumer bootstrap path: verification only, not a code change (see Decision 00d finding).** `docs/bootstrap.md` Step 1 (plugin install) already runs before Step 2 (`bootstrap.sh` scaffolding); the 4 rules-turned-skills load from the **installed plugin** with zero per-project copying once 00a ships — `template/base/` has no `general.md.template` etc. and never did, because these were never meant to be copied per-consumer (only `safety.md.template` is, since it requires project-specific `{{SAFETY_PATH_N}}` customization). Verify: a scratch-bootstrapped project with `flow@flow` installed shows all 4 in `claude plugin details flow@flow`'s skill list, satisfying the handoff's verify clause ("all intended rules active by a mechanism that is actually read") without adding `copy_n` lines that would just reintroduce 00c's drift in every consumer project.
5. **00e — Correct every live claim.** Evidence-based file list (superseding the handoff's guess, which named 2 sites — `plugin.json`/`marketplace.json` — that carry no rules/hooks claim today per live `grep`):
   - `plugins/flow/docs/workflow.md:23` — "the portable rules (...)" + "default hooks" (item 2 above covers the hooks half; the rules half gets a mechanism note: delivered as path-activated skills).
   - `plugins/flow/skills/doctor/SKILL.md` Check 3.2 — rewritten under item 6 (00f) below; currently claims reachability it never verifies.
   - `README.md:86` — "4 auto-loading rules" — count and behavior both become true post-00a; light wording pass only if 00a's mechanism note needs surfacing here too (recommend: no change — "rules" is flow's stable product term, `paths:`-triggered skill delivery is an implementation detail).
   - `docs/automation-boundaries.md:17` — "only the auto-loading rules attach" — this is the highest-value fix but it's fixed **by 00a's mechanism landing**, not by editing this line; the line becomes true rather than aspirational. Confirm wording still reads correctly post-fix, but no rewrite expected.
   - `plugin.json` / `marketplace.json` — **finding, not a fix:** live `grep -n "portable\|rule\|hook"` over both returns nothing; the handoff's citation of these two files doesn't correspond to current content. No edit unless a fresh grep at execution time finds something this read missed.
   - Final verify (per the handoff's own clause): `git grep -n "auto-loading rules\|portable rules\|default hooks"` across `README.md`, `docs/`, `plugins/flow/docs/`, `plugins/flow/skills/doctor/SKILL.md` shows no claim the code doesn't back.
6. **00f — Doctor check verifies the loader, not disk.** Rewrite `doctor/SKILL.md` Check 3.2 from an inferred-PASS (based on Section 1 passing) to a real shell-out: `claude plugin details flow@flow`, grep the skill list for each of `general`/`plan-discipline`/`documentation`/`exploration`, `[PASS]` only if all 4 are named in the loader's own output, `[FAIL]` naming which are missing with a reinstall hint, `[SKIP]` if the `claude` CLI isn't on `PATH` (same jq-absence-style carve-out Section 1 already uses, so a broken env can't manufacture a false FAIL). Verify: deleting a skill directory removes it from `claude plugin details`'s output and the check reds — mechanically true given how the command already behaves (confirmed live above), not asserted.
7. **Fan-out + eval sweep (FB-0010).** `git grep -n "CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/rules\|plugins/flow/rules"` before and after — expect zero survivors in `plugins/`, `docs/`, `README.md` after the move (only `history.md`/`CHANGELOG.md` — immutable per-PR logs — are exempted as legitimate historical record; `dev-docs/roadmap.md` is a **living doc** per `dev-docs/README.md` and gets no such exemption). Separately, `git grep -nE '\b17 skills\b'` — currently hits `dev-docs/roadmap.md:336` ("`no-version-token:{skill,agent}-frontmatter` over all 17 skills and 9 agents") — must become 21 once 00a lands, since it would otherwise go silently stale on a live doc, exactly the fan-out class this item exists to catch. Run the full eval suite (`plugins/flow/evals/run_evals.py` or the CI-enumerated set) after 00a's skill move and confirm no hardcoded skill/rule count broke (`run_plugin_desc_evals.py`'s glob-based surfaces, the composition lint's dynamic count).
8. **Docs + version.** `CHANGELOG.md` new entry; `plugin.json` + `marketplace.json` version bump (1.32.0 → 1.33.0, pending a check for intervening merges at ship time); `dev-docs/history.md` entry (this fixes loader-reachability, not a persistence/fallback/error-handling change, so no `SAFETY` marker expected — reconfirm against `.claude/rules/documentation.md`'s criteria at write time); `dev-docs/roadmap.md` § Now reconciled by whichever ship runs next (§4.5/§4.6 of the cloud-workflow plan eliminated the standalone `/flow:land` doc-currency PR in favor of folding reconciliation into the next substantive ship's own Step 5a — not done inside this PR, and not via a dedicated land PR either).

**Scope (out):**
- Any Codex/Cursor host work (§9–15 of the roadmap doc) — explicitly a separate, later phase.
- `tools/flow` CLI, generator, drift lint (Phase 1) — not touched.
- Self-installing `flow@flow` for this repo's own dev sessions (a real alternative for 00c, considered and set aside — see Decision 00c).
- Flipping hooks to on-by-default (a real alternative for 00b, considered and set aside — see Decision 00b).
- Config-driven `paths:` globs for the rule-skills (the queued `dev-docs/roadmap.md:912` item, "activation parity for adopters with non-standard doc names") — a real, related gap (a project using uppercase `PLAN.md` won't path-match `**/plan.md`), but a distinct follow-up, not required to fix "never loads at all."
- A CI-side loader-truth regression gate for flow's own repo (`claude --plugin-dir ./plugins/flow plugin details flow@inline` wired into `ci.yml`) — that's the roadmap's Phase 0e, a natural next step but a different phase than this consumer-facing doctor check (00f).

**Decision 00b — should hooks be opt-in?**
**Recommendation: YES, keep opt-in. Confidence: HIGH.**
**Why:** `hooks/default-hooks.json`'s own header comment already states the intent ("NOT auto-applied — consumers opt-in"), and `docs/automation-boundaries.md:19` already correctly documents this ("The plugin registers no hooks by default") — the opt-in posture is the *existing, stated* design, not a gap. The shipped hook blocks writes to any path matching broad substrings (`*token*`, `*key*`, `*secret*`, etc.) — real false-positive surface (e.g. `token-refresh.test.ts`) that a consumer should consent to, not receive silently on `/plugin install`. It also matches flow's own stated product principle ("Passive over active"). Mechanically, flipping to on-by-default (declaring `"hooks": "./hooks/default-hooks.json"` in `plugin.json`, verified working above) would be the smaller code diff, but the safer default is not always the smaller diff.
**If overridden (flip to on-by-default):** add the `hooks` field to `plugin.json` (or rename the file to `hooks/hooks.json`), extend 00f's doctor check to also assert `Hooks (N)` with `N > 0` in the loader report, and rewrite `docs/automation-boundaries.md:19` + `workflow.md:23` to describe hooks as bundled rather than opt-in. Escalating rather than picking silently per the dispatch instructions.

**Decision 00c — how to reconcile the drifted project-scope copies?**
**Recommendation: one-time content sync + explicit sync-obligation note, not a structural merge. Confidence: MEDIUM-HIGH.**
**Why:** Diffing both pairs shows the overlap isn't uniform. In `general.md`, exactly 3 sections (**Scope discipline**, **Decision tracking**, **Autonomous work guardrails**) are near-identical content independently drifted in both files — real FB-0010 fan-out. The rest of each file is genuinely non-overlapping by design: the project-dev copy carries flow-repo-specific content (FB-0010 Consistency discipline, the dogfooding-specific Workflow discipline section naming `/flow:ship`) that would violate the plugin's project-agnostic quality bar if shipped there; the plugin copy carries generic consumer-facing content (Mode flags, the full `/flow:ship` auto-advance predicate) that doesn't belong in this repo's meta-rules. `documentation.md`'s pair is simpler — same domain, and the plugin's version is strictly more evolved (e.g., accommodates `/flow:ship` writing the entry *before* committing); the project-dev copy is just stale and should adopt the plugin's fields. A structural fix (deleting the project-dev copies and self-installing `flow@flow` for this repo's own sessions, so one skill source truly serves both) is real and elegant, but out of proportion for a "fix the loading bug" phase — it changes how every contributor's session in this repo loads context, interacts with the 00b hooks decision, and isn't asked for by the roadmap doc. Recommend queuing it to `roadmap.md` § Exploration instead.
**If overridden (structural merge via self-install):** requires `.claude/settings.json` `enabledPlugins`/`extraKnownMarketplaces` wiring (both real, confirmed in the installed CLI's string table), and a redesign of what's left in `.claude/rules/*` once the plugin skills cover the generic content — meaningfully bigger than Phase 00's stated scope.

**Decision 00d — bootstrap.sh: verification-only finding, not escalated (stated for the record since it changes the handoff's literal instruction).**
The handoff's wording ("Fix the consumer path... copies only safety.md.template") reads as a call to add `copy_n` lines for the other 4. Doing that would be wrong post-00a: it would recreate exactly the copy-then-drift problem 00c exists to fix, this time fanned out to every consumer project, and it isn't needed — `docs/bootstrap.md` Step 1 (plugin install) precedes Step 2 (scaffold), so the 4 rule-skills are already served by the installed plugin with no copy step, which is why no `general.md.template` etc. has ever existed in `template/base/`. Treating this as a verify-only item (not a code change) directly satisfies the handoff's own verify clause. Not escalating this one — it's evidence-backed, not a taste call — but flagging it explicitly since it changes what "fixing 00d" means.

Also found + fixed one site outside the original grep terms: `docs/first-pr.md:208` named literal old filenames.*

Deleting one of the 4 skill directories reds the check (confirmed live, since the loader's own list is what's grepped). *Verified live: green on the real tree, red when a skill dir was deleted in a scratch copy.*

**Confidence verdicts:**

**Assumption 1:** `paths:` + `user-invocable: false` SKILL.md frontmatter loads and activates exactly like the current `rules/*.md` `paths:` frontmatter (path-triggered context injection). **HIGH.** Empirically confirmed in a live scratch copy this session (skill count 17→18, new skill named in loader output). Residual gap: didn't confirm the *triggering* behavior in a live session (touching `plan.md` and observing the content actually inject) — cheap to confirm at execution/verify-build time, not blocking the plan.
**If it flips:** the docs' own literal claim ("SKILL.md supports `paths:`... binary-confirmed in the skill Zod schema") would need re-checking against actual trigger behavior, not just registration.

**Assumption 2:** Declaring `"hooks": "./hooks/default-hooks.json"` in `plugin.json` is mechanically valid (for the "flip to on-by-default" alternative, not the recommended path). **HIGH.** Empirically confirmed live (Hooks (0) → Hooks (1) in the loader report).
**If it flips:** N/A to the recommended path (00b recommends no plugin.json change).

**Assumption 3:** `docs/bootstrap.md`'s Step 1 → Step 2 ordering is what every real consumer follows (making 00d a non-issue post-00a). **MEDIUM.** The doc states it clearly and `template/base/` structurally has no rule templates to copy, but this is a claim about consumer behavior, which can't be verified from inside this repo — a consumer who runs `bootstrap.sh` first without installing the plugin first would still see zero rules active until they separately install. **If it flips:** add a `/flow:doctor`-surfaced WARN (already covered by 00f's check, which will correctly `[FAIL]`/name-the-gap in that exact scenario) rather than a bootstrap.sh code change — the doctor check is the actual safety net here, not the copy step.

## PR — AB Step 1: harness-weight audit mechanism (this branch, `conductor/ab-attention-budget-harness-weight-audit-fb-0084`, FB-0095, EXECUTED — shipping)

**Restated request:** Build the periodic harness-weight audit *mechanism* (roadmap item AB) — a cadence gate + an inspectable surface inventory over flow's always-loaded and invoked-per-use surfaces, that flags (never prunes) scaffolding that no longer earns its token cost. This is Step 1 of the four-item AB shape drafted in `roadmap.md` § Next "AB"; AB.1b (gate/step-level auditing), AB.2 (dev-doc compaction), AB.3 (context-budget report), and AB.4 (JIT-retrieval/curated-examples doctrine) remain queued as separate follow-up PRs.

**Mode:** feature (tooling — `platform: library`, so `/flow:verify-build` self-skips; non-UI, so security/a11y self-skip; `/flow:audit-coverage` has a Spec-walk to check the diff against).

**Goal:** A stdlib-only, driftless cadence gate (`--audit-due`) and a two-class surface inventory (`--surfaces`) so a fresh-context Explore agent can be spawned, on a schedule, with a documented prompt that flags (not fixes) expired-assumption scaffolding — mirroring the shape of the memory-corpus audit (`plugins/flow/tools/memory/check.mjs`) without literally reusing its counter-based cadence (flow's own linear `main` history is ground truth here, unlike per-project ship invocations).

**Plan-gate history (four escalations, all resolved by explicit human decision — none silently narrowed):**
1. **Phasing** — build only AB.1 this PR; AB.1b/2/3/4 queued. Approved.
2. **Placement** — dev-tooling (`tools/harness_audit/`, mirrors `tools/model-measure/`), not a shipped `/flow:*` skill. Approved — same posture FB-0083 kept M's Step 1 in.
3. **Gate/step-level auditing** — deferred to AB.1b. Accepted: gates need per-gate regression evidence (why was this gate added, is that reason still true) a generic surface-scan prompt can't provide.
4. **Compaction aggressiveness** — deferred; an AB.2 question, not decided here.

**Scope correction from the human at the plan gate (the one change to the drafted plan):** v1's surface set originally excluded full `SKILL.md` bodies on the grounds that they're JIT-loaded, not always-loaded. Correct in principle, wrong in practice for the single most important case: `ship/SKILL.md` is 1,384 lines — the heaviest prompt in the system — and when `/flow:ship` runs, that IS the context window. Extended v1 to include the full body of every shipped `plugins/flow/skills/*/SKILL.md` as a **separate class** (Class B, invoked-per-use — paid per invocation) from the always-loaded surfaces (Class A — paid every session), so the two cost models are never conflated into one misleading total.

**Scope (in):**
- `tools/harness_audit/harness_audit.py` (new, stdlib-only, mirrors `tools/model-measure/model_measure.py`'s shape):
  - `--audit-due`: cadence via `git rev-list --count <marker-sha>..<current-main-sha>` against a stored last-audited SHA (driftless — no decrementing counter, unlike the memory audit's `.last-audit`, because flow's own linear `main` history is ground truth). Interval default 5 merged PRs, matching the memory audit's `AUDIT_INTERVAL`. First run (no marker) is always due; a git failure (no repo, no reachable `origin/main`/`main`, an unreachable marker SHA) degrades to a loud "cannot determine cadence" message and NOT due — never a crash, never a silent guess (FB-0010 silent-skip discipline).
  - `--surfaces`: resolves and prints the inspectable inventory, split into Class A (always-loaded: `CLAUDE.md`, `.claude/rules/*.md`, `plugins/flow/docs/workflow.md`, read whole; plus the aggregate of every registered skill's/agent's frontmatter `description:` across `plugins/flow/{skills,agents}` and `.claude/{skills,agents}` — the part that actually renders into every session's system reminder, extracted from both the plain single-line and the YAML folded-block (`>`/`>-`/`|`/`|-`) forms) and Class B (invoked-per-use: the full body of every shipped `plugins/flow/skills/*/SKILL.md`; project-dev skills under `.claude/skills/*` excluded — not shipped, so not part of flow's own harness weight in the sense this audit cares about). A missing/unreadable file, or a `SKILL.md`/agent file with no `description:`, warns loudly rather than silently dropping the entry. Class A and Class B totals are printed separately and never summed (different cost models: per-session vs. per-invocation).
- A documented audit-agent prompt in `dev-docs/workflow.md` § "Harness-weight audit" (flow-internal dev workflow — not the shipped consumer-facing `plugins/flow/docs/workflow.md`) for a fresh-context `Explore` agent (read-only: Read/Grep/Glob, no Edit/Write), given ONLY the `--surfaces` output — no PR diff, no other session context. Asks three questions per surface (expired-assumption scaffolding; append-only growth per the FB-0078 class; cross-surface redundancy). **Hard-coded, non-negotiable guardrails, verbatim in the shipped prompt (fixture-pinned, not just asserted in this plan):** never flag FB-0010 footgun/incident comments, never flag a curated canonical example as prunable for length (only flag genuinely exhaustive lists), never flag anything marked `SAFETY` or living under `.claude/rules/safety.md`, and "minimal is the bar, not short."
- `tools/harness_audit/run_harness_audit_evals.py` (new) — fixture-driven, covering the mechanical parts only (cadence math against a real throwaway git fixture repo — first-run, below-interval, at-interval, marker-advance, empty-marker, no-git-repo, unreachable-marker-SHA; surface resolution against a fixture repo-root tree — static docs present, folded-block description extraction, agent description extraction, missing-description warns not crashes, full Class-B body present, project-dev skills excluded, missing skills dir warns not crashes, classes never summed in the rendered report; the audit-agent prompt's guardrail text is grepped verbatim out of the real `dev-docs/workflow.md`, so a future edit that drops a guardrail is caught here). The audit agent's actual "does this still earn its cost" judgment is best-effort LLM work like the auditor/plan-critic, not something a deterministic eval can grade.
- `.github/workflows/ci.yml` — new dedicated `harness-audit` job (sibling to `model-measure`'s), running `run_harness_audit_evals.py`. Not folded into the `evals` job's FB-0074-guarded enumeration (scoped to `plugins/flow/evals/`).
- `CLAUDE.md` § 3 "Project-dev infrastructure" table — add a `tools/harness_audit/` row.
- **`.gitignore:10` gotcha, pre-empted (already hit twice during M Step 1):** the bare `tools/` pattern blanket-ignores new files under `tools/` for anything not already tracked. `git add -f tools/harness_audit/` used at commit time rather than rediscovering the detour M Step 1 went through.
- A live dry run of the audit-agent prompt against this repo's real `--surfaces` output (`Agent`, `subagent_type: Explore`) — findings recorded below and in `history.md`, not acted on (no pruning in this PR).

**Scope (out):**
- Any actual pruning/editing of `CLAUDE.md`, `.claude/rules/*`, `workflow.md`, or skill/agent prompts based on this run's findings — each such edit is its own reviewed PR (prompt changes are code changes), not a bulk sweep riding this one.
- AB.1b (gate/step-level auditing), AB.2 (compact `roadmap.md`/`plan.md`), AB.3 (stdlib context-budget/token report — this PR's "chars"/"lines" columns are a cheap triage proxy, not a token estimate), AB.4 (JIT-retrieval/curated-examples doctrine) — all queued separately, unchanged in shape from `roadmap.md` § Next "AB".
- Wiring into the shipped `/flow:ship` pipeline — this stays dev-tooling per the placement escalation above.
- A new `/flow:*` skill wrapping this script — no consumer entry point yet (same reasoning M Step 1 used).

**Live dry-run findings (informational only, not acted on this PR):** spawned a fresh-context `Explore` agent with the exact shipped prompt + this repo's real `--surfaces` output. Class A (`CLAUDE.md`, `.claude/rules/*.md`, `workflow.md`, description frontmatter): no issues flagged — no expired-assumption scaffolding, no append-only growth in the audited files themselves (`workflow.md`'s own anti-pattern section already documents the reverse-chronological-doc failure mode as load-bearing memory, not weight), no cross-surface redundancy between the CLAUDE.md install-surface table and workflow.md's loop-order reference (different jobs). Class B (`ship`/`verify-build`/`doctor` spot-checked, per the prompt's budget guidance): two candidates — (1) `verify-build/SKILL.md`'s dated "as of 2026-05-28" contract section still lists items as "UNKNOWN" that a later §5a section already resolves — stale, unreconciled; (2) `ship/SKILL.md`'s closing "Config slots" table duplicates ~11 of `workflow.md`'s already-documented slot defaults, a two-places-to-update fan-out risk. Neither acted on here — both are natural first candidates for whoever picks up AB.2/the pruning follow-up.

**Spec-walk:**
- [x] `--audit-due` correctly computes "due" from merged-commit count since the marker SHA, defaults to not-due below the interval, and handles a missing/first-run marker file without crashing. *Verified:* `test_audit_due_first_run`, `test_audit_due_below_interval`, `test_audit_due_at_interval`, `test_audit_due_advances_marker`, `test_audit_due_empty_marker_treated_as_first_run` — all green against a real throwaway git fixture repo (not mocked).
- [x] A git failure (no repo, unreachable marker SHA) degrades to a loud message and NOT due, never a crash. *Verified:* `test_audit_due_no_git_repo_degrades_gracefully`, `test_audit_due_unreachable_marker_sha_degrades_gracefully`, green.
- [x] `--surfaces` resolves the v1 surface list (both classes) against real repo paths and reports (not crashes on) a missing/empty surface. *Verified:* `test_always_loaded_includes_static_docs`, `test_always_loaded_extracts_folded_description_only`, `test_always_loaded_agent_description_extracted`, `test_always_loaded_missing_description_warns_not_crashes`, `test_invoked_surfaces_includes_full_skill_body`, `test_invoked_surfaces_excludes_project_dev_skills`, `test_missing_surfaces_directory_warns_not_crashes`, `test_report_separates_classes_and_never_sums_them` — all green; also live-smoke-tested against this repo's own real surfaces (37 Class A entries / 124,711 chars, 17 Class B entries / 438,857 chars, as of this PR's final diff).
- [x] The documented audit-agent prompt explicitly protects FB-0010 footgun comments, curated canonical examples, and SAFETY markers from being flagged, and this is fixture-testable at the prompt-text level. *Verified:* `test_audit_agent_prompt_protects_footgun_curated_safety`, green — greps the guardrail sentences verbatim out of the real `dev-docs/workflow.md`, not just this plan's prose.
- [x] `run_harness_audit_evals.py` wired into its own CI job, not folded into the `evals` job's FB-0074-guarded enumeration. *Verified:* new `harness-audit` job added to `.github/workflows/ci.yml` running `python3 tools/harness_audit/run_harness_audit_evals.py`.
- [x] `CLAUDE.md` § 3 table carries the new row; no other plugin-artifact file changes. *Verified:* `git diff $(git merge-base origin/main HEAD) -- CLAUDE.md` shows exactly the one added row; `git diff $(git merge-base origin/main HEAD) -- plugins/` is empty.
- [x] A live dry run of the audit agent against the v1 surface set produces at least one concrete, evidence-backed candidate finding (or an honest "nothing flagged"). *Verified:* see "Live dry-run findings" above — two Class B candidates, zero Class A candidates, recorded in `history.md`, neither acted on.

**Confidence verdicts:**

**Assumption:** merged-commit-count-since-marker-SHA (via `git rev-list --count`) is a driftless cadence signal for this repo specifically.
**Confidence:** HIGH
**Why:** this repo's `main` is linear (squash-merged PRs per the git log), so `git rev-list --count` between two SHAs on `main` is unambiguous. No consumer-project generalization is claimed — this is dev-only tooling for flow's own repo.

**Assumption:** "frontmatter-description-only" is the correct scope for Class A's skill/agent contribution — i.e., that a skill's/agent's frontmatter `description:` is what actually renders into every session's system reminder, while the full body is JIT-loaded only on invocation.
**Confidence:** MEDIUM
**Why:** inferred from this session's own observed system-reminder shape (every registered skill and agent listed by name + one-line description, full bodies absent until invoked/spawned), not verified against Claude Code's context-assembly internals directly (flow has no way to introspect that). **If it flips:** `--surfaces`' description-globs list is a named, inspectable constant in `harness_audit.py` — cheap to correct if a future observation contradicts it.

**Risks / open questions:**
- No ground truth exists for "still earns its cost at current model capability" — unlike the memory audit's partially-mechanizable fire-log-staleness check, this audit's judgment is pure best-effort LLM work. v1 produces candidates for a human to weigh; it never auto-prunes, by design.
- `plugins/flow/tools/memory/check.mjs` itself has no eval harness (confirmed by inspection — a real gap in the precedent this PR mirrors, not fixed here). Worth a one-line `roadmap.md` § Next note so it isn't lost.
- The two live-dry-run Class B findings (stale `verify-build/SKILL.md` contract section; duplicated config-slots table in `ship/SKILL.md`) are real, evidence-backed candidates but are explicitly NOT fixed in this PR (scope: mechanism only, no pruning) — flagged for whoever picks up the pruning follow-up.

**Files touched:** `tools/harness_audit/harness_audit.py` (new), `tools/harness_audit/run_harness_audit_evals.py` (new), `tools/harness_audit/.gitignore` (new — excludes the local `.last-audit` marker), `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (new `harness-audit` job), `CLAUDE.md` (§3 table row), `dev-docs/workflow.md` (new "Harness-weight audit" section), `dev-docs/plan.md` (this entry + Current Focus bullets), `dev-docs/roadmap.md` (AB entry updated: Step 1 done, AB.1b/2/3/4 queued), `dev-docs/history.md` (ship-time entry with the live dry-run findings), `dev-docs/feedback.md` (FB-0095 entry), `dev-docs/reserved-feedback-numbers.md` (FB-0095 line cleared at merge). **No version bump, no manifest changes, no CHANGELOG entry** — every touched file is non-shipped, matching the M Step 1 precedent (nothing in the plugin install bundle changes).
