Fixed the consumer-facing slot-count guard being a strictly weaker twin of the internal one FB-0079 hardened (line-oriented grep, single-space pattern, doc-ish-only scan targets) by hoisting the wrap-tolerant predicate into a new shared `skills/doctor/lib/slot_count_scan.py`, imported by both `run_merge_status_evals.py` and Check 2.5's own shell block. **Process note (FB-0096):** this branch skipped the plan-approval gate — implemented and eval-verified before presenting a plan — caught it via a self-run `/flow:critique-plan`, and was directed by the human to keep the work rather than revert-and-redo. See the PR block immediately below for the full Spec-walk, and `dev-docs/history.md` 2026-08-27 for the write-up.

## PR — `/flow:doctor` Check 2.5 hoisted to the shared, wrap-tolerant slot-count predicate (`conductor/doctor-check-25-hoist-slot-count-predicate-fb-0079-class`, FB-0096, v1.36.0, IMPLEMENTED — shipping)

> **PROCESS NOTE:** dispatched with an explicit instruction to stop at the plan gate before executing. Did not — implemented and eval-verified the fix first, then ran `/flow:critique-plan` against a plan narrating already-done work, which correctly flagged the gate had never been held open. The human directed keeping the work (green, small, fully reversible) rather than reverting to redo the ceremony, on condition the skip is reported here, in the PR body, and in `dev-docs/history.md` — not papered over. See FB-0096.

**Restated request:** roadmap § Next "`/flow:doctor` Check 2.5 is the line-oriented twin of a guard we just made wrap-tolerant" — the consumer-facing slot-count guard doctor ships is weaker than the internal sweep `run_merge_status_evals.py` runs over flow's own tree (FB-0079 hardened the internal one after a wrapped `all 30\n  slots` slipped a line-oriented grep; Check 2.5 still has every property that produced that miss).

**Mode:** bugfix (`platform: library`-shaped — a doc/eval/shell-script change, no UI surface; security/a11y/verify-build self-skip).

**Goal:** One shared, wrap-tolerant predicate that both the internal eval harness and the consumer-facing doctor check invoke, so the two can no longer silently drift the way Check 2.5 already had.

**Scope (in):**
- New `plugins/flow/skills/doctor/lib/slot_count_scan.py` (stdlib) — `scan_paths(paths, expected, exclude_substrings=())` matching `(\d+)\s+slots?\b` over each file's full text (not per-line), with the same `.sh`-comment historical-narrative exemption the internal sweep already had; plus a CLI (`--expected N path...`, exit 0/1/2).
- `run_merge_status_evals.py` — replaced its private inline sweep with an import of the new module (mirrors the existing `_lint()`/`skill-composition-lint.py` loader pattern); added checks that the module catches a synthetic wrapped fixture, that Check 2.5's SKILL.md text actually invokes `slot_count_scan.py`, and that Check 2.5's real shell block (extracted + executed via `sh -c`) also catches the same wrapped fixture.
- `doctor/SKILL.md` Check 2.5 — resolves the shared module the same way it already resolves `SCHEMA` (CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT-first), invokes it instead of `grep -rEn`, translates its exit code into `[PASS]/[WARN]/[SKIP]`. Added `bootstrap.sh` to the scan-target list.

**Scope (out):** the pre-existing "FB-XXXX slot" false-positive class (the regex matches "0010 slot" inside "FB-0010 slot-count fan-out" prose) — confirmed identical in the shipped pre-fix implementation, not introduced or worsened here, and out of the roadmap item's named scope. Not generalizing Check 2.5 to scan `template/` — that's the separate, already-tracked "Carryover doctor Check 2.5 generalization (template/ files)" cross-track dependency (§ Now "Cross-track dependencies"), a different concept (internal-sweep scan breadth) from this PR (internal/consumer predicate parity).

**Spec-walk:**
- [x] `slot_count_scan.py` catches a synthetic newline-wrapped `all 30\n  slots` fixture (the exact FB-0079 shape) that a line-oriented grep would miss. *Verified:* `slot-count-scan-catches-wrapped-fixture` in `run_merge_status_evals.py`, green; also manually confirmed via direct CLI invocation.
- [x] Check 2.5's SKILL.md text invokes the shared module rather than a private grep (regression guard against silently regrowing the line-oriented gap). *Verified:* `doctor-check-2.5-invokes-shared-scan`, green.
- [x] Check 2.5's REAL shell block, extracted and executed, flags the same wrapped fixture — not just the library in isolation. *Verified:* `doctor-check-2.5-block-extractable` + `doctor-check-2.5-shell-catches-wrapped-fixture`, both green; block executed via `sh -c` with `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT` pointed at the checkout, from a scratch temp dir.
- [x] The internal sweep's existing two checks (vacuous-scan guard, no-stale-count) still pass after the hoist. *Verified:* `slot-count-sweep-scanned-files` + `no-stale-slot-count-in-shipped-surfaces`, green.
- [x] No regression across the full eval suite. *Verified:* all 25 `plugins/flow/evals/run_*.py` harnesses green.
- [x] Manually exercised three cases against the real, extracted shell block (not just the eval): wrapped-stale → WARN with survivor; clean 33-slots doc → PASS; plain non-wrapped stale count → WARN. All matched expectation.

**Tradeoffs discussed:** see `dev-docs/history.md` 2026-08-27 — the FB-XXXX-slot false-positive scope-out, and the live self-referential catch (an early draft comment literally containing "28 slots" tripped the new guard on itself).

**Review pipeline findings (`/flow:staff-review`, run at the ship-time rigor gate since no marker existed for this source):** design-engineer and push-further lenses found nothing applicable (no UI/visual surface in this diff). Staff-engineer (NIT, fixed): the `bootstrap.sh` scan-target addition doesn't close the FB-0079 corollary-3 gap it claimed to — the template never copies `bootstrap.sh` into a consumer project, and it doesn't exist at flow's own repo root either; corrected the rationale comments/history to stop overclaiming, kept the addition as a harmless zero-cost one. Staff-engineer (FOLLOW-UP, applied): the stale `dev-docs/plan.md` symlink-hardening backlog item described a `grep -rEn` mechanism that no longer exists post-hoist; updated it to reflect `Path.rglob`'s (verified, narrower) symlink behavior. UX-designer (2 NITs, fixed): survivor lines leaked Python's `repr()` (a literal `\n` for a wrapped match) and lost their line number — `slot_count_scan.py` now emits `path:line: "N slots" (wrapped across a line break)`; the `SCANNED` field extraction via `awk '{print $3}'` had no validation — now falls back to a visible raw-output note instead of rendering blank on a format drift. UX-designer (1 NIT, fixed): the wildcard exit-code branch swallowed `$OUT`, the one diagnostic that would explain why the scan failed — now surfaced, matching Check 1.4's pattern. UX-designer (1 FOLLOW-UP, deferred — no automated PASS-path regression coverage for Check 2.5's real shell block, only manual verification; not required for this PR, noted for whoever next touches the print format) and 1 low-priority note (exit-code-2 conflates usage-error vs vacuous-scan; addressed with a code comment, not a fix, since doctor's own call site never hits it).

**Version + FB collision — three episodes, each following a rate-limit-window stall.** Episode 1: drafted as **FB-0092**; `#133`/`#136` opened claiming **FB-0092**/**FB-0093**. Renumbered to **FB-0094**, bumped v1.32.0 → v1.33.0; pushed to persist before rebasing. Episode 2, at the first real rebase: `#133` had merged, `#134`/`#135`/`#137` all claimed versions (v1.34.0/v1.33.0/v1.35.0). Re-derived fresh: v1.32.0 → **v1.36.0**. Episode 3, at a SECOND rebase: `#134` and `#135` had both now merged — `#134` independently claiming **FB-0094** for an unrelated concept (memory-effectiveness instrumentation). Renumbered again, **FB-0094 → FB-0096**; re-confirmed v1.36.0 was still the next free minor above `main`'s new v1.34.0 and `#137`'s live v1.35.0 claim, so it held. Full account: `dev-docs/reserved-feedback-numbers.md` audit trail + `dev-docs/history.md` 2026-08-27.

**Files touched:** `plugins/flow/skills/doctor/lib/slot_count_scan.py` (new), `plugins/flow/evals/eval_utils.py` (new, `/simplify`), `plugins/flow/evals/run_merge_status_evals.py`, `plugins/flow/evals/run_role_slot_evals.py` (`/simplify`), `plugins/flow/skills/doctor/SKILL.md`, `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`, `plugins/flow/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `dev-docs/roadmap.md`, `dev-docs/plan.md` (this block), `dev-docs/history.md`, `dev-docs/feedback.md` (FB-0096), `dev-docs/reserved-feedback-numbers.md`.

## PR — Memory-effectiveness instrumentation (this branch, roadmap #3, reduced scope)

> **Scope cut by the orchestrator/human after plan review, 2026-08-27.** The initial plan
> covered three pieces from `roadmap.md` § Next "#3": (a) fire-count instrumentation, (b)
> dead-entry surfacing, (c) fire-rate×recency ranking of injection. Cut to **(b) only**. The
> plan's own analysis surfaced the reason: flow's `check.mjs` only *reports on* the memory
> directory — the harness's native auto-memory loader is what actually reads
> `~/.claude/projects/<canonical>/memory/`, and flow has no index or injection-order hook to
> sort. So (c) could only ever reorder a curation-facing `--list` output over a corpus capped
> at 30 files, not what the model actually sees — real machinery for a cosmetic payoff. (a)
> (a deterministic `--record-fire` writer) existed only to make (c)'s ranking robust to
> freehand date formatting, so it fell with (c). `--dead` is the one piece with a genuine
> downstream consumer: the periodic audit agent at `/flow:ship` § 4b.vi, which previously had
> to eyeball Fire-log dates across up to 30 entries by hand. See FB-0093.

**Restated request:** `dev-docs/roadmap.md` § Next "#3 — Memory-effectiveness instrumentation":
flow's failure-pattern memory corpus is count-capped + mtime-sorted with a manually-annotated
Fire log, and nothing measures whether an entry ever fired. Reduced scope: mechanize the
"dead entry" half of the periodic audit.

**Mode:** feature (code + eval + docs), fully inside `plugins/flow/` — `tools/memory/check.mjs`
is a **shipped plugin artifact** (referenced via `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` from `/flow:ship`,
`/flow:ship-spike`, `/flow:post-merge`), not dev-tooling — corrected from the original dispatch
brief, which cited it as a `tools/model-measure/`-style dev tool. `platform: library` ⇒
`/flow:verify-build` + `/flow:accessibility-review` self-skip; the new eval harness is the
behavioral gate.

**Goal:** `tools/memory/check.mjs --dead [--days=N]` (default 60, matching the number already
committed in `/flow:ship` § 4b.vi's prose) mechanically lists memory entries with no activity —
most recent `Fire log` date, falling back to `First seen`, falling back to file mtime — older
than the threshold. `/flow:ship` § 4b.vi now runs it before spawning the periodic-audit Explore
agent and feeds its output in as a deterministic candidate list; the agent still makes the
archive-vs-keep judgment call (a slow-firing pattern can still be correct), it just no longer
computes the date arithmetic itself.

**Scope (in):**

1. **`plugins/flow/tools/memory/check.mjs`** — a lenient date-extracting parser
   (`extractDates`/`fieldLines`/`parseEntry`) reading the `Fire log` and `First seen` bullets
   already written by the existing freehand `ship § 4b.v` append (no format pinning needed —
   the parser just regex-scans for `YYYY-MM-DD` substrings), a three-tier `lastActivity`
   fallback (last fire → first seen → mtime), and the new `--dead [--days=N]` flag. `--list`,
   `--count`, `--audit-due`, and the default summary are all **unchanged** — this PR only adds
   the new flag.
2. **`plugins/flow/skills/ship/SKILL.md` § 4b.vi** — runs `--dead` before spawning the audit
   agent, passes its output in as input; the "60+ days" language in the prose is now backed by
   a mechanical check instead of being purely descriptive.
3. **`plugins/flow/skills/ship-spike/SKILL.md`** — mirrored bullet updated to match (inherits
   most of its text by reference to ship's § 4b, so only the one summary line changed).
4. **`plugins/flow/docs/workflow.md`** § "Continuous improvement" guardrail 5 — one sentence
   noting the staleness half is now mechanically computed.
5. **`plugins/flow/evals/run_memory_check_evals.py`** (new) — builds a real fixture memory dir
   under `~/.claude/projects/` (required by `check.mjs`'s own path-validation guard) with
   synthetic entries spanning: multi-fire recent, single-fire-long-ago, first-seen-fallback on
   both sides of the default boundary, a `--days=` override case, a legacy entry with no date
   bullets at all (malformed-input path), an empty memory dir, and regression coverage for
   `--count`/`--list`/`--audit-due` (the last one saves/restores the shared `.last-audit`
   marker file, which lives beside the script per-install rather than per-fixture-dir, so the
   eval doesn't perturb real ship-counter state). Wired into `.github/workflows/ci.yml`
   (verified against the repo's own harness/runner join check).

**Scope (out):**

- Fire-rate×recency ranking of `--list`, `--record-fire`, the at-cap lowest-N archive hint,
  and pinning the `Fire log` field's exact write syntax in `rules/documentation.md` — all cut
  per the scope decision above; none of them are load-bearing for `--dead`.
- The known `check.mjs` worktree mis-resolution bug (`roadmap.md:215`) — pre-existing, unrelated.
- A new `flow.config.json` schema slot for the dead-entry-day threshold — hardcoded constant
  (`DEAD_ENTRY_DAYS = 60`), mirroring the existing unconfigurable `AUDIT_INTERVAL = 5`. Moot
  now that the original plan's Decision 1 (days vs. ships) is the only surviving judgment call,
  already resolved calendar-days by the human.
- `post-merge/SKILL.md` § 4a — no change needed; without `--record-fire`, there's no second
  write path to reconcile against it.

**Spec-walk (acceptance criteria):**

- [x] `check.mjs --dead` (default 60d) and `--dead --days=N` return the correct stale-entry
      set on a fixture straddling the boundary. (pinned by
      `run_memory_check_evals.py`'s boundary + override cases; verified green locally)
- [x] A legacy entry with no `Fire log`/`First seen` bullets at all still parses without
      crashing anywhere `--dead` touches it. (pinned by
      `run_memory_check_evals.py`'s legacy-entry case)
- [x] A malformed `--days=` value degrades to the default with a loud stderr warning, never a
      silent wrong-default or a crash. (pinned by the `--days=bogus` case)
- [x] `--count`, `--list`, `--audit-due` behavior is unchanged from before this PR. (pinned by
      the harness's regression cases; `--audit-due`'s shared `.last-audit` marker is
      saved/restored around the test)
- [x] `/flow:ship` § 4b.vi feeds `--dead` output to the audit agent instead of leaving it to
      eyeball dates. (manual read of the `ship/SKILL.md` diff)
- [x] `run_memory_check_evals.py` green locally and wired into `ci.yml`; the repo's own
      harness/runner join check (`ci.yml` "every eval harness is wired" step) passes.
- [x] No slot-count fan-out — no new `flow.config.json` schema slot added; `workflow.md`'s "33
      slots" line is unaffected (verified: schema still has 40 top-level property keys, same
      as before this PR).

**Decision resolved by the human (not re-litigated here):** dead-entry threshold is
calendar-days, default 60 — matches the number `/flow:ship` § 4b.vi's prose already committed
to, avoids inventing a new per-ship, per-entry counter the "N ships" framing would have
required. The ranking-formula question from the original plan is moot (ranking cut).

## PR — `toolchain` manifest kind + a validated toolchain-absence branch in the skip auditor (this branch, KEYSTONE — canonical cloud-workflow plan §4.2 / §5 Step 1)

> **PLAN APPROVED at the gate (relayed via the orchestrator), with four decisions — two of which changed the design.**
> **A — producer IN scope, but MINIMAL:** § 1.2 gets its third self-skip case so a toolchain-absent skip is actually emitted (without it the LEGITIMATE branch classifies a skip nothing produces — dead code). Nothing beyond what makes the feature work end-to-end.
> **B — reason-CONDITIONED, not reason-independent** (the one place the human overrode my recommendation, and the reasoning is right: in gate machinery a too-permissive `LEGITIMATE` branch *is* the silent failure the gate exists to prevent). `LEGITIMATE` requires **both** a toolchain-shaped skip reason **and** a validated ground-truth predicate confirming the toolchain is genuinely absent on this host. Neither alone.
> **C — `blocked` class.** Confirmed. **D — take the `template/` one-line fix.** Confirmed.
>
> **Consequence of A-minimal + B, recorded because it deleted a large amount of previously-planned machinery:** the earlier draft carried a shared *behavior-diff predicate* (`touches_behavior` + a relocated `collect_files` + a new `SOURCE` kind in `file_patterns.py`, plus an agreement fixture) purely to stop the producer self-skipping on a docs-only diff and yielding a **ready** PR. Under B that machinery is unnecessary: branch 4 returns `LEGITIMATE` + a `toolchain` manifest entry for **every** validated toolchain-absent skip, including a docs-only one — which is a **draft**, exactly today's outcome on such a host (verify-build runs → cannot launch → `Unknown` → escalate/draft). Preserving the status quo needs no diff predicate at all. `file_patterns.py`, `collect_files`, `resolve_base` and `DEFAULT_SOURCE_PATTERN` are therefore **untouched**, and the plan is materially smaller than at the gate. Cost: a docs-only PR from a toolchain-less host still drafts rather than going ready — unchanged from today, so not a regression, and relaxing it would mean reversing `ship/SKILL.md:313`, which is out of scope.

**Restated request:** Flow's gate vocabulary can express "there is no runnable target" (`platform: library|none`, `verifyEnabled: false`) but not **"runnable in principle, just not on _this_ host."** A cloud Linux workspace on a `platform: ios` project therefore has two bad exits, both traced live (canonical §2.2): run `/flow:verify-build`, fail to launch, get `Unknown`, and file it as `needs: regression fix` (wrong copy — nothing was exercised); or skip it, and `skip-audit-checks.py:257` classifies the skip **SHOULD-RE-RUN** because `platform='ios'` — the auditor mechanically refuses the only honest skip available. Add the missing noun.

**Mode:** feature (code + evals + docs). `platform: library` ⇒ `/flow:verify-build` self-skips; the diff touches no browser-UI file ⇒ `/flow:accessibility-review` early-exits. The behavioral gate for this PR is the two eval harnesses, both already CI-wired.

**Goal:** a session on a host that genuinely lacks the toolchain its declared `platform` requires can (a) have `/flow:verify-build` **declare** the skip itself rather than burning a launch attempt to reach `Unknown`, (b) have `/flow:audit-skips` classify that skip **LEGITIMATE** only when a **validated** host probe confirms the toolchain really is absent, and (c) have that same verdict mechanically route a `toolchain` entry onto the draft manifest, so the PR opens as a **draft** carrying plain-language copy. `LEGITIMATE` never means "the check may be forgotten"; the entry is what keeps those apart. **No path anywhere in this change makes a host less gated than it is today.**

**Scope (in):**

1. **`plugins/flow/skills/ship/lib/manifest-triage.py` — the new kind.**
   - `KIND_COPY["toolchain"]` (auto-extends `KINDS`, and therefore `add-entry --kind`'s write-time allow-list at `:743`). Copy describes only what exists today — canonical §4.2's draft wording says the checks are "queued for a machine that does", but that queue (`needs-mac-verify` + `/verify-queue`) is §5 Step 3 and out of scope, so shipping it would point a non-engineer at something that does not exist. *means* = "This change needs a build toolchain this machine doesn't have, so nothing here could exercise the behavior — the checks did not run."; *clears_when* = re-run `/flow:verify-build` on a machine that has the toolchain and confirm `overall_verdict` is PASS; *needs_you* = "Nothing I can do from here. Re-run this on a machine that has the toolchain and tell me, or mark the PR ready yourself on GitHub if you accept it un-verified."
   - `CHECK_ONLY += "toolchain"` — only a passing check clears it, never a human's assertion; identical semantics to `verify-build`, which is the property `CHECK_ONLY`'s own comment anticipates. A waiver is recorded but never subtracted, and "waive and ship as-is" is never offered.
   - **`_class_for`: `toolchain` ⇒ `blocked`, verb-independent (Decision C).** The generic fallback would give `ask`, and `ask` + `CHECK_ONLY` renders a numbered question whose only options are "leave it" and "something else" — a question with no answerable answer, plus two strings that are actively wrong here (`:636`'s "I won't mark a **failing build** ready" — nothing was built; and `DEFAULT_THEN`'s "re-run the check … and mark the PR ready once it passes" — impossible on that host). `blocked` is the engine's own definition of this situation and `render_decisions` already routes it to "**Needs you outside this session** — I can't do these, and they can't be waived". `_then` already returns `BLOCKED_THEN` for blocked entries, so the record deliberately carries **no** `then` field.
   - No new `VERBS` entry: the emitted line uses the in-vocabulary `re-run`, and classification is verb-independent for this kind.

2. **`plugins/flow/skills/verify-build/lib/toolchain.py` (NEW) + `/flow:verify-build` § 1.2 — the minimal producer (Decision A).** § 1.2 today self-skips on exactly two conditions (`verifyEnabled == false`; `case "$PLATFORM" in library|none`), so on a toolchain-less `platform: ios` host verify-build **runs**, cannot launch, and returns `Unknown → ESCALATE per FB-0011` (`verify-build/SKILL.md:87`) — and Step 2a.1 reports `"status": "ran"`, which the new auditor branch (under `skip-audit-checks.py:254`'s `if status == "skipped":`) never sees. Without the producer the branch classifies a skip nothing emits.
   - **`toolchain.py`** ships one table and two probes: `PLATFORM_TOOLCHAIN = {"ios": ("xcodebuild", "xcrun")}`, `missing(platform)` → the unresolved binaries, **`absent(platform)` → `True` only when *every* binary in the table is missing**, and `skip_reason(platform)` → the canonical sentence the producer writes and the auditor matches (one owner for the prose contract, not just the data one — a `/simplify` altitude catch). **`absent()` is the predicate both consumers use** — never "`missing()` is non-empty". The difference is the partial-toolchain case: on a Mac with Xcode present but `xcrun` off `PATH`, `missing()` is non-empty while `absent()` is `False`, so the gate **runs** instead of being suppressed on a host that could have performed it. Erring toward running is conservative; erring toward skipping silently drops the behavioral gate. Plus a CLI entry point so § 1.2's bash block calls the same code rather than re-deriving it.
   - **`ios` only, deliberately.** The producer has no `NEEDS-JUDGMENT` escape hatch — a wrong table there suppresses the gate on a capable host, and the entry is `CHECK_ONLY` + `blocked` so no waiver can subtract it. Android makes this concrete: the near-universal build entry point is the repo-local `./gradlew` wrapper, which `shutil.which("gradle")` never resolves, so a fully capable Android machine would self-skip on every run. `tauri` has the same unresolved question (`cargo` is usually present even where the platform toolchain is not). iOS is the motivating and verified case (canonical C1: no `swift`/`xcodebuild`/`clang` in the cloud sandbox). `android`/`tauri` are a named follow-up, admitted only with a wrapper-aware probe and a capable-host fixture — not by widening a dict.
   - **§ 1.2 gains a third `case`**, symmetric with the two that exist: `platform` in the table **and** `absent(platform)` → exit 0 with `skip_reason: "toolchain absent: xcodebuild, xcrun not on PATH"`. That reason is toolchain-shaped by construction, so it satisfies Decision B's needle requirement without the agent choosing wording.
   - **No diff condition, and none is needed** (see the approval note): branch 4 drafts every validated toolchain-absent skip, so a docs-only diff on such a host lands on a draft — today's outcome — rather than becoming ready.

3. **`plugins/flow/skills/audit-skips/lib/skip-audit-checks.py` — the validated branch (Decision B).** Appended as the **last** step inside the existing `verify-build` `status == "skipped"` block, immediately before the `NEEDS-JUDGMENT` fallback. The existing chain keeps the order it has on disk — `:255` `platform library/none` → `:259` `verifyEnabled` → `:263` `doc-only` → `:271` fallback — and no existing branch is moved, re-conditioned or re-worded:
   - **Entered only on BOTH conditions, per Decision B:** `toolchain_required(platform)` is non-empty **and** `_reason_has(skip, *REASON_NEEDLES)` — both via the module's accessors, never a raw-dict membership test or a hand-kept needle list. A toolchain-shaped reason alone proves nothing (forgeable); a host fact alone would excuse any skip on that host. Both, or fall through.
   - **`absent(platform)` ⇒ `LEGITIMATE` + `manifest_kind = "toolchain"`.** The claim is confirmed against the host, and the entry is what keeps `LEGITIMATE` from meaning "ungated".
   - **Not `absent` (at least one binary resolves) ⇒ `SHOULD-RE-RUN`**, naming the binary found (`"skip claims the toolchain is absent but xcodebuild is on PATH"`), `manifest_kind` null.
   - **Anything else falls through unchanged** — no needle, or an ungated platform (`web`, `cli`, unset) ⇒ the existing `NEEDS-JUDGMENT`. Never a mechanical clean pass.
   - **A new `manifest_kind` field on the per-stage output** (`null` for every pre-existing verdict). This is the non-forgeable carrier for item 4: the *engine* decides that a `LEGITIMATE` skip still owes the PR an entry, so a session cannot talk its way out of it. `classify()`'s return becomes a 4-tuple at **every** return site rather than an optional/`len()`-tolerant shape — a forgotten site then raises an unpack error under the harness (fail-loud) instead of silently defaulting to "owes nothing", which is the FB-0010 silent-skip shape.
   - **`--which-from PATH` determinism override** (a file of command names to treat as present), in the family of the existing `--files-from` / `--diff-from` / `--config` overrides the module header already documents as "all overridable for eval determinism". Without it the red fixture is unrunnable on CI's Linux runners, where no Apple toolchain exists — i.e. the red case would silently never execute. Default is the real `shutil.which`; only the harness passes it.
   - **Accepted residual, pinned by its own box.** A reason containing the bare word `none` (e.g. "toolchain absent — none of the Apple tools are present") matches branch 1 first and gets `SHOULD-RE-RUN — skip claims platform library/none but platform='ios'`. Unhelpful, but **safe, and self-healing**: verify-build is in `_AUTO_RERUN`, so that verdict takes `ship/SKILL.md:419` (re-invoke the stage, re-audit once) — the re-invocation is exactly where the producer fires, so the second pass carries the canonical reason, lands on branch 4, and drafts with the right copy. One wasted cycle, never a wrong terminal. Improving the first pass would require relocating branch 1, which changes verdicts on multi-vocabulary reasons and belongs in a PR that can pin the reorder on its own evidence.

4. **`plugins/flow/skills/ship/SKILL.md` Step 2a.3 — the in-flow emitter.** Today's routing table ends with "**All `LEGITIMATE`** → emit a one-line confirmation … and proceed" — which is what would turn a toolchain `LEGITIMATE` into a ready PR with no behavioral gate. It gains a preceding, engine-driven case:
   - **`LEGITIMATE` carrying a `manifest_kind`** → the skip was honest *and* the check still never ran, so it is not a clean pass. Emit through the validated write path, with the **literal** invocation spelled out (`add-entry --kind toolchain --needs re-run …`) rather than a `<placeholder>`: `run_manifest_triage_evals.py:440` harvests producer sites via `re.findall(r"add-entry --kind ([a-z0-9-]+)", src)`, which a placeholder cannot match. Verdict ≠ `READY` ⇒ draft PR through the existing §7a.6 machinery — no change to §7a.6's draft-creation decision or read-back assertion.
   - The "all `LEGITIMATE` → proceed" bullet is re-worded so it cannot swallow the case above ("all `LEGITIMATE` **and none carrying a `manifest_kind`**").

5. **`plugins/flow/skills/audit-skips/SKILL.md` — carrying `manifest_kind` across the fork boundary.** `/flow:ship` Step 2a.2 invokes `Skill("flow:audit-skips")`, which runs the engine **inside a fork** and returns only the `SKIP-AUDIT SUMMARY` text; that summary's shape is fixed by the SKILL's `## Output` block ("Produce EXACTLY this shape") and today has no field for it. Without this item the field never reaches the ship agent and item 4's emitter is unreachable **while every engine-level eval stays green** — the two-halves-in-different-files contract FB-0074 is about.
   - Stage line gains the field: `- <stage>: <LEGITIMATE | SHOULD-RE-RUN> — <reason>[ · auto-resolvable: … ][ · manifest: <kind>]`, echoed faithfully from the mechanical block.
   - `RESOLUTION:` vocabulary gains the case it cannot express: all-LEGITIMATE **with** a `manifest:` field is not "proceed", it is "proceed, K owe the manifest".
   - **The join is checked mechanically, not documented:** a bidirectional assertion in `run_skip_audit_evals.py` — the engine emits the key **and** the `## Output` block names it — so deleting either half fails rather than going quietly green.

6. **Evals — red/green/end-to-end, both harnesses already CI-wired (no `ci.yml` change; its harness↔runner join confirms that).**
   - `run_skip_audit_evals.py`: **GREEN** — `platform: ios`, toolchain-shaped reason, `--which-from` empty ⇒ `LEGITIMATE` + `manifest_kind == "toolchain"`. **RED** — same report and config, `--which-from` listing both binaries ⇒ `SHOULD-RE-RUN`, reason names `xcodebuild`, `manifest_kind` null. **Decision-B pins:** a gated toolchain-less host whose reason has **no** needle ("ran out of time"), and one with a `null` reason, both ⇒ `NEEDS-JUDGMENT` with `manifest_kind` null — a permissive `LEGITIMATE` is what B exists to forbid. **Partial toolchain:** `--which-from` listing only `xcodebuild` ⇒ `missing == ["xcrun"]` but `absent is False` ⇒ `SHOULD-RE-RUN`, so a partially-equipped host is never let off. **Ungated platform** (`web`) ⇒ `NEEDS-JUDGMENT`. **Residual** — a reason containing `none` ⇒ branch 1's `SHOULD-RE-RUN` + null field. **Paired positives (rule 3):** `platform library` and `verifyEnabled:false` still ⇒ `LEGITIMATE`, each with `manifest_kind` null, so the new branch can neither be satisfied by deleting the branches it follows nor over-fire. Plus `toolchain.py` unit cases (`missing`/`absent` across full / partial / empty `--which-from`, and no-op for `library`/`web`/unset) and a § 1.2 prose assertion that **all three** self-skip cases are present — the paired positive that stops the new case being added by replacing an old one.
   - `run_manifest_triage_evals.py`: a `toolchain` row in `TABLE_CASES` (⇒ `blocked`); `add-entry --kind toolchain` accepted at write time and round-tripping through `parse`; a manifest holding only a `toolchain` entry ⇒ `verdict != "READY"` and `in_residual`; `render-manifest` emits its plain-language triple and the rendered block still passes `pr-coherence.py --is-draft true` / fails `--is-draft false`; the entry is **not** waivable and a recorded waiver does **not** subtract it; `toolchain` added to `test_clears_when`'s enumeration; `test_producer_lines`' exact kind list 8 → 9 **paired with an explicit `"add-entry --kind toolchain" in src` assertion** (the equality alone is not the guard — `kinds_seen` unions the `add-entry` harvest with the inline-template harvest, so a template-form bullet would balance it too).
   - **END-TO-END:** the GREEN case's `manifest_kind` fed through `manifest-triage.py add-entry` + `classify` ⇒ `verdict != "READY"`. This pins the **engine-composition** half; it does not prove ship performs the call — that step is agent-followed prose, pinned by `test_producer_lines`' string-presence assertion.

7. **Fan-out sweep — by grep result, not by remembered list (FB-0010).** Four contracts change. Each grep runs over an explicit pathspec chosen so the pass condition is reachable (`dev-docs/`, `CHANGELOG.md` and `research/` are append-only or point-in-time records that legitimately describe the pre-change contract; this plan file contains the patterns in the greps themselves), and each is **paired with a positive** so it cannot go green by deletion:
   - **verify-build's skip enumeration** (a third condition now exists): `git grep -nE "library\|none|library/none|platform=library" -- plugins/ template/ docs/ README.md` returns the live sites it hits today — not zero — **and** every one names the toolchain case. Known survivors: `ship/SKILL.md:20` (the auto-invocation prohibition — the most load-bearing), `:313`, `:480`, `:1154`, `:1204`; `docs/workflow.md:238` (Step 8's readiness predicate) and `:273`; `verify-build/SKILL.md:121`, `:437`; `verify-build/lib/not-tested-checklist.md:80`; `ship-spike/SKILL.md:184`, `:361`; and `docs/automation-boundaries.md:11`, the consumer-facing twin of `ship/SKILL.md:20` that a `plugins/`-scoped grep structurally cannot reach and that `README.md:105` presents as "the honest list of known gaps".
   - **`platform`-slot semantics** (`ios` stops being an inert hint): `git grep -nE "informational hint|do NOT alter verify-build|run the verify pass" -- plugins/ template/ docs/ README.md` still hits the schema + template description sites (not zero — that would mean the description was deleted, not corrected) **and** neither still says `ios` cannot affect verify-build's launch. Survivors: `schema/flow.config.schema.json:91` and `template/base/flow.config.json.example:49` (Decision D).
   - **The kind count 8 → 9**: `git grep -nE '8 (producer|kinds)|of eight|same 13 classifications' -- plugins/` returns no survivor. Wrap-tolerant deliberately — `manifest-triage.py:573-574` splits "six / of eight entries" across a line break, which a line-oriented `six of eight` misses. Survivors: `manifest-triage.py:4`, `:573-574`; `run_manifest_triage_evals.py:21`, `:426`, `:454`, `:614`, and `:143` ("the same 13 classifications" → 14). `:433`'s `len(templates) >= 8` is a floor on inline templates and the new emitter is an `add-entry` invocation, so it stays correct — verified, not assumed.
   - **Ship's record vocabulary**: `git grep -nE 'skip-audit=\[?all-legitimate' -- plugins/` returns **exactly** the four known sites `:321` (the template's closed two-value alternation), `:324` (the worked example), `:426`, `:1113` — not zero, not five — **and** each carries the third value. Without it a toolchain PR records `skip-audit=all-legitimate` in the PR body while that same audit is *why* the PR is a draft.

8. **`dev-docs/roadmap.md` — currency (FB-0043).** Reconcile § Now to v1.32.0 and add one § Now entry naming canonical §5 Step 1. Nothing else touched.

9. **Docs + version.** `plugins/flow/docs/workflow.md` (the toolchain concept — one paragraph); `CHANGELOG.md` (v1.32.0, explicit "Breaking changes: none"); `plugin.json` + `marketplace.json` **1.31.0 → 1.32.0** (1.31.0 was taken by #128 while this branch was at its gate); `dev-docs/history.md` at ship, **marked `SAFETY`** — this changes gate fallback behavior in both directions (a verify-build attempt becomes a declared skip; a skip that fell to `NEEDS-JUDGMENT` becomes `LEGITIMATE`; Step 2a.3's terminal all-LEGITIMATE case is re-conditioned), which `.claude/rules/documentation.md` requires the marker for.

**Scope (out):**
- The Trio-local **placement rule**, the `needs-mac-verify` **label**, `/verify-queue`, and the **orchestrator** (canonical §5 Steps 3–4). Not designed, not stubbed, not referenced as if they exist.
- **No shared behavior-diff predicate** — no `touches_behavior`, no relocation of `collect_files`/`resolve_base`, no `SOURCE` kind in `file_patterns.py`. Decision B removed the need (see the approval note); `file_patterns.py` is untouched.
- **No `android`/`tauri`** in the table. **No new `flow.config.json` slot** — the 32-slot surface stays 32; the schema diff is a `platform` *description* correction only.
- **No `CONDUCTOR_IS_LOCAL`/vendor env-var signal**, though canonical §4.2 offers it as an alternative. `which` is the vendor-neutral fact the env var is only a proxy for, and canonical §4.4 rejects Conductor specifics in plugin artifacts.
- No change to §7a.6's **draft-creation decision machinery**, to `_AUTO_RERUN`, or to the `Unknown`-blocks-ship rule. (§7a.6's *templates and record vocabulary* at `:1065`/`:1113`/`:1154`/`:1204` are edited per item 7 — the section spans `:1036`–`:1240`, so a flat "§7a.6 untouched" would contradict the sweep.)
- **Undeclared `platform` is out of the mechanism, deliberately.** The slot is optional, so an iOS project that never declared it keeps today's behavior exactly: verify-build runs, returns `Unknown`, ship escalates/drafts. Safe, just without the better copy. Autodetecting instead would make a gate verdict depend on a heuristic — the opposite of this PR's premise. A `/flow:doctor` nudge for Apple-shaped repos with no declared `platform` is the named follow-up.

**Spec-walk:**
- [x] `manifest-triage.py` exposes `toolchain` as a first-class kind: `add-entry --kind toolchain …` exits 0 and round-trips through `parse` with `kind == "toolchain"`, while an unknown kind still exits 2. *Verified:* run_manifest_triage_evals.py `add-entry --kind toolchain is accepted at write time` + `its output round-trips through parse` + the existing `bogus` kind still exits 2 — all green.
- [x] A `toolchain` entry classifies `blocked`, renders under "Needs you outside this session", is **not** waivable, and a recorded waiver does not subtract it from `residual`. *Verified:* `toolchain + 're-run' ⇒ blocked` in TABLE_CASES; `a blocked toolchain entry is never waivable`; `a recorded waiver is NOT subtracted (CHECK_ONLY, same as verify-build)`.
- [x] A manifest holding only a `toolchain` entry ⇒ `verdict != READY`, and `render-manifest`'s output still passes `pr-coherence.py --is-draft true` and fails `--is-draft false`. *Verified:* `verdict is not READY — the PR opens as a draft` (BLOCKED) + `pr-coherence on the toolchain block, --is-draft true ⇒ exit 0 / false ⇒ exit 1`.
- [x] Every kind — `toolchain` included — carries a `clears_when` re-check. *Verified:* `test_clears_when`'s enumeration now includes `toolchain`; green.
- [x] **The producer exists and is symmetric:** § 1.2 carries a **third** self-skip case (gated `platform` + `absent(platform)` → early exit with a toolchain-shaped reason) **alongside** the two it already has, neither removed nor re-worded. *Verified:* `producer-case-exists` + the paired `producer-keeps-verifyEnabled-case` / `producer-keeps-library-none-case`. Also executed live: the extracted § 1.2 block skips on `platform: ios` here, falls through on `web`, and prints the unchanged message on `library`.
- [x] **`absent()`, not `missing()`-non-empty, is the predicate:** with `--which-from` listing both binaries ⇒ `absent is False`; listing only `xcodebuild` ⇒ `missing == ["xcrun"]` but `absent is False` (the partial-toolchain host still runs the gate); empty ⇒ `absent is True`. § 1.2's case is conditioned on `absent(...)`. *Verified:* `toolchain-partial-is-not-absent` (xcodebuild present, xcrun absent ⇒ SHOULD-RE-RUN); direct CLI run confirms `absent` for empty, `present` for full and for partial.
- [x] **GREEN:** `platform: ios`, a toolchain-shaped skip reason, no Apple binary on the host ⇒ `LEGITIMATE` **and** `manifest_kind == "toolchain"`. *Verified:* `toolchain-green-legitimate` + `toolchain-green-owes-the-manifest` (manifest_kind == 'toolchain') + `toolchain-green-reason-names-the-host-fact`.
- [x] **RED:** identical report + config on a host where `xcodebuild` resolves ⇒ `SHOULD-RE-RUN`, the reason names `xcodebuild`, `manifest_kind` null. *Verified:* `toolchain-red-should-re-run` + `toolchain-red-names-the-binary-it-found` (asserts the reason text, not just the verdict) + `toolchain-red-owes-no-manifest-entry`.
- [x] **Decision B — a toolchain-shaped reason alone is not enough, and neither is the host fact alone:** on a gated toolchain-less host, a skip whose reason carries **no** toolchain needle ("ran out of time") and one with a `null` reason **both** ⇒ `NEEDS-JUDGMENT` with `manifest_kind` null. A skip whose reason *claims* toolchain absence on a host that has it ⇒ `SHOULD-RE-RUN`. Only the conjunction earns `LEGITIMATE`. *Verified:* `toolchain-hostfact-alone-{unrelated-reason,null-reason}-needs-judgment` (host fact alone ⇒ NEEDS-JUDGMENT) and `toolchain-ungated-platform-{web,unset}-needs-judgment` (reason alone ⇒ NEEDS-JUDGMENT); the RED case covers claim-on-an-equipped-host.
- [x] **END-TO-END:** the GREEN case's `manifest_kind` fed through `add-entry` + `classify` ⇒ `verdict != "READY"` — an honest toolchain skip still produces a **draft**, never a ready PR. *Verified:* `end-to-end-add-entry-accepted` + `end-to-end-honest-skip-still-drafts` — the engine's manifest_kind piped into `manifest-triage.py add-entry` + `classify` yields BLOCKED, not READY.
- [x] Ship Step 2a.3's "all LEGITIMATE → proceed" bullet can no longer swallow a `manifest_kind`-carrying verdict, and the preceding bullet prescribes the **literal** `add-entry --kind toolchain --needs re-run`. *(verify: `test_producer_lines` gains an explicit `"add-entry --kind toolchain" in src` assertion; the 8→9 equality alone would also be satisfied by a template-form bullet.)* *Verified:* §2a.3 gains the `LEGITIMATE · manifest: <kind>` bullet with the literal `add-entry --kind toolchain`, and the terminal bullet now reads "all `LEGITIMATE`, and none carrying a `manifest:` field". Pinned by `the toolchain producer prescribes the VALIDATED write path, not a template line`.
- [x] **The human-facing record can say it too:** ship's `Final-pass reviews:` line and the `## Flow run` row carry a third value, so no toolchain PR records a clean pass while being drafted by that same audit. *Verified:* `ship/SKILL.md:321` template, `:324` example, `:434` record instruction and the `## Flow run` row all carry the third value; grep returns those four sites and no two-value survivor.
