**Deletion criteria (FB-0088):**
- **`toolchain` kind + § 1.2's third case + the Step 2a.3 emitter:** one mechanism — delete together if every supported host gains the toolchain its `platform` declares (a macOS cloud runner becoming the norm), or if canonical §5 is abandoned before Step 3. Tell: the kind is in `KINDS` but no `add-entry --kind toolchain` site remains.
- **`PLATFORM_TOOLCHAIN`:** delete if `platform` gains a first-class per-project toolchain declaration — the table then encodes what the project states directly.
- **`manifest_kind` field:** delete if no `LEGITIMATE` verdict can ever owe the PR an entry. Today exactly one can.
- **`--which-from`:** delete if CI ever runs this harness on a toolchain-bearing host, or if the branch takes an injected resolver instead of probing `PATH`.

**Confidence verdicts:**

**Assumption 1:** `absent()` over a per-platform binary table is sufficient ground truth. **HIGH.** It is the fact `command -v xcodebuild` tests, which canonical §4.2 names as the probe, and it was the observed differentiator in the live cloud repro (C1). Directly observable, needs no project declaration, and under Decision B it is *one half* of a conjunction — a claim can no longer earn `LEGITIMATE` on the host fact alone. *If it flips:* a partial toolchain reads as present ⇒ `SHOULD-RE-RUN`, the conservative error.

**Assumption 2:** routing a `manifest_kind`-carrying `LEGITIMATE` to `add-entry` keeps a toolchain-less ship at least as gated as today, and does not over-fire. **HIGH.** The field is `null` for every pre-existing verdict by construction, so the only behavior that changes is the case this PR introduces; the paired null-assertions pin both directions and the end-to-end case pins the draft. *If it flips:* a consumer keying on "all skips legitimate ⇒ ready" sees a draft where it expected ready — the safe direction, and visible immediately.

**Assumption 3:** `toolchain` belongs in `CHECK_ONLY` and classifies `blocked` (Decision C). **HIGH on `CHECK_ONLY`** (canonical §4.2: "never waivable-to-ready, same as `verify-build`"); **HIGH on `blocked`** now that the human confirmed it — verified that no `ship/SKILL.md` step branches on `verdict == "BLOCKED"` and `pr-coherence.py` carries no kind or verdict coupling. Residual: that read covers this repo, not a consumer's own tooling.

**Assumption 4:** the two eval harnesses are an adequate behavioral gate (no `/flow:verify-build` run). **HIGH.** Both engines are stdlib Python with CLI entry points, both harnesses are CI-wired, and `ci.yml`'s harness↔runner join prevents an unwired harness. Flow's own `platform: library` makes verify-build self-skip — the documented path. The two SKILLs' prose is the part a harness cannot execute; each is covered by a prose assertion.

**Assumption 5:** requiring a **declared** `platform` is an acceptable precondition. **MEDIUM.** The slot is optional, so an undeclared iOS project gets none of this and keeps today's behavior (run → `Unknown` → escalate). Safe but silent. Autodetecting would make a gate verdict depend on a heuristic. *If it flips:* the fix is a `/flow:doctor` nudge, not code here.

**Risks / open questions:**
- **`--which-from` is a trust surface.** It exists because CI has no Apple toolchain, so the red case is otherwise unrunnable and would silently never execute. Passed only by the harness; the default is the real `which`; same posture as the existing `--config`/`--files-from` overrides.
- **`ship/SKILL.md` prose is not executable**, so Step 2a.3's routing is agent-followed. What makes it hard to skip: the *engine* supplies `manifest_kind`, and `test_producer_lines` fails if the emitter site disappears.
- **`PLATFORM_TOOLCHAIN` can age.** Two safety stories: the auditor degrades an unknown platform to `NEEDS-JUDGMENT`; the producer has no such hatch, which is why it ships `ios` alone and why widening is gated on a capable-host fixture.
- **`roadmap.md:624`'s standing `## Flow run` skip-vocabulary follow-up** is adjacent to this PR's record-vocabulary sweep and deliberately left open — a separate item with its own scope, discharged here rather than silently disappearing.
- **Version + shared-doc concurrency.** 1.31.0 was taken by #128 during the gate; this claims **1.32.0**. `plan.md` / `roadmap.md` / `history.md` / `CHANGELOG.md` are shared by construction — append-only docs resolve as "main's copy plus this branch's one new entry".
- **No FB number reserved, deliberately.** No user correction is being synthesized; this executes an approved design. Decision B *is* a human override of my recommendation, so if it proves out as a general rule ("in gate machinery, never a permissive LEGITIMATE branch"), that is worth an FB — reserved before any cross-file reference is written, per protocol.

**Rejected alternatives (for `history.md`):**
- *Reason-independent `LEGITIMATE`* (my recommendation at the gate). **Overruled by the human, correctly.** The argument for it was that the manifest entry, not the verdict word, carries the gate. The argument against — which wins — is that a too-permissive `LEGITIMATE` branch in gate machinery is precisely the silent failure the gate exists to prevent, and it would have had the skip auditor excusing a "ran out of time" skip because the host happened to lack a toolchain. Under B the two options are near-identical for flow's own path anyway, since the producer always writes a needle-bearing reason.
- *The shared behavior-diff predicate* (`touches_behavior`, relocated `collect_files`, a `SOURCE` kind). Built to stop the producer self-skipping on a docs-only diff and yielding a ready PR; **deleted** once B made branch 4 draft every validated toolchain-absent skip, which preserves today's outcome without it.
- *Relocating branch 1 below `doc-only`* for nicer copy on a "platform library" reason. Rejected: changes verdicts on multi-vocabulary reasons; the residual is safe and self-healing.
- *A `toolchainCmd` config slot.* Rejected: grows the 32-slot surface to encode a fact the host answers, and lets a project *declare* its way to a skip.
- *Reusing `verify-build` with new copy instead of a new kind.* Rejected: that copy says the build was exercised and did not do what the plan said — actively wrong when nothing was exercised.
- *A `len()`-tolerant 4th return from `classify()`.* Rejected: a forgotten site would silently default to "owes nothing" — the FB-0010 silent-skip shape, in the engine whose job is refusing silent skips.

**Files touched:** `plugins/flow/skills/verify-build/lib/toolchain.py` (**NEW**), `plugins/flow/skills/verify-build/SKILL.md` (§ 1.2 third case + `:437`), `plugins/flow/skills/ship/lib/manifest-triage.py`, `plugins/flow/skills/audit-skips/lib/skip-audit-checks.py`, `plugins/flow/skills/ship/SKILL.md` (Step 2a.3 emitter + fan-out), `plugins/flow/skills/audit-skips/SKILL.md` (`## Output` contract), `plugins/flow/skills/ship-spike/SKILL.md`, `plugins/flow/skills/verify-build/lib/not-tested-checklist.md`, `plugins/flow/evals/run_skip_audit_evals.py`, `plugins/flow/evals/run_manifest_triage_evals.py`, `plugins/flow/docs/workflow.md`, `plugins/flow/schema/flow.config.schema.json` (`platform` description), `template/base/flow.config.json.example` (same), `docs/automation-boundaries.md`, `plugins/flow/.claude-plugin/plugin.json`, `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `dev-docs/roadmap.md`, `dev-docs/plan.md`, `dev-docs/history.md` (at ship). **Not touched:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `plugins/flow/skills/verify-build/lib/file_patterns.py`, anything under `.claude/`.


## PR — Model-measurement harness, Step 1: per-subagent token + model attribution (this branch, FB-0089, EXECUTED — shipping)

**Restated request:** Build the per-subagent model/token MEASUREMENT harness (roadmap item M), measurement-first. Attribute tokens per subagent + which model ran, from the session transcript. Ships measurement only — no routing change, no agent model swap. This is Step 1 of the three-item M harness drafted in the (now-superseded) "QUEUED" block retained below; Steps 2 (offline Opus-vs-Sonnet A/B eval) and 3 (randomized/shadow sampler) remain queued as separate follow-up PRs.

**Mode:** feature (measurement/tooling — `platform: library`, so `/flow:verify-build` self-skips; non-UI, so security/a11y self-skip; `/flow:audit-coverage` has a Spec-walk to check the diff against).

**Goal:** A stdlib-only report that, given a Claude Code session transcript, attributes total tokens and the model(s) that ran to each subagent invocation (by `subagent_type`), plus a "main" bucket for the orchestrating thread's own turns — so a future routing decision (FB-0083) can be made from measured data instead of a benchmark or a cost argument.

**Live verification done before drafting this plan (per FB-0085 — verify the mechanism against the runtime, not against assumptions):** rather than design blind, this session spawned a real throwaway subagent (`Agent(subagent_type: "Explore")`) from inside this very session and inspected the resulting transcript files on disk. Findings, all directly observed (not inferred):

1. **This environment's actual transcript shape does not match the original queued draft's assumption.** The queued block (retained below) assumed subagent usage lives inline in the main `.jsonl` as `isSidechain: true` records — the format `extract_session.py`'s existing `normalize_turns`/`extract_tool_calls` skip logic already defends against, and presumably the format that logic was written against. **That format was NOT what this session produced.** Instead: the main transcript (`<projects-dir>/<session-uuid>.jsonl`) had **zero** `isSidechain` records after the spawn, and the subagent's entire conversation instead landed in a **sibling file**: `<projects-dir>/<session-uuid>/subagents/agent-<agentId>.jsonl`, with a companion `agent-<agentId>.meta.json` sidecar (same stem, same prefix) containing `{"agentType": "Explore", "toolUseId": "toolu_01AFLcfkg3yUSNAUyo3VA35S", "spawnDepth": 1}`. The `toolUseId` matched, byte-for-byte, the `id` of the parent transcript's `tool_use` block (`name: "Agent"`, `input.subagent_type: "Explore"`) that spawned it — an unambiguous, direct correlation key, no positional bracketing or content-matching heuristic needed for this shape.
2. **Per-record `usage`/`model` fields are NOT safe to sum by naive per-record iteration — they repeat.** In the main transcript, 55 `assistant`-type JSONL records collapsed to only **23 distinct `message.id` values**, each id appearing 1–4 times with an *identical* `usage` block on every repeat (confirmed by direct comparison, e.g. one `message.id` had `output_tokens: 180` on all 4 of its repeated records). Summing every record naively would overcount total tokens by roughly 2–4x. The report must **dedupe by `message.id`** before summing, keeping one snapshot per id.
3. Both formats need support: this session only produced the sidecar-directory format (item 1), so the legacy inline `isSidechain` format is built to the existing code's documented assumption and this repo's consumer base (most run plain Claude Code CLI, not this Conductor-hosted dev environment), not re-verified live in this sandbox. Flagged as a confidence gap below, mitigated by graceful degradation rather than a guess.

**Scope (in):**
- `tools/model-measure/model_measure.py` (new, stdlib-only, imports `find_session_file` / `load_session` from `plugins/flow/scripts/extract_session.py` rather than duplicating session-loading logic — FB-0010 fan-out discipline; the import direction is dev-tool → shipped-plugin-script, one-way and harmless, the same direction `dev-docs/check-index.py` already reads across the repo):
  - Primary path: read `<session-dir>/subagents/*.meta.json` + matching `agent-*.jsonl` files (the sidecar format verified live above); group by `agentType`, aggregate token totals + distinct models + invocation counts; nested subagents (`spawnDepth > 1`) attribute to their own type, not folded into a parent's total.
  - Fallback path: when no `subagents/` directory exists, scan the main transcript for inline `isSidechain: true` spans, bracket each span between a `Task`/`Agent` `tool_use` (capturing `input.subagent_type`) and its matching `tool_result` (by `tool_use_id`), and attribute accordingly. Spans that cannot be unambiguously bracketed (overlapping concurrent spawns) are labeled `unattributed` rather than guessed.
  - Dedupe assistant records by `message.id` before summing usage (finding 2 above) in BOTH the main-thread bucket and every subagent bucket.
  - Graceful degradation: missing transcript, empty transcript, malformed JSONL lines, a malformed/incomplete `.meta.json`, or a `.meta.json` whose `agent-*.jsonl` is missing all produce a clear, loud, counted note in the report rather than a crash or a silently-dropped total (FB-0010 silent-skip discipline).
  - CLI: `python3 model_measure.py [--session-file PATH]`, prints a plain-text table to stdout; exits 0 for the informational/no-data cases above (this is a read-only report, not a gate).
- `tools/model-measure/run_model_measure_evals.py` (new) — fixture-driven harness covering: sidecar-format single/multiple/nested subagents; the message.id-repeat dedup case (reproducing the exact shape observed live above); legacy inline-format bracketing (single span, two sequential spans, ambiguous overlapping spans → `unattributed`); missing/empty/malformed transcript; malformed or dangling `.meta.json`; the main-thread-bucket isolation case.
- `.github/workflows/ci.yml` — a new dedicated job (e.g. `model-measure`, sibling to the existing `dev-docs` job) that runs `python3 tools/model-measure/run_model_measure_evals.py`. **Not** folded into the `evals` job's enumerated list: that job's FB-0074 harness/CI join-check specifically enumerates `plugins/flow/evals/run_*.py` (shipped-plugin regression tests); this harness tests a non-shipped dev-tool, so it gets its own job rather than widening that check's scope or silently exploiting it.
- `CLAUDE.md` § 3 "Project-dev infrastructure" table — add a `tools/model-measure/` row (dev tooling for flow's own subagent-routing measurement, not shipped — see the placement decision below). Also sweep `CLAUDE.md`'s other restatement of the three-surface boundary (How to Work item 2: "Changes under `.claude/` are project-dev infra") to include `tools/` — grepped as the only other prose enumerating the boundary's paths, so this is the full sweep, not a partial one.
- A short usage note in `model_measure.py`'s own module docstring (how to invoke it, what the columns mean) — no new `plugins/flow/docs/*.md` page; this ships as a bare script, not a `/flow:*` skill (see Scope-out and the placement decision below).

**Placement decision (raised by `/flow:critique-plan`, resolved before execution):** this script was originally drafted under `plugins/flow/scripts/` (the shipped-plugin surface, alongside `extract_session.py`). Every existing file in that directory is invoked by at least one shipped `/flow:*` skill; this one, by its own Scope (out) reasoning, has no skill or command that ever calls it — it measures flow's *own* subagent fleet to inform the FB-0083/FB-0084 routing decision, not a capability delivered to consumer projects. Per CLAUDE.md's three-surface boundary ("Respect the three-surface boundary... Never mix"), a script with no consumer entry point that ships to every install anyway is exactly the class that boundary exists to prevent. Moved to a new repo-root, non-shipped location, mirroring the existing precedent of `dev-docs/check-index.py` (a bare Python script with its own dedicated CI job, outside `plugins/`, that nothing in the shipped surface calls). If a future PR gives this capability a consumer-facing reason to exist (e.g., Step 2's A/B eval wants a `/flow:model-measure` entrypoint), that PR can promote it into `plugins/flow/scripts/` then — promoting non-shipped dev-tooling into the shipped surface later is cheap; the reverse (retracting something already shipped to every install) is not.

**Placement detour during execution (found, then partially corrected, then fully corrected — kept here because the middle step was a real misdiagnosis worth recording):** `.gitignore:10` blanket-ignores `tools/` ("Local prototypes + diagnostics (generated; not plugin surface)"), and a plain `git add tools/model-measure/*.py` at the plan-approved path was refused. The **first diagnosis was wrong**: I concluded a file under an ignored path "cannot live" in the repo at all, and relocated twice chasing a directory name that avoided the string `tools` — first to `dev-docs/tools/model-measure/` (still matched, because a gitignore pattern with a trailing slash and no leading slash matches a directory of that name at **any depth**, not just the repo root), then to `dev-docs/model-measure/` (worked, but a `/flow:simplify` altitude-lens pass then correctly flagged it as contradicting CLAUDE.md's own doctrine — `dev-docs/` is declared "dev-tracking only," and a Python module + CI-tested harness is not tracking, it's project-dev infra).

**The actual mechanism, found by checking precedent instead of reasoning from the ignore rule alone:** git only applies `.gitignore` to **untracked** files. A file already in the index is exempt from its own ignore rule forever after — only *adding a new one* needs `git add -f`. This repo already proves it three times over: `plugins/flow/tools/memory/check.mjs` and two `template/stacks/*/tools/preflight/*` scripts all sit under a path segment named `tools` and are tracked and edited normally. Confirmed directly: `git check-ignore -v plugins/flow/tools/memory/check.mjs` returns exit 1 (not ignored) precisely because it's tracked, while a freshly-created sibling in the same directory is correctly still flagged. So the plan-approved location was right all along — **`tools/model-measure/`** — and the fix is `git add -f` once at commit time, not a relocation. Reverted both detour hops; `CLAUDE.md`'s § 3 table row and the "How to Work" boundary-sentence sweep (both originally scoped, both reverted when the first misdiagnosis looked final) are restored to their original form. No other consequence: the CI job name, the eval count, and every Spec-walk criterion below are unchanged, only the path (twice, and then back).

**Scope (out):**
- Any agent `model:` frontmatter change. No routing table. No swap of any kind — Opus stays default everywhere, per FB-0083.
- The offline Opus-vs-Sonnet A/B eval (M Step 2) and the randomized/shadow sampler (M Step 3) — both remain queued, unchanged in shape from the retained block below.
- Any Conductor-orchestrator-side usage ledger (e.g. a cross-workspace `usage.tsv`) — that is a different system at a different layer; this PR's report reads a single session's own transcript only.
- A new `/flow:*` skill wrapping this script. The task is "a stdlib-only report," and the report has no reviewer-gate role (it doesn't feed a plan/audit/ship decision by itself) — wrapping it in a skill now would be surface with no consumer yet (FB-0088: don't build ahead of a use). If the Step 2 A/B eval later needs a slash-command entrypoint, that PR can add one then.
- Re-verifying the legacy inline `isSidechain` format against a live vanilla-Claude-Code (non-Conductor) session — not reproducible from this sandbox (see Confidence verdict 2). Built to spec, fixtured, and gracefully degrading; not blocking on it.

**Spec-walk:**
- [x] The report correctly attributes tokens + model(s) to a subagent invocation when the session uses the sidecar `subagents/<session>/agent-*.jsonl` + `.meta.json` format verified live above. *Verified:* `test_sidecar_single_invocation` in `run_model_measure_evals.py`, green; also live-smoke-tested against this session's own real transcript (correctly found the `Explore` test spawn + 11 `general-purpose` invocations from the `/flow:critique-plan` forks run during this session).
- [x] The "main" bucket (the orchestrating thread's own turns) reports a token total that includes only the main transcript's own assistant records — not a subagent's, and not omitted or zeroed out when subagent invocations are also present in the same session. *Verified:* `test_main_bucket_isolated_from_subagent`, green — asserts an exact known-value match AND disjointness from the subagent total in the same fixture.
- [x] Multiple invocations of the same subagent type aggregate (summed tokens, unioned model set, invocation count > 1); distinct types stay separate. *Verified:* `test_multiple_invocations_aggregate_same_type_distinct_types`, green.
- [x] A nested subagent (`spawnDepth > 1`) attributes to its own type, not its parent's total. *Verified:* `test_nested_subagent_attributes_to_own_type`, green.
- [x] Records sharing one `message.id` contribute to the token total exactly once, not once per repeated record. *Verified:* `test_message_id_repeat_dedup`, green, AND mutation-tested — swapping in a naive per-record-summation implementation makes this exact check fail with the precise 4x-overcount value, confirming the fixture is not vacuous.
- [x] The legacy inline-sidechain format (no `subagents/` dir; `isSidechain: true` records in the main file) attributes correctly when a `Task`/`Agent` `tool_use` → `tool_result` bracket is unambiguous. *Verified:* `test_inline_single_span_attributes` + `test_inline_two_sequential_spans`, green.
- [x] An inline-sidechain span that cannot be unambiguously bracketed (overlapping concurrent spawns) is labeled `unattributed` rather than guessed. *Verified:* `test_inline_ambiguous_overlap_is_unattributed`, green — also asserts the ambiguous tokens do NOT leak into either candidate subagent's bucket.
- [x] Missing session transcript, empty transcript, and malformed JSONL lines are all handled without a crash — each produces a clear, informational report and exit 0. *Verified:* `test_missing_transcript_no_crash`, `test_empty_transcript_no_crash`, `test_malformed_jsonl_lines_no_crash`, all green.
- [x] A malformed/incomplete `.meta.json`, or one whose `agent-*.jsonl` is missing, is skipped with a loud, counted warning — never silently dropped from the total with no trace. *Verified:* `test_malformed_meta_json_warns` + `test_meta_json_missing_transcript_warns`, green.
- [x] `run_model_measure_evals.py` is wired into `ci.yml` as its own dedicated job (not folded into the `evals` job's FB-0074-guarded enumeration, which is scoped to `plugins/flow/evals/`). *Verified:* new `model-measure` job added to `.github/workflows/ci.yml` running `python3 tools/model-measure/run_model_measure_evals.py`; the `evals` job's harness/CI join-check re-run and still reports "25 harnesses, all wired" (unchanged from before this PR — confirms it wasn't accidentally widened to cover this non-shipped harness).
- [x] No agent `model:` frontmatter changed; no routing table added. *Verified:* `git diff $(git merge-base origin/main HEAD) -- plugins/flow/agents/` is empty (0 lines).
- [x] `CLAUDE.md` § 3 "Project-dev infrastructure" table carries a `tools/model-measure/` row, and the "How to Work" item 2 restatement of the three-surface boundary now also names `tools/` alongside `.claude/`. *Verified:* `git diff $(git merge-base origin/main HEAD) -- CLAUDE.md` shows exactly those two edits.

**Confidence verdicts:**

**Assumption:** The sidecar `subagents/<session-uuid>/agent-<id>.jsonl` + `agent-<id>.meta.json` format (same stem, same prefix; with `agentType` + `toolUseId` + `spawnDepth`) is the transcript shape produced when a subagent is spawned in this development environment.
**Confidence:** HIGH
**Why:** Directly observed, not inferred — this session spawned a real `Explore` subagent and inspected the resulting files on disk (see verification narrative above). The `toolUseId` correlation was confirmed byte-for-byte against the parent transcript's `tool_use.id`.

**Assumption:** Records sharing a `message.id` carry an identical, complete `usage` snapshot (safe to dedupe by keeping one), rather than incremental per-record deltas that would need summing.
**Confidence:** HIGH
**Why:** Directly reproduced in this session's own transcript — a `message.id` with 4 repeated records had byte-identical `output_tokens` (and full `usage` block) on every repeat. Documented as an explicit assumption in the module docstring so a future maintainer knows what to re-check if a Claude Code release ever changes this to incremental deltas.
**If it flips:** the fixture pins the current (snapshot) shape; if a future transcript format sums incrementally instead, the eval fixture would need a new case and the dedup logic would need to sum-then-not-dedupe — a visible, testable change, not a silent drift.

**Assumption:** The legacy inline `isSidechain: true` format (no `subagents/` directory) is a real shape produced by at least some Claude Code hosts/versions flow's consumers run, and the `Task`/`Agent` `tool_use` ↔ `tool_result` bracket is the correct correlation mechanism for it.
**Confidence:** MEDIUM
**Why:** `extract_session.py`'s pre-existing `isSidechain` skip logic (predates this PR) implies the format is real and was built/verified against it at some point, but this Conductor-hosted sandbox only ever produces the sidecar format (item 1's assumption) — the inline fallback path is built to the existing code's documented assumption, not re-confirmed live here.
**If it flips:** if the real inline format uses a different correlation signal than a `tool_use`/`tool_result` bracket (or doesn't exist in current Claude Code versions at all), the fallback path degrades to "no `subagents/` dir and no `isSidechain` records found → main-thread-only report" rather than fabricating a subagent table — a visible gap, never a wrong number. Re-verifying on a live vanilla-Claude-Code CLI session is a natural fast-follow, not a blocker for this PR.

**Risks / open questions:**
- Neither transcript shape is a documented public contract — a future Claude Code release could change either format without notice. Mitigation: fixtures pin today's observed shape; both code paths degrade to an honest "no subagent invocations detected" rather than crashing or silently misattributing if the shape moves.
- The ambiguous-overlap case (concurrent subagent spawns in the legacy inline format) is a real gap in coverage, not just an edge case — flow itself dogfoods parallel `Agent` calls routinely (this very session's Handoff Notes reference parallel-lens `/flow:staff-review` spawns). Shipping it as an honest `unattributed` bucket rather than a wrong guess is the deliberate tradeoff; a future PR could add finer disambiguation (e.g. content-matching a sidechain's final message against a candidate `tool_result`) if the ambiguous bucket proves too large in practice. **`/flow:staff-review` (staff-engineer lens) sharpened this**: `attribute_inline_sidechains`'s `flush()` reads the *current* `open_spawns` count at the moment a bracket closes, so a staggered partial-close (three-plus interleaved spans where A closes while B is still open, and more of B's records arrive afterward) attributes those trailing records to B alone rather than flagging them ambiguous — a narrower miss than full N-way overlap, not yet fixture-covered. FOLLOW-UP for whoever next extends the inline-format path: add a staggered-partial-close fixture.
- This PR measures whatever subagents happen to run in a session; it does not (and per FB-0083, must not) draw or act on any conclusion about swapping a model — that judgment call is explicitly deferred to Step 2's A/B eval against ground truth.
- **FOLLOW-UP from `/flow:staff-review` (UX-designer lens):** the report is stdout-only plain text; Step 2's A/B eval and Step 3's shadow sampler will likely want to consume these numbers programmatically rather than re-parsing a fixed-width table. Decide at Step 2's kickoff whether `model_measure.py` grows a `--format json` mode or Step 2 re-derives its own aggregation — flagged now so it isn't decided by drift.

**Files touched:** `tools/model-measure/model_measure.py` (new), `tools/model-measure/run_model_measure_evals.py` (new), `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (new dedicated job), `CLAUDE.md` (§ 3 Project-dev infrastructure table row), `dev-docs/plan.md` (this entry — Spec-walk checked at ship), `dev-docs/roadmap.md` (already repointed, this session, at both citations of the old "PR — Model-measurement harness (QUEUED)" heading — see § Now item 1 and § Next "M"), `dev-docs/history.md` (ship-time entry), `dev-docs/feedback.md` (FB-0089 entry at ship), `dev-docs/reserved-feedback-numbers.md` (FB-0089 line cleared at merge). **No version bump, no manifest changes, no CHANGELOG entry** — every touched file is non-shipped per the Placement decision above (nothing in the plugin install bundle changes), matching the #105 dev-docs-index precedent ("No plugin artifacts changed"). Zero agent `model:` changes.

## PR — D1 Phase 1: experience/ambition lens + design-brief template + pre-prototype orchestrator (this branch, `conductor/d1-phase-1-experience-lens-brief-orchestrator`)

**Restated request:** Implement Phase 1 of `dev-docs/handoffs/d1-prototype-first-gate.md` (FB-0081) — the experience/ambition lens agent (D3, content = FB-0046), the design-brief template (six fields, ~20-second read), and the pre-prototype review orchestrator that fans `auditor` + `plan-critic` + the new lens out over a brief in one extraction, one triaged verdict. Explicitly not Phase 2 (prototype phase, human gate 1, `workflow.md`'s Step 1–2 reorder) or Phase 3 (auto-written technical plan + machine-gate) — those are separate PRs per the handoff's own §13 breakdown.

**Mode:** feature (small; agent prompt + skill + docs + eval, `platform: library` so `/flow:verify-build` self-skips; this repo is `uiSurface: true` but no runnable UI changes here — `/flow:audit-coverage` has a Spec-walk to check the diff against).

**Goal:** Ship the "review the brief before building anything" half of D1's target loop (handoff §3 step 3) as a standalone-invocable skill, so a human (or a later Phase-2 trigger) can get one triaged verdict — auditor + plan-critic + a new quality/ambition lens — before any prototype work starts.

**Scope (in):**
- `plugins/flow/agents/lens-experience.md` (new): two lenses in one agent — (a) experience/product-designer (right problem? ambition ceiling? journey/edge-states/friction/feel), severity `BLOCKER`/`REDIRECT`/`FOLLOW-UP`; (b) push-further-on-quality, inheriting `lens-push-further`'s restraint-first bucket framing (re-scoped for pre-pixel review: `brief-revision`/`prototype-target`/`future-exploration`) with a loud anti-scope-creep guard (never proposes new functionality). `tools: Read, Grep, Glob, Bash`, matching every `lens-*.md` sibling exactly.
- `plugins/flow/docs/workflow.md` § "D1 design-brief template" (new, standalone section — does not renumber the existing 11-step loop, that's Phase 2's job): the six fields from handoff §3 step 2 (Problem / Whose moment / Constraints / Intended scope / Deliberately excluded / Where this pushes past the literal request), a ~80-word-total guideline (documented, not mechanically enforced — nothing produces a brief yet to enforce it against), plus a `/flow:review-brief` row in the skills cheat sheet and the shipped-surface bullet list.
- `plugins/flow/skills/review-brief/SKILL.md` (new, standalone-invocable — passes the "would anyone run it alone?" test the same way `/flow:critique-plan <path>` does): reuses `extract_session.py --mode plan --plan-file` against the brief (one extraction, unmodified machinery), fans it to `flow:auditor` + `flow:plan-critic` + `flow:lens-experience` in one tool message, triages into `decision-required` (→ a numbered answerable-question list, FB-0075's shape — no draft-PR manifest exists at this stage, so this is not gate machinery) vs. captured-non-blocking, and a clean pass states `proceed to the prototype phase` (Phase 2 not yet built, said explicitly). **Not `context: fork`** — it needs full `Agent`-tool fan-out, incompatible with `critique-plan`'s single-agent fork+`agent:` dispatch — so its jq-absence handling follows the **blocking** shape (`MISSING=""`/`exit 1`, run via an explicit Step 0 `Bash`-tool check), not the fork-routed-signal shape, per `run_jq_guard_evals.py`'s policy (jq-absence-handling-2026-06).
- New eval harness `plugins/flow/evals/run_review_brief_evals.py` (stdlib only), wired into `.github/workflows/ci.yml`. Three check groups: (1) mechanical — `extract_session.py --plan-file` renders correctly against two fixture briefs (`fixtures/review-brief/brief_low_ambition.md`, `brief_tight_scope.md`); (2) lens contract — the hand-authored `.expected.txt` fixtures satisfy the handoff's own two named cases (a conformant-but-low-ambition brief carries an Ambition-ceiling finding; a genuinely-tight brief renders both clean states) plus the shared section-header/footer contract; (3) orchestrator composition — `review-brief/SKILL.md` names all three `subagent_type`s and states both triage outcomes (positive-pair per FB-0010, not just the escalation half), and `workflow.md`'s template section names all six brief fields.
- Mark Phase 1's checkboxes done in `dev-docs/handoffs/d1-prototype-first-gate.md` § Spec-walk + update the status banner.

**Scope (out) — explicitly deferred, per the handoff's own PR breakdown:**
- No trigger logic anywhere reads `role`/`uiSurface` (D1 §7's trigger is Phase 2 work).
- No prototype phase, no human-gate-1 mechanics, no `workflow.md` Step 1–2 re-ordering or Step 8/9 "not a third gate" rewrite (Phase 2).
- No auto-written technical plan / machine-gate (Phase 3). No `plan-discipline.md`/`planner.md` changes.
- §9.2 (proportionality "small surface" threshold) is **not** resolved here despite the handoff's Phase-1 checklist saying to — see the open call below.

**Three open calls, presented at the plan gate with recommendation + confidence + justification (per FB-0090), human-approved as-recommended:**

1. **§9.2 proportionality threshold.** Deferred to Phase 2, when the trigger it gates actually exists — pinning a fixture for logic nothing calls yet would be speculative. **Confidence: MEDIUM** (a scheduling call, deviates from the handoff's literal Phase 1 checklist).
2. **~20-second-read numeric target.** ~80 words total across the six fields (≈250 wpm × 20s), documented as a guideline, not mechanically enforced in Phase 1. **Confidence: MEDIUM** (a defensible ballpark, not corpus-calibrated — no real briefs exist yet to calibrate against).
3. **Experience-half severity taxonomy.** `BLOCKER`/`REDIRECT`/`FOLLOW-UP` (matching `plan-critic`, since this half reviews a document the way `plan-critic` does, not a diff the way the other three `lens-*.md` siblings do) rather than the diff-lenses' `BLOCKER`/`NIT`/`FOLLOW-UP`. Surfaced only after `/flow:critique-plan` (self-run before presenting, per the task brief) flagged the initial draft's silent deviation as a `REDIRECT`-worthy Spec violation against the handoff's "match the `lens-*.md` frontmatter + output shape" instruction. **Confidence: MEDIUM** (functionally defensible; a real deviation from a literal instruction, so it was the human's call, not a default).

**Spec-walk:**
- [x] `lens-experience.md` exists, matches `lens-*.md` frontmatter (`tools: Read, Grep, Glob, Bash`), and its output contract is pinned by fixtures demonstrating both the low-ambition-flags and tight-scope-clean cases named in the handoff. *Verified:* `run_review_brief_evals.py` `lens-*` + `lens-frontmatter-*` checks, green.
- [x] The design-brief template (six fields, ~80-word guideline) is documented in `workflow.md`, not wired into the numbered loop steps. *Verified:* `run_review_brief_evals.py` `workflow-template-field-*` checks (all six field names present) + `workflow-has-template-section`.
- [x] `/flow:review-brief` performs one extraction, fans it verbatim to all three reviewers in one tool message, and triages into `decision-required` (numbered question list) vs. non-blocking, never routing to a draft-PR manifest (no PR exists at this stage). *Verified:* `run_review_brief_evals.py` `skill-*` checks (all three `subagent_type`s named, `decision-required` routing present, `proceed to the prototype phase` clean-pass line present, one-tool-message guarantee stated).
- [x] jq-absence on this new skill is handled per the **blocking** policy (`run_jq_guard_evals.py`'s dynamically-discovered jq-using-skill population), not silently exempted or miscategorized as fork. *Verified:* `run_jq_guard_evals.py` green with `review-brief` in the discovered (non-carve-out, non-fork) population — required a Step-0 `MISSING=""`/`exit 1` restructure after the harness caught the initial fork-style guard as wrong for a non-forked, `Agent`-fanning-out skill.
- [x] `run_review_brief_evals.py` is wired into `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and passes; the CI harness/runner join-check (which fails if a harness on disk isn't in the enumerated list, or vice versa) confirmed green locally. *Verified:* manual reproduction of the join-check script against the working tree.
- [x] `dev-docs/handoffs/d1-prototype-first-gate.md` § Spec-walk Phase 1's four checkboxes are checked off, with the status banner updated to "Phase 0 + Phase 1 shipped." *Verified:* diff to the handoff.
- [x] No existing eval harness regressed. *Verified:* all 25 harnesses (24 prior + the new one) re-run locally, all green.

**Confidence verdict:**
**Assumption:** Shipping the orchestrator as a standalone `/flow:review-brief` skill (rather than, say, inline workflow.md prose with no invocable artifact) is the right Phase-1 deliverable shape, even though D1's trigger doesn't exist yet.
**Confidence:** HIGH
**Why:** Directly matches the handoff §13's own framing of PR 2/Phase 1 as "self-contained; delivers the 'review the brief' half," and mirrors the precedent `/flow:critique-plan <path>` already set for standalone document review (FB-0068, v1.18.0) — the same "would anyone run it alone?" test the handoff itself applies to justify agents-not-skills for the lens family.
**If it flips:** If Phase 2's trigger design ends up needing a different invocation shape (e.g. the orchestrator folded into a single combined Step 2+3 skill), `/flow:review-brief` itself doesn't need to change — Phase 2 would just add a caller, the same way `/flow:staff-review`'s fan-out structure was reused here without needing to change `auditor`/`plan-critic`.

**Risks / open questions:**
- The three open calls above are all MEDIUM confidence by design — none is a settled fact, and Phase 2/3 authors should re-read them rather than assume they're closed for good.
- `dev-docs/plan.md`, `history.md`, and `reserved-feedback-numbers.md` are shared across parallel in-flight branches (per the task brief); expect a rebase onto `origin/main` at ship time.

**Files touched:**
- `plugins/flow/agents/lens-experience.md` (new).
- `plugins/flow/skills/review-brief/SKILL.md` (new).
- `plugins/flow/docs/workflow.md` (new "D1 design-brief template" section; shipped-surface bullet; skills cheat sheet row; agent-list mention).
- `plugins/flow/evals/run_review_brief_evals.py` (new).
- `plugins/flow/evals/fixtures/review-brief/*` (new: two brief `.md` fixtures + two `.expected.txt`).

## PR — D2 `role` config slot (this branch, `conductor/d1-role-slot-phase-0-v1`, MERGED #128)

**Restated request:** Implement Phase 0 of the D1 "prototype-first gate" track (`dev-docs/handoffs/d1-prototype-first-gate.md` § Spec-walk Phase 0, D2 in `roadmap.md` § Designer-signal track, FB-0081) — a small, self-contained schema+doc PR that adds an optional `role` slot to `flow.config.json` and unblocks D1's future trigger. Explicitly NOT the trigger itself, the experience/ambition lens (D3), the design-brief template, the prototype phase, or human gate 1 — those are later D1 PRs (Phase 1–3) per the handoff's own "Suggested PR breakdown" (§13).

**Mode:** feature (small; schema + doc surface only — no runnable behavior change, `platform: library` so `/flow:verify-build` self-skips; the diff itself is schema/docs-only with no UI files in it, so security/a11y self-skip on file patterns even though this repo's `uiSurface` is `true`; `/flow:audit-coverage` has a Spec-walk to check the diff against).

**Goal:** Add the optional `role` enum slot to `plugins/flow/schema/flow.config.schema.json` so a project can declare `designer` or `engineer`; have `/flow:doctor` report the resolved value; document the slot (and that D1's trigger will read it) in `plugins/flow/docs/workflow.md`; and pin the shape with a schema round-trip eval. Unset must continue to mean exactly today's classic plan-gate behavior — this PR changes no runtime behavior, only exposes a slot for D1 to consume later.

**Scope (in):**
- `role` property in `flow.config.schema.json`: optional string enum `["designer", "engineer"]`, no `default` key (so an absent slot is a distinct, valid state from either enum value — "unset" must stay mechanically distinguishable, not silently coerced to one role). Docstring states unset ⇒ classic plan-gate behavior and that D1 (linked) is the (not-yet-built) consumer of this slot.
- `/flow:doctor` gains a new check that reads `flow.config.json.role` and reports it: `[PASS] role: designer` / `[PASS] role: engineer` / `[PASS] role unset — classic plan gate (D1 prototype-first trigger not active)` / `[WARN] role: <value>` for any value outside the enum (jq's `.role // empty` read has no schema enforcement behind it at runtime, so a typo'd role must not silently PASS as if resolved). Standard jq-absence `[SKIP]` guard, matching every other config-reading check in doctor.
- `plugins/flow/docs/workflow.md`: document the `role` slot (what it is, its two values, unset default) next to the other config-context slots, plus one explicit sentence that this Phase-0 PR does not wire it to any behavior — D1 is the future consumer. Bump the "32 slots" schema-size literal to "33 slots" (FB-0010 fan-out: `grep -rn '32 slots'` across every doc, update the one live/current-state reference — `plugins/flow/docs/workflow.md:23` — and leave the historical narrative mentions in `CHANGELOG.md`, `dev-docs/plan.md`, `dev-docs/roadmap.md`, and `dev-docs/research/service-agnostic-2026-07.md` untouched, since `/flow:doctor` Check 2.5 itself instructs leaving intentional historical counts alone).
- New eval harness `plugins/flow/evals/run_role_slot_evals.py` (stdlib only, following the `run_status_docs_evals.py` shape): schema shape (property exists, type `string`, enum is exactly `["designer","engineer"]`, no `default` key present), doctor SKILL.md contains the new check + reads `.role`, workflow.md documents the slot and carries "33 slots" (not "32"). Wired into `.github/workflows/ci.yml` alongside the other `run_*_evals.py` invocations.
- Mark Phase 0's checkboxes done in `dev-docs/handoffs/d1-prototype-first-gate.md` § Spec-walk when this ships (the handoff is the tracked execution plan; leaving it stale would misrepresent D1's status to the next agent who reads it cold, per the doc's own "picking this up cold" instructions).

**Scope (out) — explicitly deferred to later D1 phases, per the handoff's own PR breakdown:**
- No trigger logic anywhere reads `role` yet (D1 §7's trigger is Phase 1+ work).
- No `agents/lens-experience.md` (D3), no design-brief template, no pre-prototype orchestrator (Phase 1).
- No prototype phase, no human-gate-1 mechanics, no workflow.md Step 1–2 re-ordering or Step 8/9 "not a third gate" rewrite (Phase 2).
- No auto-written technical plan / machine-gate (Phase 3).
- No change to `plan-discipline.md` or `planner.md` (those move only when the gate itself moves, in Phase 2).
- No enum values beyond `designer`/`engineer` — the handoff's "designer / engineer / …" ellipsis is left open for a future PR to extend; inventing more values now without a concrete consumer would be undirected scope growth.

**Spec-walk:**
- [x] `role` is a valid optional slot in the schema: `jq -e '.properties.role'` returns an object with `"type": "string"` and `"enum": ["designer", "engineer"]`, and no `"default"` key. *Verified:* `run_role_slot_evals.py` `shape-*` checks + `jq -e '.properties.role'` against the live schema file, green.
- [x] A `flow.config.json` with `role` unset, `role: "designer"`, and `role: "engineer"` are all schema-shape-valid (round-trip: write each variant to a temp config, re-read via `jq`, confirm the value comes back unchanged and additionalProperties still permits the rest of the file). *Verified:* `run_role_slot_evals.py` `roundtrip-*` cases (unset / designer / engineer), green.
- [x] A `flow.config.json` with `role: "product-manager"` (outside the enum) is NOT silently treated as either known role — `/flow:doctor`'s new check reports `[WARN]`, not `[PASS]`, naming the bad value. *Verified:* `doctor/SKILL.md` Check 2.11's `case` statement WARNs on any value outside `designer|engineer`; `run_role_slot_evals.py` `doctor-5-warn-on-invalid` + `roundtrip-3-invalid-detected` green.
- [x] `/flow:doctor` reports the resolved role in all three states (unset / designer / engineer) with the exact wording from Scope (in). *Verified:* new Check 2.11 in `doctor/SKILL.md`; `run_role_slot_evals.py` `doctor-*` contract-greps green.
- [x] `plugins/flow/docs/workflow.md` documents the `role` slot, its two values, its unset default, and that D1 is the (not-yet-built) consumer — and does NOT claim any trigger is active yet. *Verified:* § "Project config slots" table row + narrative paragraph, explicit "no skill reads `role` yet" disclaimer; `run_role_slot_evals.py` `docs-1`/`docs-2` green.
- [x] The "N slots" literal is internally consistent: every doc that states a *current* schema slot count says "33", not "32"; every doc that narrates a *past* release's count (CHANGELOG entries, roadmap "shipped at vX" prose, plan.md historical bullets, the point-in-time research doc) is left alone. *Verified:* a repo-wide `git grep -n "32 slots"` (not just the sites this plan anticipated) found **three** live/current-state references, not the one originally scoped — `workflow.md` (updated to "33 slots"), `doctor/SKILL.md`'s frontmatter (updated; Check 2.5's own grep is line-based and can't see across the frontmatter's wrapped "32\n  slots", so it would never have caught this one), and `template/base/CLAUDE.md.template` — the consumer-facing scaffold every new project's `bootstrap.sh` copies, also a live claim, not historical, and also missed in the original plan (updated to "33 slots"). `run_role_slot_evals.py` `docs-3`–`docs-6` + `doctor-7` green. The remaining `git grep` survivors (`CHANGELOG.md`, `dev-docs/plan.md`, `dev-docs/roadmap.md` ×2, `dev-docs/research/service-agnostic-2026-07.md`) are confirmed historical narrative (past-release prose, a point-in-time research snapshot), left untouched per Check 2.5's own documented convention.
- [x] `run_role_slot_evals.py` is wired into `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and passes. *Verified:* added alongside `run_jq_guard_evals.py`; local run green (20/20 checks after the `/simplify` pass below trimmed 4 redundant checks and rewrote a tautological section). Every other eval harness in the repo (24 files) re-run and confirmed green — none regressed from the doctor/workflow.md/schema edits.
- [x] `dev-docs/handoffs/d1-prototype-first-gate.md` § Spec-walk Phase 0's three checkboxes are checked off, with a one-line pointer to this PR, once shipped. *Verified:* checked off + status banner updated to reflect Phase 0 shipped / Phases 1–3 not started.
- [x] No runtime behavior changes for any existing project (schema-additive only; `additionalProperties: true` was already the schema's stance, so an unset `role` round-trips through every existing consumer unchanged). *Verified:* `git grep -n '\.role\b' -- plugins/` returns several hits, but every one besides the new doctor Check 2.11 is an unrelated `role` field — a session-turn's `user`/`assistant` role in `extract_session.py`/`bounding_logic.py`/`harvest_lesson.py`, and a DOM/ARIA `role` attribute in `annotation-layer.html` — not `flow.config.json`'s config slot. Doctor's `.role // empty` read against `flow.config.json` is the only consumer of the new slot.

**Confidence verdict:**
**Assumption:** A closed two-value enum (`designer`/`engineer`), no default, additive-only, is the right shape for Phase 0 — i.e. it will not need a breaking reshape once D1's actual trigger (§7 of the handoff) is built.
**Confidence:** HIGH
**Why:** `dev-docs/handoffs/d1-prototype-first-gate.md` §9.5 rates "D2 role slot is low-risk" HIGH directly ("a new optional config slot defaulting to classic behavior; additive... worst case a naming change"), and the same handoff's Phase-0 checklist is explicit about exactly this shape (enum incl. designer/engineer; optional; unset ⇒ classic). This PR does not decide the trigger's logic (§7's "same trigger as the prototype... OR uiSurface" design fork is explicitly still open and explicitly out of scope here), so nothing about the trigger can invalidate this slot's shape — only add a reader of it.
**If it flips:** If D1's trigger design later needs more than two values (e.g. a `mode: tiny` proportionality signal folded into the same slot instead of `uiSurface`), the fix is additive (extend the enum) or an adjacent slot — not a rename or removal of what ships here.

**Risks / open questions:**
- §9.2 (proportionality trigger threshold) and §9.3 (auto-plan-quality spike, LOW/SPIKE) are both later-phase concerns; this PR takes no position on either and does not need to, since it adds no trigger.
- The enum's ellipsis ("designer / engineer / …" in roadmap.md D2) is deliberately not resolved here — no third value has a concrete consumer yet, and guessing one would be undirected scope growth this PR's own Scope (out) forbids.
- `dev-docs/plan.md`, `history.md`, and `reserved-feedback-numbers.md` are shared across parallel in-flight branches (per the task brief); expect a rebase onto `origin/main` at ship time, not a conflict to avoid pre-emptively.

**`/simplify` pass (4 parallel cleanup agents, applied after execution):**
- **Critical catch (reuse angle → led to a real bug):** `plugins/flow/evals/run_merge_status_evals.py` hardcodes `check("schema-slot-count-32", len(props) == 32, ...)` as a deliberate FB-0010 tripwire — my `role` slot addition would have made this existing, CI-wired eval FAIL. Fixed (`-32` → `-33`, docstring + inline comment trail updated). The same file's `no-stale-slot-count-in-shipped-surfaces` sweep (recursively scans `plugins/` + `template/`, wrap-tolerant) was already GREEN against my `workflow.md`/`CLAUDE.md.template` fixes before I even knew this harness existed — independent confirmation those fixes were correct and complete.
- **Fixed (simplification angle):** `run_role_slot_evals.py`'s "roundtrip" section was tautological — it constructed values from its own `EXPECTED_ENUM` constant and re-asserted membership in that same constant, exercising no real file I/O or schema truth. Rewrote to write real temp `flow.config.json` files, read them back, and check membership against the schema's own live-loaded enum.
- **Fixed (reuse angle):** the same eval's `docs-3`..`docs-6` checks re-implemented (weaker than) `run_merge_status_evals.py`'s existing slot-count-fan-out sweep. Deleted — that sweep is now the single owner of "does any shipped surface contradict the live slot count," including the two sites this PR touches.
- **Fixed (reuse angle, FB-0074 lesson):** Check 2.11 was a jq-reading doctor check not registered in `run_jq_guard_evals.py`'s extract-and-execute harness — exactly the "new guarded check silently untested" gap FB-0074 named. Registered it alongside Checks 2.3/2.4 (same no-`[FAIL]`-branch shape); confirmed live under a jq-stripped PATH.
- **Skipped, noted (efficiency angle):** Check 2.11 calls `jq` twice (validate-parse, then extract). Matches the established idiom in Checks 2.3/2.4 exactly — fixing only this call site would introduce an inconsistency with its neighbors rather than remove one.
- **Skipped, noted for a future roadmap item (altitude angle):** Check 2.11 hardcodes `designer|engineer` in its `case` statement rather than reading the enum from the schema dynamically via `jq`. Legitimate observation, but matches the pre-existing pattern for every other enum slot in doctor (e.g. `platform`) — no doctor check anywhere derives its expected values from the schema at runtime today. Generalizing that is a real repo-wide improvement but is out of proportion for a 2-value Phase-0 slot in an otherwise small PR; left as-is rather than introducing a one-off inconsistency.
- Every eval harness in the repo (24 files) re-run after all fixes: all green.

**`/flow:staff-review` (4 parallel lenses, run after rebasing onto origin/main):** no BLOCKERs. Two cheap NITs applied in-tree: (1) doctor's unset-role message leaked the internal "D1 prototype-first trigger" codename into user-facing terminal output — reworded to plain language; (2) the designer/engineer PASS branches lacked the same "not yet wired" disclaimer the unset branch implied — both now read `role: designer (informational only — no flow skill reads this yet)`. One eval-hardening NIT applied: `run_role_slot_evals.py`'s `doctor-*` checks were unscoped substring matches against the whole `SKILL.md` (could pass vacuously on an unrelated "2.11"/"designer" mention elsewhere); added a `section_after()` helper to scope them to Check 2.11's own block. Design-engineer + push-further lenses: no findings (no visual surface in scope; "nothing to push — surface at ceiling for its scope," respectively). Four FOLLOW-UPs captured, not fixed (see below). All 24 eval harnesses re-confirmed green after the fixes.

## PR — Step 2 concurrent-work sweep is inert on cloud hosts (MERGED #126, PHASE0, docs-only, v1.30.0 unchanged — retained record, superseded as "active" by the FB-0089 block above)

**Restated request:** `plugins/flow/docs/workflow.md` Step 2's "Before activating a queued item — check for concurrent work" sweep leads with `git worktree list`. On any cloud host (Conductor-style: one isolated clone per workspace, not linked worktrees of a shared repo) that command returns only the current workspace, so the sweep silently reads as "no concurrent work" precisely on the hosts where many parallel agents drawing from one queue make duplicate activation most likely. Fix Step 2 so its evidence is remote state — which behaves identically on a local checkout and a cloud workspace — rather than local worktree state, which does not.

**Mode:** feature (docs-only diff to a narrative workflow doc; no runnable target — `platform: library`, so `/flow:verify-build` self-skips; non-UI, so security/a11y self-skip; `/flow:audit-coverage` has a Spec-walk to check the diff against).

**Scope (in):**
- Reorder the Step 2 sweep so remote-state commands (`git fetch` + remote-tracking branches + `gh pr list`) are the primary evidence, not `git worktree list`.
- Add an explicit `git fetch` so remote-tracking refs aren't stale on a long-lived local clone (the current sweep's `git branch -a` silently depends on a fetch having already happened — the same silent-skip shape one level down).
- Retain `git worktree list` but relabel it explicitly as a **local-only supplementary signal** — present when useful (a local multi-worktree setup), never treated as evidence of absence.
- The sweep explicitly names what to do when `gh pr list` fails or `gh` is unavailable/unauthenticated: say so out loud ("gh unavailable — PR check skipped, rely on branch evidence only") rather than silently proceeding as if the PR check had run and found nothing. This closes the same silent-skip shape one precondition down from the bug being fixed.
- Take and record an explicit position on whether this needs an eval fixture.

**Scope (out):**
- No `tools/flow` CLI or executable engine for this sweep. That is the open design fork in the roadmap § Exploration entry landing separately on branch `calgary` ("prose in the SKILL, portable but unenforceable" vs "move it behind the `tools/flow` CLI the service-agnostic roadmap proposes") — this PR does not preempt that decision, it only fixes the correctness bug in the prose version that exists today.
- No changes to `research/`, `dev-docs/README.md`, or `dev-docs/roadmap.md` — reserved to branch `calgary` per the task brief.
- No changes to any other Step 2 "Required fields" prose (Mode/Goal/Scope/Spec-walk/Confidence/Risks/Files/Visual-walk) — out of scope for this fix.
- No host-diversity CI runner — none exists in this repo today, and building one is disproportionate to a doc-prose fix.

**Spec-walk:**
- [x] Step 2's sweep no longer treats `git worktree list` as its first/primary command — the block leads with `git fetch` + a remote-branch listing + `gh pr list`. *Verified:* `plugins/flow/docs/workflow.md` § "Before activating a queued item" now opens with `git fetch --quiet origin`, then `git branch -r --sort=-committerdate`, then `gh pr list`; `git worktree list` moved to the last line.
- [x] The new primary commands return the same *kind* of evidence (remote branches + open PRs matching the item) whether run from a local checkout with linked worktrees or from an isolated single-clone cloud workspace — i.e., they do not depend on `git worktree list`'s local-only view to surface a hit. *Verified:* this branch's own cloud workspace already demonstrated the failure mode being fixed — `git worktree list` → self only, `git fetch`/`git branch -r`/`gh pr list` → full remote picture, per the 2026-08-22 repro cited in Current Focus above. No further fixture needed — see the eval-fixture decision below.
- [x] `git worktree list` is retained in the sweep but is explicitly labeled local-only / not evidence of absence, so a future reader can't mistake an empty result for "nothing concurrent." *Verified:* the line's trailing comment reads `LOCAL-ONLY supplement: ... an empty/self-only result is NOT evidence of no concurrent work — see the note above`.
- [x] No other doc or skill in the plugin duplicates the old `git worktree list`-first sweep (FB-0010 fan-out check). *Verified:* `git grep -n "git worktree list" -- plugins/` returns exactly one hit, in the fixed block.
- [x] Eval-fixture need is decided, not skipped (see below), and the decision is recorded in `dev-docs/history.md` at ship time. *Verified:* decision recorded below and in the history.md entry.
- [x] The sweep says explicitly what to do when `gh pr list` fails or `gh` is missing/unauthenticated (name it out loud, don't silently treat it as "no open PRs"). *Verified:* the `gh pr list` line now reads `gh pr list ... || echo "gh unavailable — PR check skipped, rely on branch evidence only"`, and the paragraph below the block restates the same rule in prose.

**Eval-fixture decision (decided now, per the task brief's explicit ask): NO new eval fixture in this PR.** Reasoning: every eval harness in this repo (`run_scratch_isolation_evals.py`, `run_root_anchor_evals.py`, etc.) pins an *executable* engine — a `lib/*.py` module or a `!`-block extracted and actually run from a `SKILL.md`. Step 2's sweep has neither: it is narrative prose inside `workflow.md`, read and manually run by the planning agent, with no extraction point any harness targets today. Two options exist to fixture it anyway, and both are wrong for this PR: (a) build an extraction+execution harness for this prose block, which *is* the `tools/flow` CLI design fork branch `calgary`'s roadmap entry is explicitly still deciding — building it here would preempt that decision by accident; (b) assert on the doc's string content only (e.g., "workflow.md contains `git fetch`"), which cannot prove the sweep behaves identically across hosts — it can only prove the right words appear, the exact "grep the value, not the behavior" anti-pattern `.claude/rules/general.md`'s FB-0010 section warns against. The actual cross-host evidence — that `git worktree list` alone goes empty on a cloud host while the remote commands don't — is already established by live reproduction (2026-08-22, cited above) and does not need a synthetic fixture to replay something already observed directly. If this sweep later gains an executable engine (the CLI fork resolving toward "yes"), that engine gets fixtures then, same as every other executable surface in this repo.

**Confidence verdict:**
**Assumption:** The fix (remote-state-first sweep, with an explicit named fallback when `gh` is unavailable) behaves identically — same evidence, same failure mode when it fails — on a local checkout and a cloud workspace.
**Confidence:** HIGH
**Why:** `git fetch` and remote-tracking branch listing are already confirmed working in this exact cloud workspace (this session ran them successfully above) and carry no dependency on the local worktree table that fails here. The one real gap a first draft of this plan left open — `gh pr list` silently degrading to "no open PRs" when `gh` is missing/unauthenticated, the same silent-skip shape one precondition down — is closed by naming that fallback explicitly in Scope (in) and Spec-walk above, so "identical behavior everywhere" now means identical including on the failure path, not just the happy path.
**If it flips:** If some host makes even `git fetch`/`gh pr list` unreliable in a way distinct from "gh absent" (not observed, not expected), the sweep would need a documented behavior for that case too — same fix shape, named explicitly rather than silently degrading.

**Risks / open questions:**
- The long-term home for this sweep (prose vs behind a `tools/flow` CLI) is an open roadmap fork owned by branch `calgary`; this PR takes the position that the fix belongs in prose *now* (fastest correctness fix, zero new infrastructure) and that migrating it behind a CLI is a natural follow-up once that CLI exists — not a blocker on this fix.
- Because this is prose, not shipped skill code, the `gh`-unavailable fallback is advisory (a human/agent following the doc) rather than mechanically enforced — it cannot `exit 1` the way a shipped skill's bash block can. That's consistent with everything else in this doc section (the whole sweep is advisory prose today); enforcement becomes mechanical only if/when this sweep gets an executable engine (the CLI fork).
- `git branch -r --sort=-committerdate` after `git fetch` reflects remote-tracking refs repo-wide, not scoped to any particular queued item — a human/agent still has to eyeball the output for a match, same as today; this fix does not add filtering.

**Files touched:** `plugins/flow/docs/workflow.md` (Step 2 sweep block only), `dev-docs/plan.md` (this entry), `dev-docs/history.md` (entry at ship time, after execution).
