## PR — A draft PR is a last resort, not a deliverable: `/flow:ship` manifest resolution + an answerable hand-off (v1.23.0, shipped #85, `9efa9a4`, FB-0075)

**Restated request:** Across repos (music-app, ripe, flow), sessions keep ending with the agent handing back a **draft PR** as if that were a productive result. The user is not an engineer: a draft is not something they can act on, so every one costs a round-trip of "now go fix it." What they need instead is one of exactly two things — (a) a **blocking decision they can actually make**, presented so that answering it resolves the draft, or (b) if the resolution is high-confidence (or the pipeline just needs re-running), **the agent does it** and hands back something actionable.

**Revision note:** this block is revision 3. Rev 1 was rewritten after `/flow:critique-plan` returned 2 BLOCKERs (a stop-before-PR third human gate violating the two-gate thesis; a resolution-ordering incoherence with §7a's HEAD-SHA freshness check). Rev 2 was rewritten after `/flow:audit-plan` returned 4 issues that invalidated the central claim — the classification table was keyed on kind tags five producer sites do not emit, on a `needs:` vocabulary four sites already violate, and it treated `rigor` as auto-resolvable when a `rigor` entry exists *only after* a re-run was already tried. Correcting those shrank the auto-resolve claim from "seven sources" to **one**, and moved the PR's centre of gravity from "auto-resolve more" to "make every entry answerable." That is the honest shape.

**Evidence from the repos the user named (gathered at the audit gate — rev-7 ISSUE 4 correctly flagged that the original diagnosis was derived from flow's source alone, never from a single real draft PR).** Of music-app's 8 PRs, **4 opened as drafts and were later flipped ready**; ripe adds a 5th. All five carry a `## Flow run` table, so all five are flow-shipped. The round-trip cost the user is describing, measured: **47 min, 65 min, 5 h, 11 h, and 13 days** from draft-open to ready. Recovered kind distribution across ~6 manifest items (from the surviving `## Flow run` prose):

| kind | occurrences | the actual manifest item, and how it was resolved |
|---|---|---|
| `skip-audit` (visual-verification) | 2 | PR #4: stopped firing once a `uiFilePatterns` **misconfiguration** was fixed at the root. PR #7: **waived un-performed** (on-device verification impossible in the Simulator) |
| `rigor` (stale staff-review marker) | 1 | PR #3's **sole** blocker — resolved by re-running staff-review on the final tree. This is the case #81 (`6fb5766`) now automates. #81 landed **2026-07-28 02:54Z**; PR #4's ship ran ~4 h later on a host with the installed release **flow 1.21.0** (which ends at #80), so the fix did not fail — it had not shipped |
| `visual-deliverable` | 1 | PR #4, same root cause as its skip-audit item: the `uiFilePatterns` fix made `visual_significant` correctly `false` |
| `coverage` (undeclared behaviors) | 1 | PR #4: 5 behaviors declared retroactively in the plan doc, then **human-waived** (no behavioral gate — `verifyEnabled: false`) |
| `a11y` `[decision-required]` | 1 | PR #7: **waived un-performed** (two view-state behaviours, accepted as UNTESTED) |
| `verify-build` | **0 observed** | music-app sets `verifyEnabled: false` (`platform: ios`); **ripe sets it `true`** (`platform: swift`) — so this is a ~6-item sampling result, not a structural impossibility |

Two patterns in that sample matter more than the counts. **(i) Three of six items were resolved by a human waiver, at least two of them un-performed** — the human's actual answer, after multi-hour-to-multi-day delays, was "I accept this." **(ii) Two of PR #4's three items were one bug wearing two hats** — a `uiFilePatterns` misconfiguration made a non-visual diff look visually significant, firing both `[skip-audit] visual-verification` and `[visual-deliverable]`. Neither was fixable by re-running anything; both were fixable by a one-line config diagnosis the agent is perfectly capable of drafting. That is the archetype for the drafted-resolution design: not "the agent re-runs it" but "the agent says *your config claims this diff is visual; it isn't — change this line?* and you say yes."

Three corrections this forces on the plan, and they are not cosmetic:
0. **A fix on flow's `main` retires nothing in a consumer repo until a release ships and that repo updates.** The rigor case proves it: #81 was merged four hours before a draft it would have prevented, but music-app was running the installed 1.21.0. This PR's value in music-app and ripe is gated the same way — on v1.22.0 shipping and those repos picking it up. Worth saying plainly rather than implying the user sees the benefit on merge.
1. **The auto-resolve half is real but small.** `rigor` is already automated by #81. `visual-deliverable` **does** occur (PR #4) — so §7a's attempt-then-gate is not speculative — but that instance would not have been fixed by a re-run either; it was a config diagnosis. Meanwhile **none** of the six observed items was resolved by the agent simply re-running a stage. Any framing that sells this PR as "the agent now just does it" would be overselling: on this evidence, re-running resolves close to nothing, and *drafting the resolution for a human to approve* resolves nearly everything.
2. **The dominant real-world resolution is a waiver — three of six items, at least two "waived un-performed"** after multi-hour-to-multi-day delays. So the load-bearing deliverable is the **ask half**: an answerable question with waive as a first-class, honest, one-word option. That is what actually collapses a 13-day round trip.
3. **`verify-build` did not fire in this sample, but it can.** ripe runs with `verifyEnabled: true`, so a `verify-build` draft — the one kind waiver rule 2 deliberately refuses to let a human clear in one word — is a live possibility on its next ship, not a hypothetical. The rev-3/rev-6 invariant and ordering work therefore keeps its priority; what the evidence changes is only the claim that it is where *today's observed* pain lives.

**A finding worth its own follow-up:** this distribution was recoverable only because those PR authors wrote unusually good `## Flow run` prose. Ship **scrubs the manifest block when it marks a PR ready**, so flow systematically destroys the evidence needed to tune its own draft gate — there is no way to ask "which kinds cause drafts?" across a repo's history. Route to `roadmap.md` (a durable per-kind draft-cause record, e.g. a preserved collapsed section or a `flow:draft-causes` trailer), out of scope here.

**Diagnosis (read from the source, not recalled).** `/flow:ship` accumulates a **draft manifest** from **8 producer sites** covering **8 kinds** (site `:271` produces two kinds, `security` + `a11y`; kind `verify-build` is produced at two sites, `:273` + `:308`). Step 7's draft decision (`SKILL.md:888`) is then purely mechanical: manifest non-empty ⇒ `gh pr create --draft`. Three distinct problems compound into the symptom:

1. **One source drafts without ever attempting a resolution.** §7a's visual-deliverable gate (`:841–886`) is a pure assertion: if the walkthrough frames or the visual-history entry are missing, it drafts. Both are agent work. This is the user's "the pipeline just needs to be rerun" case, and flow's own precedent says it should self-resolve first — Step 1.0a does exactly that for the rigor marker (#81, `6fb5766`), Step 2a does it for skip-audit, Step 2 does it for a verify-build regression (bounded FB-0012 retry). §7a is the one gate that never tries.
2. **The other seven kinds are genuine decisions — but written unanswerably.** They are already-attempted or doctrinally not-the-agent's-call (`:272` "Do NOT auto-add the criterion yourself — that is the agent grading its own homework"; `:585` "Do **not** silently rewrite the un-fenced doc"). The problem is not that the agent should decide them; it is that they arrive as `needs: declare + verify the criterion, or human waive` with **no drafted resolution attached**, so the only move available to a non-engineer is "go fix it."
3. **There is no decision surface.** Step 8 (`:1072–1078`) outputs a PR URL and a one-line summary. Nothing presents the open decisions in-session, so the draft PR body *is* the hand-off — and it is written for a reviewer who reads diffs.

**Prerequisite the audit surfaced — the manifest line is not actually parseable today.** The canonical line shape is defined once (`:899`: a `kind` token from a 6-value enumeration, then `— needs:` from a 9-verb vocabulary). The producer sites do not follow it: **five sites prescribe no `[<kind>]` bracket token at all** (`:118`, `:271`, `:272`, `:273`, `:308`) — only `:118` literally prescribes a line opening `[decision-required] …`; the other four name the tag in prose and prescribe either a bare `needs:` fragment (`:272`) or a quoted finding string (`:308`). So normalization at those four is **adding** a slot that was never there, not evicting a wrong token — a different edit, and a different diff to review — and four different kinds are today indistinguishable to any parser — and **four sites use off-vocabulary `needs:` verbs** (`:118` "resolve"; `:272` "declare + verify the criterion"; `:273` "declare the criteria + re-verify"; `:344` bare "declare"; `:584` is free prose). The `:899` enumeration is also missing `rigor` and `coverage` entirely. This is a textbook FB-0010 fan-out contradiction, and it is load-bearing here: no deterministic triage is possible over an unparseable line. **Normalizing all 8 producer sites onto the `:899` shape is in-scope and is the first change**, not a follow-up.

`[decision-required]` survives unchanged as the reviewers' **resolution-confidence axis** (FB-0034) — it is orthogonal to `kind` — but normalization must give it a **named destination**, not just evict it from the bracket slot (rev-4 ISSUE 3: "absent from the bracket" and "dropped entirely" are indistinguishable to a grep, and dropping it would leave the security/a11y prose pointing at a signal the line no longer carries). So the `:899` shape gains a fourth field: `[<kind>] <finding> — needs: <verb> — confidence: <auto-fixable|decision-required> — candidate resolutions: <...>`, and `parse` surfaces it.

**Mode:** feature. **Status:** implementation-complete; all 15 Spec-walk items verified; full eval suite green (20 harnesses). Plan went through 5 `/flow:critique-plan` + 4 `/flow:audit-plan` rounds before build, every finding applied. Flow's own repo is `platform: library` + `uiSurface: false`, so `/flow:verify-build` and `/flow:accessibility-review` self-skip — the deterministic core is pinned by a new `run_manifest_triage_evals.py`; the orchestration is prose contract in the SKILL.

**Design — three changes, each at the site that owns the problem.**

**(A) §7a attempts before it gates.** On a visually-significant change with a missing deliverable, §7a now runs the resolution once — re-run `/flow:verify-build` to capture frames, and/or author the visual-history entry via §5c's hand-author path — and only creates the manifest entry if the gate still fails afterward. Bounded to **one attempt** (the existing §2a single-re-audit discipline), and the entry **clears only when §7a's own assertion re-passes** — never on the agent's say-so (FB-0062: a verdict is trusted only if its artifact exists and matches HEAD).

**Ordering is load-bearing** (plan-critic ISSUE 2). Step 7 pushes at `:807`, *before* §7a runs, and §7a compares the buffer's `head_sha_short` against `git rev-parse --short HEAD` (`:850,870`). So the sequence is fixed: **apply the file-changing resolution → commit → push → re-run the producing check against the new HEAD → re-apply Step 2's verify-build accounting to the fresh buffer → re-assert §7a → draft decision.**

The verdict re-check in that sequence is load-bearing, not ceremony (rev-6 ISSUE 1). §7a's assertion is **artifact-shaped, not verdict-shaped** — `:866-871` clears `BUF_OK` on `branch == $BRANCH && sha == $HEAD_SHA && FRAMES >= 1`, with no reference to `overall_verdict`. So a §7a re-run that captures a frame but returns FAIL/Unknown would overwrite the `verifyFindingsPath` buffer Step 2 already blessed, satisfy the re-assert, clear the `visual-deliverable` entry, and reach the draft decision with an empty manifest — a ready PR on a non-PASS build, produced by the very step added to reduce drafts. Step 2's accounting closed hundreds of lines earlier and never re-runs on its own, so §7a must re-apply it: a non-PASS verdict on the fresh buffer adds a `verify-build` entry. Re-running before committing stamps the buffer with a SHA the resolution commit immediately invalidates — spending the one attempt on a self-inflicted failure and leaving the resolution commit off the remote the PR opens against.

**(B) Every manifest entry carries a drafted resolution.** This is the half that answers "high-confidence recs — just do it," discharged safely: the agent does the *work* of proposing (drafts the missing Spec-walk criterion, drafts the corrected status line, names the specific candidate fixes), and the human supplies only the *approval* that doctrine reserves for them. Each entry gains, alongside its existing fields, a **plain-language triple**: what this means · what I need from you · what happens then. Where the agent cannot draft a resolution (a leaked secret, an unvetted dependency), the entry says what the human must do outside the session instead.

**(C) Step 8 becomes the decision surface — no new gate.** Per the critique gate (FB-0034 `feedback.md:613` — three outcomes, escalation routes into an existing gate; FB-0044 `feedback.md:375` — "Reserve **stop-before-PR** for genuine one-way-door decisions … otherwise escalation routes INTO a draft PR + NOT-READY manifest"; and FB-0064 `feedback.md:135`, which for the status-surface source specifically requires "route to the draft manifest, never a hard mid-loop halt (a false positive must not wedge a ship — draft-routing is the ceiling)"), the pass **never halts before the PR**: the pipeline always completes, the PR is always created, `ask`+`blocked` entries keep it a draft. What changes is the hand-off — Step 8 leads with a **numbered decision list** (plain language, the drafted resolution, a recommendation first with its reasoning per the standing user rule, "waive and ship as-is" on every `ask` entry **except `verify-build`** (waiver rule 3), and what was *already attempted* so the user sees the agent tried), plus a separate "needs you outside this session" list for `blocked` — which is never phrased as a question and never offers a waive. An answer given at the hand-off resolves through the **existing Step 7c reconcile fast-path** — apply, recompute the manifest (subtracting recorded waivers), re-render the body, and `gh pr ready` once nothing uncleared remains. No second ship run, no hand-edit. If nobody answers, nothing is wedged: the draft stands, now answerable in one word.

**Classification (deterministic, `skills/ship/lib/manifest-triage.py`) — keyed on `kind`, with one verb sub-split:**

| kind | producer | prior auto-attempt | class | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `visual-deliverable` | §7a `:886` | **none today** | **auto** *(new, per (A))* | capture + authoring are agent work; §7a is the only gate that never tries |
| `rigor` | §1.0a `:118` | yes — re-run once (#81) | `ask` | the entry exists *only because* the re-run was impossible or itself unresolved |
| `skip-audit` | §2a `:344` | yes — re-run + re-audit once | `ask` | auto-resolvable verdicts already re-ran at §2a; a manifest entry is the decision-required residue. Confirmed empirically: both observed `skip-audit` manifest items needed a config fix or a waiver, neither a re-run (§2a's batch re-runs happen *before* the manifest and never reach it) |
| `verify-build` (non-convergence) | §2 `:308` | yes — FB-0012 bounded ≤3, oscillation-checked | `ask` | a fourth attempt is a loop |
| `verify-build` (`no_plan_fallback`) | §2 `:273` | n/a | `ask` | agent drafts the criteria; declaring them itself is grading its own homework |
| `coverage` | §2 `:272` | n/a | `ask` | `:272` states the rule explicitly — never auto-add the criterion |
| `status-surface` | §5a.5 `:584` | n/a | `ask` | `:585` — never silently rewrite an un-fenced human doc; the entry *is* the proposal |
| `security` / `a11y` | §2 `:271` | n/a | `ask` **or** `blocked` | `secret rotation` / `dep vetting` ⇒ `blocked` (out-of-session action); every other verb ⇒ `ask` (FB-0011 autonomy bar: competing-merit and one-way-door fixes escalate) |
| *any* | — | — | most-conservative-for-that-kind | fail-safe: an unrecognized or absent `needs:` verb classifies `blocked` for `security`/`a11y` (their out-of-session modes are the dangerous ones to mis-class) and `ask` for every other kind — **never `auto`**, and never silently dropped |

So exactly **one** kind is auto-resolvable, and the honest headline is: *one source learns to try before it drafts; seven learn to ask a question the user can answer.* Claiming more would require the agent to overrule doctrine that exists for good reasons — and the evidence above says the ask half is where the value is anyway: the one newly-auto-resolvable kind never appeared in five real draft PRs, while a waiver resolved half the items that did.

**An `auto` entry that survives its one attempt demotes to `ask`, and the demotion is persisted** (plan-critic rev-3 ISSUE 1 + rev-4 ISSUE 1). After §7a's bounded attempt fails, the situation is identical to the six already-attempted kinds — an attempt was made and did not resolve it — so the entry is created, reclassified `ask`, and stays on the residual manifest. Because §7c recomputes "purely from gate state," an in-session demotion would evaporate on the next reconcile and the entry would re-classify `auto` — rendering as neither a question nor a blocked item, i.e. exactly the unanswerable draft this PR exists to remove. So **attempt-state is persisted alongside waivers**, and `classify` reads it: `visual-deliverable` returns `auto` only when no attempt is recorded, `ask` once one is. This is also what keeps "bounded to one attempt" true across a reconcile — the attempt counter survives the recompute.

**Where that state lives matters** (audit gate, rev-5 ISSUE 1). §7c is explicitly the *out-of-band* path (`:1061-1070`) and may run in a later session, from `/flow:land`, or on another host — so a `/tmp` record is a same-session **cache**, not the source of truth, and the rigor-marker precedent does not transfer (a missing rigor marker fails *closed* — "re-run"; a missing ship-state record would fail *open* — back to `auto`). The durable record is the **PR body**, which already travels with the branch and which §7c already re-fetches: the `## Waived at ship` section carries waivers, and each manifest entry renders its own `already-attempted` marker. §7c must therefore **fetch and parse the live body BEFORE it recomputes** — its documented steps 1–4 recompute from gate state, *write* a re-rendered body at step 2, and only fetch at step 4's post-write read-back, so by the time the body is read it has already been overwritten by a recompute that had no waiver or attempt state. So §7c gains a **step 0: fetch the live PR body and parse `## Waived at ship` + the per-entry `already-attempted` markers**, feeding step 1. `/tmp` is read when present as a fast path; step 0 is what makes the durable surface actually load-bearing rather than nominal (rev-7 ISSUE 3). And the fail-safe is explicit: **no state recoverable from either surface ⇒ classify `ask`, never `auto`** — a question the human can answer is the safe direction; a silent re-attempt or an unanswerable residual item is not.

**"Residual" has one definition:** every entry whose `clears_when` re-check has not passed, **minus recorded waivers — except `verify-build`, which is never subtracted** (waiver rule 2). Not "`ask ∪ blocked`" — that is a *consequence* (after demotion the two coincide), not the rule. Keying the draft decision on class rather than on the re-check is exactly how a `visual-deliverable` entry could vanish into a ready PR, which `SKILL.md:843` exists to prevent; stating the waiver subtraction as part of the definition rather than as a separate step is what keeps the two rules jointly falsifiable by one eval fixture (rev-6 ISSUE 3).

**Waiver semantics (plan-critic rev-3 ISSUE 2 + ISSUE 3) — three rules, each with a reason:**
1. **A waiver is persisted, not just printed.** §7c recomputes the manifest purely from gate state (`:1065`, "the same accounting Step 2 + Step 7a do, nothing more"), and a waiver changes no gate state — so without a record the recompute re-adds every waived entry and the manifest can never empty. `manifest-triage.py waive` writes to the **per-branch ship-state record** — the `/tmp/flow-ship-state-<branch-slug>.json` cache (shaped after `lib/rigor-marker.py`) *and* the PR body's `## Waived at ship` section, which is the durable half (see the durability note above) — keyed by a fingerprint of `(kind, finding)` so a waiver applies to the finding it was given for and lapses if that finding changes. §7c step 1 subtracts recorded waivers from the recompute, and the record is what produces the `## Waived at ship` PR-body section.
2. **Waiving a `verify-build` entry records it but keeps the PR a draft.** `:308`/`:310` state without qualification that no merge-ready PR is ever produced on a non-PASS build. That rule gets no carve-out here, and it is defended twice because the rev-3 lesson (never key a merge-readiness decision on a proxy) recurs on exactly this vector: a waived `verify-build` entry is **exempt from the §7c waiver subtraction** — it stays in the residual set, rendered as waived — **and** §7c step 3's ready-flip is re-keyed from "manifest is empty" to `verdict == READY` from `manifest-triage.py`, so no future emptiness path can flip a non-PASS build to ready either. The human's own merge click remains the only way past it. Every other kind may go ready on a recorded waiver — that is the human exercising the merge gate they already own, not the agent self-certifying.
3. **`blocked` entries are never waivable, and neither are `verify-build` entries.** "Waive and ship as-is" is offered on `ask` entries *other than* `verify-build`. A leaked secret or an unvetted dependency must not become one-word-dismissible — which is also why the fail-safe row above sends an off-vocabulary `security`/`a11y` verb to `blocked` rather than to `ask`. `verify-build` is excluded for a different reason (rev-6 ISSUE 2): offering "waive and ship as-is" on an entry that by rule 2 keeps the PR a draft would be a **lie to a non-engineer** — the label promises shipping and delivers the same draft, and GitHub will not merge a draft until someone marks it ready. So a `verify-build` entry renders an honest action instead: what a passing build actually requires, plus the plain statement that the human can mark the PR ready on GitHub themselves if they accept the risk — **the agent will not do it for them.**

**Invariants this must not weaken:**
- **An auto-resolve clears an entry only when the check that produced it re-runs and returns clean** (FB-0062). Triage output carries a per-entry `clears_when`; `classify` has no way to express "cleared" — clearing is the SKILL's job, after the named re-check passes.
- **No merge-ready PR on a non-PASS build** (§2's existing invariant, `:308`/`:310`, unqualified). A `verify-build` entry leaves the manifest only via a genuine PASS. A waiver on that kind is recorded but **does not flip the PR to ready** — see waiver rule 2 above.
- **A waiver is never a silent delete.** Every waived entry is written to the persisted waiver record *and* a new `## Waived at ship` PR-body section, so the merge gate still sees what was set aside.
- **Body↔draft coherence (FB-0067) is untouched.** `render-manifest` preserves the `🚫 NOT READY TO MERGE` sentinel and the `<!-- flow:not-ready-manifest -->` fences byte-for-byte, so `lib/pr-coherence.py`, `/flow:doctor` Check 2.10, and `/flow:land`'s pre-merge check keep matching.

Emits JSON: per-entry `{kind, needs, confidence, class, action, clears_when, drafted_resolution?, already_attempted?, waived?}` + `verdict` ∈ `READY | DECIDE | BLOCKED`.

That is the sibling half of this doctrine and it already owns the `skills/contribute/SKILL.md` change as a separately-queued item, so duplicating it here would collide. This PR is the `/flow:ship` half; the two are consistent and cross-referenced.

They become better-phrased asks with drafted resolutions, never auto-fixes.
- **Changing the reviewers' `[auto-fixable]` / `[decision-required]` axis** — the tagging contract is correct; only its position in the manifest line and ship's routing of tagged findings change.

**For the Step 4c lesson harvest (a real reviewer-loop lesson, and it is about the author, not the reviewer):** three revisions of this plan carried a finding that `/flow:critique-plan` had *fabricated* an FB-0064 citation. It had not. The quoted text — "route to the draft manifest, never a hard mid-loop halt (a false positive must not wedge a ship — draft-routing is the ceiling)" — is on `dev-docs/feedback.md:135`, verbatim, inside FB-0064's body. The error was mine: a verification grep run as `grep -n ceiling dev-docs/feedback.md | cut -c1-300` **matched** line 135 and the `cut` truncated the match out of view, and the absence of the phrase in the visible prefix was read as proof of absence — from a command whose own match was the evidence against that reading. `/flow:audit-plan` caught it two revisions later. Two transferable lessons: (a) **a truncating filter on a verification grep can invert its meaning** — if a grep matched, the match is the finding, and `cut`/`head` must never be the last word on "the text isn't there"; (b) **an accepted audit finding is not a verified one** — the finding was applied to the plan and re-asserted twice without anyone re-opening the cited line. Both belong in the reviewer/verification-discipline lane rather than the plan-critic prompt lane. Eval-fixture skeleton: this session's rev5-audit → rev9-audit window.

**Confidence verdicts (load-bearing assumptions):**
- HIGH — the draft decision is unconditional: `SKILL.md:888` read directly.
- HIGH — the 8 producer sites, their bracket tokens, and their `needs:` verbs are as stated: each of `:118`, `:271`, `:272`, `:273`, `:308`, `:344`, `:584`, `:886` and the `:899` template read directly at their line content (not recalled, and not inferred from a headings grep — the rev-1 error the auditor caught).
- HIGH — six of the eight kinds already make an auto-attempt or are doctrinally barred from one, so `visual-deliverable` is genuinely the only `auto` row: `:112–123` (rigor re-runs first), `:343` (skip-audit re-runs + re-audits once), `:305–308` (verify-build's bounded ≤3), `:272` + `:585` (the two explicit never-auto rules) all read directly.
- HIGH — the reconcile route already exists and needs no new mechanism: Step 7c (`:1061–1070`) recomputes the manifest, re-renders the body, and flips draft state, explicitly built for a blocker cleared outside a full ship run.
- MEDIUM — the `needs:` vocabulary is enforced by prose, not schema, so a future producer can drift off-vocabulary again. Normalizing the 8 sites fixes today's four violations; the fail-safe (unrecognized ⇒ `ask`) is what makes tomorrow's drift safe rather than silent. A JSON-schema'd manifest line would be the durable fix and is deliberately not attempted here.
- MEDIUM — ship-state durability across a cross-session §7c / `/flow:land` reconcile. `/tmp` demonstrably does not survive a reboot, a fresh clone, or another host, and `:1061-1070` shows §7c is exactly the out-of-band path where that happens; the design therefore treats `/tmp` as a cache, the PR body as the durable record, and an unrecoverable state as `ask`. Reconstructing from a rendered body is best-effort parsing — pinned by an eval, not guaranteed — which is why the fail-safe direction (never `auto`) is what carries the safety, not the reconstruction. **Reconstruction fails in two directions, and both need a rule**: recovering too little is covered above (no state ⇒ `ask`); recovering too *much* is the dangerous one — a waiver greedily or ambiguously matched to an entry it was not given for would subtract a real blocker and could flip the PR ready. So: **a waiver is honored only on an exact `(kind, finding)` fingerprint match against an entry present in the same recompute; any ambiguity or partial match ⇒ not subtracted.**
- MEDIUM — the ordering fix assumes the producing check is re-runnable at Step 7 (post-commit, post-push). True for `verify-build` by construction, but a re-run has real cost on a large project; the one-attempt bound keeps the worst case at one extra stage run.

## PR — `/flow:post-merge` skill, v1 (v1.21.0, shipped #80, FB-0072)

## PR — Three gates that could report success without doing their job (v1.22.0, FB-0074) — **MERGED (#86, `129f582`)**

A `/flow:contribute` drain. Three confirmed lessons applied, two dismissed with a reproduced symptom, one escalated to the roadmap, seven held. Unifying rule: *a contract whose two halves live in different files, with nothing mechanically checking the join, degrades silently — and the degradation is indistinguishable from success.*

**Spec-walk:**
- [x] `/flow:audit-coverage`, `/flow:audit-skips` and `/flow:critique-plan` resolve an explicit repo root **before** any relative read, precedence **cwd-git-toplevel first, `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` second**; an unresolvable root emits a distinct `ROOT-UNRESOLVED` / `root_error` that is NOT confusable with the clean-skip line. (verify: `run_root_anchor_evals.py` — extracts each guard from the live SKILL.md and executes it in 4 scenarios × every guard; asserts an exact guard count per skill, that the unresolved output contains neither `SKIPPED` nor `"note"`, and that a conflicting `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` does NOT win over the cwd's git root — the worktree regression.)
- [x] `/flow:ship` Step 2a routes `root_error` to a `[decision-required]` draft-manifest entry, never a clean pass. (verify: `run_root_anchor_evals.py` prose assertions over `audit-skips/SKILL.md` + `ship/SKILL.md`.)
- [x] `render-test-plan.py` stamps **every** emitted path (machine-judged, self-report, empty-criteria, no-buffer fallback) with `<!-- flow:test-plan-rendered -->` plus a content digest over the Test-plan section's checkbox **states and count**. (verify: `run_render_evals.py` asserts the marker on one case per path; `run_pr_coherence_evals.py` runs the REAL renderer and feeds its REAL output to the checker.)
- [x] `pr-coherence.py test-plan-provenance` FAILS a hand-authored Test plan (no stamp) AND a genuinely-rendered one whose boxes were flipped afterwards (digest mismatch), while PASSING: a filled-in `<how to verify>` line, prose edits around the block, unrelated checkboxes elsewhere in the PR body, and a fenced documentation example. (verify: `run_pr_coherence_evals.py` — 14 provenance cases covering each direction, including the two false-positive classes that would hard-block a legitimate ship at Step 7b's `exit 1`.)
- [x] `/flow:ship` Step 7b asserts provenance on the **re-fetched** body and distinguishes exit 1 (forgery) from exit 2 (tool unavailable), with a documented human-waive route; the renderer call has the installed-else-checkout fallback. (verify: `ship/SKILL.md` §7b branches on `TP_RC`; `verify-pr-body.sh::flow_assert_test_plan_provenance` returns 2 when the checker is unreachable.)
- [x] `doctor/lib/skill-composition-lint.py` FAILS on a fenced `Skill("x")` whose target sets `disable-model-invocation: true`, and does NOT fire on prose mentions, self-calls, cross-plugin targets, or a callee with no flag; it warns (never silently passes) on an unclosed fence or unparseable frontmatter. (verify: `run_skill_composition_evals.py` — 16 cases incl. 4 fence-delimiter variants (` ``` `, `~~~`, info-string, nested) and a live assertion that flow's own `skills/` stays clean; confirmed to FAIL against `origin/main`'s pre-fix `post-merge/SKILL.md`.)
- [x] `/flow:post-merge` §3 no longer emits `Skill("flow:land")`; it hands the step to the human and §6 holds the archive verdict at 🚫 until a `docs: land #N` PR exists and is merged. (verify: `run_merge_status_evals.py::skill-does-not-CALL-land` — fence-scoped, so the SKILL's own prose explaining the anti-pattern doesn't satisfy it; the old check asserted the bug and kept passing on that prose.)
- [x] Every claim about `/flow:contribute` opening a *draft* and about `/flow:post-merge` *calling* `/flow:land` is swept repo-wide (FB-0010 fan-out): 8 + 7 sites incl. `docs/workflow.md`, both manifests, `workflow-help`, and the v1.21.0 CHANGELOG entry. (verify: `git grep -niE 'calls /flow:land|called by .flow:post-merge'` and the contribute-draft grep both return only deliberately-annotated historical records.)
- [x] `.github/workflows/ci.yml` asserts its hand-written harness list matches `evals/run_*.py` in **both** directions. (verify: the step passes on the current tree; removing a runner line or renaming a harness each make it exit 1 — both directions exercised.)
- [x] All 21 CI-wired eval harnesses green (20 at #86; this PR adds `run_scratch_isolation_evals.py`); every new fixture verified to FAIL on the pre-fix tree. (verify: run each harness listed in `ci.yml`; `git archive origin/main` for the pre-fix checks.)

## PR — `/flow:post-merge` skill, v1 (this branch, v1.21.0, FB-0072) — IMPLEMENTED — pending /simplify + staff-review + ship

**Restated request:** Build part B of the `/flow:post-merge` capture (roadmap § Next, from #78/#79): the human-invoked skill run at the "merged — anything left, safe to archive?" moment. **Five orchestrated steps** — the roadmap's four original post-merge functions (merge-detect (queue-safe) → merge-gate feedback synthesis → stale-branch cleanup → archive-safety verdict) **plus** the doc-currency step, which delegates to `/flow:land` by **calling** it rather than reimplementing reconciliation (the compose-not-combine decision from #79). Ordered §2–§6: merge-detect → `/flow:land` → feedback synthesis → cleanup → archive verdict. v1 scope per the roadmap: feedback synthesis writes **user-scope stores only** (agent memory + the `/flow:contribute` queue) with content-match dedup — no `feedbackPath` repo write, no watermark (both deferred to v1b).

**Mode:** feature. **Status:** planning; Spec-walk below. flow's own repo is `platform: library` + `uiSurface: false`, so verify-build/a11y self-skip — the deterministic core (the three-state merge gate + the archive-safety verdict) is pinned by a new `run_merge_status_evals.py`; the orchestration is prose contract in the SKILL.

**Files:**
- `skills/post-merge/SKILL.md` (NEW) — `disable-model-invocation: true` (human runs it after merging, like `/flow:land`); `allowed-tools: Read, Edit, Write, Glob, Grep, Bash, Skill`. Orchestrates §1 pre-flight (gh/jq/git) → §2 merge-detect (queue-safe, via the helper) → §3 `Skill("flow:land <PR#>")` (composition — runs only after merge is confirmed, so land's own two-state §1a gate is already satisfied; land needs no change) → §4 feedback synthesis (user-scope only) → §5 stale-branch cleanup → §6 archive-safety verdict.
- `skills/post-merge/lib/merge-status.py` (NEW, stdlib) — `classify` (three-state: MERGED / CLOSED-unmerged / OPEN-pending from a `gh pr view --json` blob), the bounded poll driver honoring `postMergeWaitSeconds`, and `archive-check` (git-state cleanliness → `safe` / `not-safe: <reasons>`). Deterministic + testable.
- `schema/flow.config.schema.json` — NEW slot `postMergeWaitSeconds` (integer, default 150, min 0); slot count **29 → 30**.
- Slot-count fan-out (29 → 30): `marketplace.json`, `plugin.json`, `docs/workflow.md`, `doctor/SKILL.md` (Check 2.5 + the "all 29 slots" line), `template/base/CLAUDE.md.template`, `CHANGELOG.md`.
- Skill-catalog fan-out (register the new skill): `plugin.json` + `marketplace.json` descriptions, `README.md`, `docs/workflow.md` (Step 11 positioning + skill table), `workflow-help/SKILL.md`.
- `evals/run_merge_status_evals.py` (NEW) + wired into `.github/workflows/ci.yml`.
- Version v1.20.0 → v1.21.0; CHANGELOG; history (SAFETY — branch deletion + feedback persistence); feedback FB-0072 (+ reserve it in `reserved-feedback-numbers.md` before drafting); roadmap § Next `/flow:post-merge` entry → moved to shipped; plan Current Focus reconciled.

**Spec-walk:**
- [x] `skills/post-merge/lib/merge-status.py classify` returns three states from a `gh pr view --json` blob: `MERGED` → proceed; `state:CLOSED` + not merged → **terminal** (fail loud); `state:OPEN` → **pending** (poll signal, NOT a failure). (verify: `run_merge_status_evals.py` — one case per state with injected JSON, incl. `autoMergeRequest` present/absent.)
- [x] The poll driver honors `postMergeWaitSeconds` (default 150, `0` = fail-fast): still-OPEN at the cap → a distinct graceful "still queued, re-run" exit code, never the terminal-fail code. (verify: `run_merge_status_evals.py` — cap-reached path returns the graceful code, not the terminal one; `0` short-circuits.)
- [x] `merge-status.py archive-check` (run in a temp git repo) → `safe` only when no uncommitted + no unpushed + no stray-untracked AND the branch is merged; otherwise `not-safe:` naming each reason. (verify: `run_merge_status_evals.py` — clean / dirty / unpushed / untracked fixtures.)
- [x] `skills/post-merge/SKILL.md` orchestrates the five functions and **calls `Skill("flow:land")`** for doc-currency ⚠️ *(FB-0074/v1.22.0: this criterion was verified by a contract-grep that the string was present — but the call itself was rejected at runtime, so the grep pinned the bug rather than the behavior. The eval is now inverted: post-merge must NOT emit the call, and must delegate to the human instead.)* (does NOT reimplement reconciliation); §5 uses `git branch -d` (never `-D`) + a graceful remote-delete (skip if already gone); §4 writes user-scope stores only (reused `harvest_lesson.py` prescan/enqueue/mark + memory tools, content-match dedup) with NO `feedbackPath` repo write. (verify: SKILL frontmatter `disable-model-invocation: true`; `git grep -n "branch -D" skills/post-merge` empty; contract-grep checks in the eval that the SKILL references `Skill("flow:land")` + `merge-status.py` + does not write `feedbackPath`.)

(verify: full eval suite green; `git grep` version + slot-count parity; ci.yml diff.)

**Explicitly OUT of v1 (deferred to v1b, per the roadmap):** the transcript-timestamp watermark + the `feedbackPath` FB-inbox for repo-doc `FB-XXXX` capture; robust multi-session / Conductor-worktree transcript-window resolution (v1 synthesizes best-effort from the current session since the last ship — honest about the limitation); converging `/flow:land`'s standalone §1a merge gate onto the shared three-state helper (v1 leaves land's gate as-is since post-merge polls before calling it). Named so the plan-critic doesn't read them as gaps.



**Restated request:** drain the lesson-contribution queue. The top-scoring lesson (0.8, correction-sourced, reported from two independent projects) says `extract-visual-states.py` grabs the first `Visual-walk` block in a shared multi-PR plan, so an active PR that declares none silently inherits a retained PR's capture state-set and a forced `visual_significant`. A near-identical earlier report was wrongly dismissed as `already-encoded` in the previous drain (#74) — the recurrence is what caught it.

**Mode:** feature. **Status:** implementation-complete; final-pass review run. Flow's own repo is `platform: library` + `uiSurface: false`, so `/flow:verify-build` and `/flow:accessibility-review` self-skip — the eval harness + the before/after reproductions are the behavioral gate here.

**Spec-walk:**
- [x] A retained-section `Visual-walk` is no longer adopted when the active PR declares none: `assertions: []`, `co_located: false`, loud warning naming the owning section + the remedy. (verify: `test_anchor_co_location` + `test_anchor_co_location_cli` in `run_walk_extract_evals.py`; reproduced by hand on a 2-PR plan before/after.)
- [x] The `visual-significance` override no longer fires on a non-co-located block, and propagates the parser's warnings so the operator gets the line numbers. (verify: `run_visual_significance_evals.py` 13/13; hand-run against the repro plan shows `override: null` + both WARN signals.)
- [x] Inert on the shapes that aren't the bug — <2 anchor headings, no anchor at all, `Visual-walk` authored above its sibling `Spec-walk`, and any unanchored `extract_block` call keep byte-identical behavior. (verify: `test_anchor_co_location_regression_guards` + `test_cli_backward_compat_keys`.)
- [x] The two shapes the anchor proxy does NOT cover (active PR with no `Spec-walk`; retained section authored visual-first) are documented where authors read and pinned so neither reads as a fresh bug. (verify: `test_anchor_known_limitation_tiny_mode` + `test_anchor_known_limitation_retained_visual_first`; prose in `walk_extract.py` docstring, `rules/plan-discipline.md`, `verify-build/SKILL.md`, roadmap.)
- [x] A recurrence of a previously-dismissed lesson is surfaced mechanically, not by prose alone: `dedup` exits 4 (distinct from 3 = already-queued) with the prior reason/date. (verify: `dedup-1-recurrence-of-dismissed`, `dedup-1b-recurrence-not-conflated-with-queued`, `dedup-3-already-queued-stays-3` in `run_contribution_evals.py`.)
- [x] No regression across the suite. (verify: all 17 eval harnesses green; `run_walk_extract_evals.py` 47 → 67 checks.)

**Staff-review follow-ups routed (not fixed here).** Four findings went to `roadmap.md` § Exploration rather than expanding this PR: the artifact-less-reviewer breadcrumb (now unblocked by `.flow/` + `flow_scratch.py`, and the highest-value item there); detached-HEAD never clearing the stamp gate; the `flow-detached` global fallback; and the missing "declared: none" sentinel for `referenceGlob`. Two naming/consistency NITs also deferred: `flow_scratch.py` is misnamed after `/simplify` reduced it to stamping only (rename touches the harness + two SKILLs, so it rides the next audit-skips change), and `visual-significance.json` lacks the `<owner>-` prefix the other seven `.flow/` artifacts carry.

**Confidence verdicts (load-bearing assumptions):**
- HIGH — the reported bug reproduces and the fix closes it: verified by running the pre-fix module (from `git show HEAD:`) and the post-fix module against the same plan fixtures.
- HIGH — the fix is a strict improvement, not a regression: both known-limitation shapes leak *identically* on the pre-fix module, confirmed by direct measurement.
- MEDIUM — "before the second anchor heading" is the right region proxy. It is correct for `feature`-mode plans and wrong for two shapes; a genuinely universal per-PR boundary marker is a plan-format decision, routed to `roadmap.md` rather than guessed at here.

**Files touched:** `skills/verify-build/lib/{walk_extract,extract-visual-states,visual-significance}.py`, `scripts/contribution_store.py`, `evals/run_{walk_extract,contribution}_evals.py`, `rules/plan-discipline.md`, `skills/{verify-build,contribute}/SKILL.md`, `dev-docs/{history,roadmap,plan}.md`.

## PR — Generalize the walkthrough annotation layer to any DOM element (v1.20.0, shipped #75, FB-0071)

**Restated request:** health-tracker hand-built a DOM-general click-to-comment layer (its FB-0011) that was always meant to be upstreamed into flow but never was. Generalize flow's image-only FB-0051 annotation overlay so a reviewer can pin a note to ANY element on a rendered report: a DevTools-style hover-inspect picker (adapt agentation's `elementFromPoint`+`pickTarget` shape, vanilla/dependency-free), keyboard parent/child traversal (new), a stable content-derived anchor that survives per-iteration regeneration, a location-descriptor export (not raw coordinates), shipped as a reusable partial any flow HTML-emitting skill/subagent can inject. Keep the working `file://` properties (no native modals, `execCommand` clipboard, localStorage, numbered pins, inline editor, two-step Clear, Copy-notes). Stdlib/vanilla only, project-agnostic. Land alongside or after the V3 renderer; don't block on it.

**Mode:** feature. **Status:** SHIPPED — merged as #75 (`5006e161`). The layer is client-side JS, so `run_report_render_evals.py` pins the deterministic contract (picker/anchor/export tokens present; native-modal + async-clipboard tokens absent; always-inject) and the interaction was driven end-to-end in the in-app browser (the report is not a flow *product* UI surface — flow's own repo is `platform: library` + `uiSurface: false`, so verify-build/a11y self-skip on the ship that opens this PR; the browser pass is the real behavioral gate here).

**Spec-walk:**
- [x] Picker: `mousemove`→`elementFromPoint`→`pickTarget()` walk-up (skips non-visual tags + `#annot-*` chrome + sub-8px boxes) → highlight overlay on `getBoundingClientRect()`; explicit Pin-mode toggle; crosshair cursor. (verify: browser pass — hover highlights the element under the cursor; `run_report_render_evals.py` layer-contract asserts `elementFromPoint`/`pickTarget`/`getBoundingClientRect` present.)
- [x] Keyboard traversal: ArrowUp→`parentElement`, ArrowDown→reverse most-recent-Up via a descent stack; outline re-renders each step; Click/Enter commit + open editor; Escape cancels, no pin. (verify: browser pass — `upGrew`/`downRestored`/`escExitedPicking` observed; layer-contract asserts Arrow handlers + `parentElement`.)
- [x] Anchor = stable element identity (`data-pin-id` else nearest-heading + tag + role + text-sample), NOT DOM index; cosmetic click-offset stored for placement only. (verify: browser pass — persisted note has `anchor` keys + `off`, no `xPct`; resolver is content/score-based.)
- [x] Regeneration stability: on-load resolver best-matches each anchor to a current element position-independently; unmatched notes retained as dashed "unanchored" pins + still exported, flagged. (verify: browser pass — reload re-anchored the surviving note; a no-match legacy note → `2 notes · 1 unanchored`, still in the export.)
- [x] Pins render as overlay markers positioned from the element rect (+ cosmetic offset), repositioned on scroll/resize/load — tables, paragraphs, images all pinnable (no per-image host wrapper). (verify: browser pass — pins on `<p>` in two sections.)
- [x] Export: `## <section>` groups + `at <element> "text"` descriptor replaces `x%,y%`; reading order; numbers match on-page pins. (verify: browser pass — `exportHasSections`/`exportHasDescriptors`/`exportNoCoords` all true.)
- [x] Kept + hardened: NO `confirm(`/`alert(`/`prompt(`/`navigator.clipboard` (two-step inline Clear + flash toasts + `execCommand('copy')` + visible-textarea fallback); localStorage, numbered pins, inline editor, Copy-notes retained; legacy FB-0051 image-pins migrate on load. (verify: layer-contract eval asserts those four tokens ABSENT + `execCommand`/`Confirm clear`/`localStorage` present; browser pass exercised two-step Clear + migration.)
- [x] Reusable + generalized injection: self-contained DOM-general partial; `render-report.py` injects on every rendered report (dropped `annot-shot` gate); reuse contract documented in the layer header + verify-build SKILL.md §10. (verify: `run_report_render_evals.py` always-injected renders a frameless buffer and asserts the layer is present; security eval `test_v2_capture_render.py` frameless assertion updated to the new always-inject contract.)
- [x] Evals + version + docs: `run_report_render_evals.py` extended (already CI-wired); FB-0071 reserved then written; version bump 1.19.0 → 1.20.0 (plugin.json + marketplace.json ×2 + both description lines); CHANGELOG v1.20.0; history (SAFETY); plan (this block + Current Focus). (verify: full eval + security suites green; `git grep` version parity; JSON parses.)
- [x] Final-pass review: `/simplify` (4 lenses — removed dead `lastClient` + vestigial `strip()`, merged `onMove` branches, dropped a `commitPick` coord round-trip, added `DEFAULT_OFF`) + `/flow:staff-review` (4 lenses, **no BLOCKER**; applied 9 cheap NITs: toolbar/copybox focus ring, JS-measured bar padding vs the wrapping-bar clip, two palette snaps to the host tokens, dark-mode bar border, commented `!important`, `⌘/Ctrl+↵` cross-platform hint, unanchored pins to informational amber off the destructive-red token, focus-return to the edited pin, `save()` warns on a localStorage failure instead of swallowing it). All re-verified in-browser (focus-restore, amber, save-warn, bar-padding, zero JS errors). Rigor marker written.

**Deferred (staff-review + /simplify follow-ups):**
- **Weak-match re-anchor confidence tell + empty-text tie-break** (push-further exploration + staff-engineer) → `roadmap.md` § Exploration (a weak `resolve()` match can render as a confident blue pin; equal-scoring empty-text siblings resolve to the first in document order). Includes the roadmap-concrete idea: a live descriptor readout on the hover outline (`elDesc` on `#annot-highlight`) so the reviewer sees the anchor before commit.
- **Palette fan-out guard** (design-engineer): the `--annot-*` fallbacks are hand-mirrored from `render-report.py`'s CSS (now cross-referenced in both files' comments); a CI grep asserting the shared hexes stay equal is the durable defense — next verify-build hygiene pass.
- **`resolve()` is O(notes × candidates × heading-walk)** (staff-engineer + /simplify efficiency): fine for today's small reports; measure before optimizing if the partial is reused on a large HTML surface.
- **Persistent in-mode picker guidance** (UX): the arrow-refine affordance lives only in a 2.4s flash + the top hint; a persistent in-mode banner is a deliberate design call, not a reflex tweak — next UX iteration on the picker.

## PR — Frame-integrity gate in /flow:verify-build (v1.16.0, this branch, FB-0066)

**Restated request:** Screenshots were captured but obvious visual defects (a broken safe-area background, seam, clipped text) were missed and the change was declared "verified." Close it in three parts: (1) a standing, fixed, **must-pass** frame-integrity checklist applied to EVERY captured frame independent of the plan's `Visual-walk` assertions — any failing item ⇒ FAIL, not Unknown; (2) make the judge describe each edge/corner *before* any verdict (anti-glance); (3) an operator-discipline rule forbidding self-certified visual sign-off from implementer-eyeballed ad-hoc screenshots. Project-agnostic; pinned by an eval (known-bad ⇒ FAIL, clean ⇒ PASS).

**Mode:** feature. **Status:** implementation-complete + `/simplify` + `/flow:staff-review` (four lenses, no BLOCKER, NITs applied in-tree) done on `claude/optimistic-easley-c37004`; security-review + audit-coverage clean; ready to ship. flow's own repo is `platform: library` + `uiSurface: false`, so verify-build/a11y self-skip on the ship that opens this PR; the change is pinned by `run_frame_integrity_evals.py` + `run_render_evals.py` (deterministic render + spec-conformance) rather than a live VLM run.

**Spec-walk:**
- [x] New `skills/verify-build/lib/frame-integrity-checklist.md` — the fixed must-pass judge prompt: 6 closed-list items, describe-each-edge/corner-before-verdict discipline, FAIL-never-Unknown rule, per-frame output schema. (verify: `run_frame_integrity_evals.py` checklist-* checks.)
- [x] `rubric.md` VLM section documents the frame-integrity pre-pass (runs first, not pairwise, describe-before-verdict, FAIL-not-Unknown); intro lists it as the fourth pass. (verify: wiring-rubric check.)
- [x] `SKILL.md` §6 spawns the frame-integrity judge against every persisted screenshot; §7 aggregates a frame-integrity FAIL to `overall_verdict: FAIL`/exit 1; §8 documents the `frame_integrity[]` buffer field; §5a notes every frame is audited. (verify: wiring-skill-* checks.)
- [x] `findings-schema.json` adds the top-level `frame_integrity[]` array (required item fields: frame/edges/corners/background_continuity/verdict; verdict enum PASS|FAIL); additive — `schema_version` stays 1.0. `findings-example.json` carries a PASS entry. (verify: schema-* + example-* checks; JSON parses.)
- [x] `render-report.py` renders a prominent "Frame integrity" section (per-frame verdict cards, described edges, FAIL-only "Failing checks:" block); empty field ⇒ nothing rendered (non-visual reports unchanged). (verify: render-bad-* / render-clean-* checks.)
- [x] `docs/workflow.md` Step 8/9 operator-discipline rule: implementer-eyeballed ad-hoc screenshots are for iterating, never sign-off; visual verdicts route through §5a. (verify: prose present.)
- [x] `evals/run_frame_integrity_evals.py` + `fixtures/frame-integrity/{badframe,cleanframe}.json`; wired into `.github/workflows/ci.yml`. Version bumped 1.15.0 → 1.16.0 (plugin.json + marketplace.json ×2 + descriptions — renumbered after rebase found `origin/main` had already claimed v1.15.0/FB-0065 for an unrelated PR, #68); CHANGELOG v1.16.0; history (SAFETY) + feedback (FB-0066) + plan (this block + Current Focus). (verify: full eval suite green; `git grep` version parity.)
- [x] `skills/ship/lib/render-test-plan.py` — a `frame_integrity[]` FAIL overrides the "confirm and merge" headline even when every criterion PASSes (the two gates are independent per SKILL.md Step 7), plus a plain-bullet "Frame integrity FAILED" section naming the failing frame(s) with evidence; absent `frame_integrity` is a no-op (byte-identical to pre-fix render); the empty-criteria/no-plan-fallback render path also carries the same override (staff-engineer finding — §5a's capture gate is decoupled from Spec-walk, so a no-criteria run can still have a frame-integrity FAIL). Caught by /simplify's altitude pass: the file was correctly left untouched by the initial cut, but "untouched" silently reintroduced the exact Potemkin-success class this renderer exists to prevent — the one committed, non-forgeable PR surface could show a false green next to a FAIL verdict elsewhere in the buffer. (verify: `run_render_evals.py` crit-6 checks — 4 cases.)
- [x] `/flow:staff-review` (four lenses, no BLOCKER) — `render_vcard()` escaping-contract fix (converged 3-of-4 lenses), dark-mode CSS for `.fi-edges`, a TOC entry for a failing Frame integrity section, PR-body evidence detail in the FAIL bullets. Follow-ups routed to `roadmap.md` § Next "Frame-integrity follow-ups" (cross-frame consistency check, adversarial self-check for the judge, design-language doc entry, empty-vs-never-ran copy ambiguity) — none block this PR. (verify: `run_render_evals.py` full suite green; rigor-gate marker written.)

## PR — Discover undeclared status surfaces in /flow:ship (v1.14.0, FB-0064)

**Restated request:** Close the orientation-doc-staleness gap. The doc-currency machinery (Step 5a/5b + doctor 2.7 + `statusDocs`) works but only for DECLARED surfaces; an undeclared orientation doc (CLAUDE.md) silently rots after a merge. Confirm the mechanism exists (don't rebuild it), then ADD a discovery + best-effort drift tier that routes to the draft manifest — the `/flow:audit-coverage` shape (best-effort, human-backstopped, never a hard halt, never a silent auto-edit of an un-fenced doc). Keep two clean tiers (declared+fenced = auto; undeclared+drifted = draft). Project-agnostic (config slot + diff, no consumer tokens).

**Mode:** feature. **Status:** implementation-complete on `claude/kind-blackburn-7ac67f`; pending final-pass review + ship. flow's own repo is `platform: library` + `uiSurface: false`, so verify-build/a11y self-skip. The ship pipeline that opens this PR runs from the **installed** plugin (v1.13.0), which predates Step 5a.5 — so 5a.5 does **not** execute on this ship; it's pinned by `run_status_surface_evals.py` and a manual live scan against flow's own CLAUDE.md/README (both surface as undeclared candidates; neither carries a stale "next" claim about just-shipped work → no flag, the correct false-positive outcome). It runs live in the ship pipeline once v1.14.0 is installed.

## PR SD — `statusDocs`: reconcile project-declared status surfaces every ship (SHIPPED v1.9.0, #54)

**Restated request:** Dogfooding flow on a consumer iOS app surfaced a doc-currency gap: two sub-PRs into a phase, its **`CLAUDE.md` + `README.md`** still read "Phase 2 — HealthKit is next" while Phase 2's first two sub-PRs had already merged. `CLAUDE.md` auto-loads into every session, so a cold agent reads stale orientation and picks the wrong next action. The forward-looking docs `/flow:ship` *does* reconcile (`planPath` "Current Focus", `roadmapPath` "Now") were correct — the drift lived in status surfaces flow has no way to know about. Fix: a **project-declarable** mechanism so any status-bearing surface gets reconciled + gated every ship. (Flow can't hardcode `CLAUDE.md` — flow's own repo has none; not every consumer uses one.)

**Mode:** feature (medium) | **Priority: high** (closes a real dogfood-discovered doc-currency hole; FB-0043 lineage — "stale direction should never happen").

**Root cause (three gaps):**
1. **Step 5a** reconciles a *hardcoded* set — `planPath`(Current Focus) + `roadmapPath`(Now). Any other status surface is invisible to it.
2. **Step 5b** asserts only a **version token** resolved from a manifest, and **silently no-ops** (`N/A`) when there's no `plugin.json`/`package.json`. Non-versioned projects get *zero* mechanical doc-currency enforcement; versioned ones get only a version-string check, never a status/phase-narrative check.
3. **No config slot** lets a consumer declare "these files also carry forward-looking status — reconcile them every ship."

**Design (the intended shape, refined to flow conventions):**

- **New slot `flow.config.json.statusDocs`** — array, default `[]` (⇒ existing projects unaffected). Each entry: `{ "path": "CLAUDE.md", "marker": "flow:status" }`. The agent reconciles **only** the region between the HTML-comment fences `<!-- flow:status -->` … `<!-- /flow:status -->` (open = `<!-- {marker} -->`, close = `<!-- /{marker} -->`). `marker` optional, default `flow:status`. A fenced region keeps the edit **narrow + mechanical** (not a CLAUDE.md restructure — respects projects that gate broad CLAUDE.md edits behind a human) and gives Step 5b a concrete target to assert.
- **Step 5a (reconcile):** after plan/roadmap, iterate `statusDocs`; for each, the agent rewrites the marker region so the narrative status matches just-shipped reality (it has that context — same as it does for Current Focus). Declared marker absent from the file ⇒ **loud `⚠️` warning** (declared a surface but didn't fence it), never silent.
- **Step 5b (gate — version-manifest-INDEPENDENT):** runs even with no `plugin.json`/`package.json`. For each entry: **(a)** assert the marker region exists (missing ⇒ BLOCKER with fix hint); **(b)** if this ship *moved status forward* — i.e. the plan "## Current Focus" section OR roadmap "## Now" section changed vs `origin/<default>` — assert each declared region also changed vs base; an un-touched region while status moved ⇒ **BLOCKER**. Keep the existing version-token assertion for versioned projects; this marker-coverage assertion is added for everyone. Empty `statusDocs` ⇒ clean skip (no behavior change). The "moved forward" trigger is scoped to those two sections (not whole-file) to hold false positives down; the residual trade (Current-Focus/Now changed for a non-status reason) is accepted per the request and documented — the fix is a one-region touch.
- **Shared stdlib helper `skills/ship/lib/status-docs.py`** (Fork A, recommended) — pure-text subcommands consumed by 5b **and** doctor (and available to 5a): `entries <config>` (parse the JSON array → `marker\tpath` lines, no jq-object-loop-in-bash — the exact fragility that bit doctor 2.5 under zsh), `region <marker> <file>` (print fenced region; exit 2 if a fence is missing), `check <config>` (per-entry: file exists + both fences present). Git stays in the shell (`git show origin/<default>:<path>` for base content); the helper is pure text → eval-testable. Mirrors the `render-test-plan.py` / `insert-visual-history.py` lib family. *(Fork B = inline bash in both 5b + doctor, matching existing 5b/2.6 duplication. Recommend A: statusDocs is an object-array — jq-in-bash loops are flow's documented footgun — and A gives a regression eval + kills the 5a/5b/doctor triplication FB-0010 warns about. Cost: one lib + one eval.)*
- **`/flow:doctor` Check 2.7** — a **bespoke** check (array-of-`{path,marker}`, NOT the scalar-path shape of the existing Check 2.4 loop over `planPath specPath …` — do not fold `statusDocs` into that loop, per critique Finding 2): for each `statusDocs` entry, assert path exists + contains its declared marker pair (calls `status-docs.py check`). Empty ⇒ clean PASS. Non-empty + missing/unfenced ⇒ FAIL with fix hint (so misconfiguration surfaces at setup, not at the next ship's 5b BLOCKER).
- **Eval `evals/run_status_docs_evals.py`** (stdlib) — covers: entries parsing (incl. default marker, malformed JSON loud-fail), region extraction, missing-fence detection, region-changed detection. Wired into CI alongside the other `run_*_evals.py`.
- **Version bump v1.8.0 → v1.9.0** (minor — new slot + new ship/doctor capability). The 5b version-token gate then requires v1.9.0 in roadmap Now + plan Current Focus (Step 5a carries it).
- **Slot-count fan-out 23 → 24** (grep-verified survivors): `doctor/SKILL.md:7`, `README.md:64,170`, `docs/workflow.md:19`, `template/base/bootstrap.sh:166`, `template/base/CLAUDE.md.template:13`, `marketplace.json:8,15`, `plugin.json:4`. (doctor Check 2.5 counts the schema dynamically — auto-correct.)

**Scope (in):**
1. Schema: add `statusDocs` (array of `{path (required), marker (default flow:status)}`, default `[]`) after `visualHistoryPath` in `flow.config.schema.json`. Slot-count fan-out 23→24 across the 8 surfaces above.
2. `skills/ship/lib/status-docs.py` (stdlib) + `evals/run_status_docs_evals.py`.
3. `skills/ship/SKILL.md`: Step 5a `statusDocs` reconcile loop; Step 5b marker-coverage gate (manifest-independent) + keep version-token gate; Config-slots table row.
4. `skills/doctor/SKILL.md`: Check 2.7 + the "all 23 slots" header → 24.
5. `docs/workflow.md`: document the slot + reconciliation behavior in the doc-currency paragraph (§ Step ~251); slot count line 19 → 24; **+ add a `statusDocs` row to the slot table at lines 461–474** (critique Finding 3 — that table is a second shipped slot reference).
6. `template/base/flow.config.json.example`: add a `$comment-statusDocs` + `"statusDocs": []` opt-in stub (discovery — like the diff-patterns comment).
7. Version 1.8.0→1.9.0 (both manifests + README + CHANGELOG v1.9.0 entry). Roadmap § Now + plan Current Focus → v1.9.0 (carried by 5a; asserted by 5b).
8. Docs: `history.md` (**SAFETY** — new ship gate / fail-and-block path), this `plan.md` block (swept at ship), `roadmap.md` (currency + note), `feedback.md` **FB-0054** (reserved), reserved-numbers swept (FB-0054 added, shipped FB-0053 retired).

**Scope (out):** Migrating flow's own repo onto `statusDocs` (flow's CLAUDE.md isn't fenced; default `[]` keeps this PR's own ship a no-op — backward-compat proof). Generalizing markers beyond status regions. Auto-*generating* status prose with no agent curation (agent authors the region; helper/gate only enforce structure + coverage). Whole-file CLAUDE.md reconciliation (narrow fenced region only — the safety constraint).

**Backward-compat invariant:** empty/absent `statusDocs` ⇒ byte-identical behavior to today, on every step (5a skip, 5b version-only, doctor PASS). This PR's own ship proves it (flow declares no statusDocs).

**Spec-walk:**
- [x] `statusDocs` slot in `flow.config.schema.json` (after `visualHistoryPath`; array of `{path,marker}`, default `[]`); slot-count 23→24 reconciled across all 8 fan-out surfaces (no surviving current-state "23"); `claude plugin validate .` clean; schema has 24 properties.
- [x] `skills/ship/lib/status-docs.py` exists (stdlib-only); `entries`/`region`/`section`/`check` subcommands behave per spec; malformed config + missing fence fail loudly (non-zero), never silent. (`section` added in /simplify to absorb the inline awk.)
- [x] `evals/run_status_docs_evals.py` passes (27 checks); wired into CI; covers entries-parse, region-extract, section-extract, missing-fence, region-changed.
- [x] Ship Step 5a reconciles each `statusDocs` marker region to just-shipped reality; absent marker ⇒ loud `⚠️` warning, never silent; malformed config surfaced at 5a (not swallowed by the pipe — staff-review fix).
- [x] Ship Step 5b BLOCKS when status moved forward (plan Current-Focus / roadmap Now changed vs base) but a declared region was left untouched; fires with NO version manifest present; empty `statusDocs` ⇒ clean skip; existing version-token gate preserved. (Validated end-to-end against block/skip/pass scenarios in a real git fixture.)
- [x] `/flow:doctor` Check 2.7 flags a declared-but-unfenced or missing `statusDocs` path; empty ⇒ clean PASS; FAIL hint shows a concrete fence example (staff-review fix).
- [x] `docs/workflow.md` doc-currency paragraph documents the slot + behavior; slot table row added; slot count → 24.
- [x] `template/base/flow.config.json.example` carries the `$comment-statusDocs` opt-in stub.
- [x] Version v1.9.0 across both manifests + README + CHANGELOG; roadmap Now + plan Current Focus reconciled to v1.9.0 (5b version-token passes). (#53's v1.8.1 folded in at rebase.)
- [x] `history.md` SAFETY entry; `feedback.md` FB-0054; reserved-numbers swept (FB-0054 reserved + shipped FB-0053 retired).

## PR V3b — Durable `visual-history.html` record + distill bridge (Deliverable-quality track — ACTIVE, planning)

**Restated request:** "Move forward with V3b" (user, 2026-06-16) — build the **durable visual record** half of V3: a single curated `visual-history.html` (the *picture* companion to `historyPath`), grown over a project's life, plus the **distill bridge** at `/flow:ship` that converts each run's ephemeral verify-build report into one curated entry. Governed by **FB-0042**; spec = `roadmap.md` § Next "PR-2 — track V3b" + blueprint § 4 contract table. Also folds in the **roadmap/plan doc-currency fix** (the `## Now` header still reads "v1.7.0"; `main` is v1.7.1).

**Mode:** feature (medium) | **Priority: high** (closes the Deliverable-quality track's V3; the "nothing reads the per-run report back" gap).

**Why this PR exists:** V3a made the per-run report a two-way human-feedback surface, but it is **ephemeral** — regenerated each iteration, discarded after merge. Without a durable target, the *decision-making* in each report dies (the roadmap's "Cross-run aggregation" gap). FB-0042 settles the two-tier model: ephemeral report **feeds** a durable, curated, committed record via a distill step at ship. This PR builds the durable end + the bridge.

**The two-artifact contract (blueprint § 4 — do not conflate):**
| | Ephemeral report (SHIPPED v1.6.1/1.7.0) | Durable record (THIS PR) |
|---|---|---|
| Slot / path | `verifyReportPath` (temp, **not committed**) | `visualHistoryPath` → `visual-history.html` (**committed**) |
| Lifecycle | per run, regenerated, discarded | append-only, grows over project life |
| Scope | exhaustive (every observation) | **curated** — only decisions that changed the user's read |
| Produced by | `render-report.py` from the buffer | **distilled at `/flow:ship`** — agent-authored, mechanically inserted |
| Screenshots | base64-inlined | **lean asset refs** in `visual-history-assets/`; CSS/SVG fallback |

**Distill source = the findings buffer (not HTML re-parse)).** The load-bearing visual decisions live as structured fields in `verifyFindingsPath` (the same buffer Step 4a already reads): `criteria[].grounding` entries (the rationale that changed the user's read) + resolved `open_questions[routing=this-iteration]`. The distill step reads those + copies the relevant persisted frames from the report's `assets/` dir into the consumer's committed `visual-history-assets/` dir. No HTML scraping. *(Reconciliation with the spec wording: blueprint § 4 + roadmap.md:166 say "distilled from the ephemeral report" — that report is itself rendered from this same buffer, so "the buffer" and "the report's load-bearing decisions" are the same `grounding`/`open_questions` data; reading structured JSON rather than re-parsing rendered HTML is the more robust path and matches how Step 4a already consumes the buffer. Not a contract change.)*

**Decisions locked at this gate (pending your approval of the 3 forks below):**
- **Authoring = agent-curated content, helper-inserted structure** (Fork 1 → A). A small stdlib helper (`skills/ship/lib/insert-visual-history.py`) takes an authored entry block + asset refs and (a) seeds the file from the lib skeleton on first write, (b) **prepends** the entry (reverse-chron), (c) regenerates the anchor-link TOC, (d) asserts no-italic-headings. Curation (which decisions are load-bearing) stays the agent's judgment; structure is mechanical (defends the FB-0010 "consistency depends on author memory" class). Mirrors the existing `render-test-plan.py` / `render-report.py` lib family.
- **Create-on-first-write, not bootstrap-scaffolded** (Fork 2 → A). The skeleton lives at `skills/ship/lib/visual-history-skeleton.html` (a lib asset). The distill step creates `visualHistoryPath` from it on the first qualifying ship when `uiSurface=true`. **No empty doc** for non-UI consumers (FB-0007), no `uiSurface` logic bolted into `bootstrap.sh` (which runs *before* config exists). Honors FB-0042(e)'s *intent* (uiSurface-gated, opt-in) while correcting its literal "scaffold into template/base/core-docs" mechanism — flagged for approval. **If approved, the reversal must land same-PR in the spec sources** (critique Finding 1, FB-0010 fan-out): update `roadmap.md:165` acceptance ("scaffold" → "created-on-first-write by the ship distill step") + FB-0042(e) wording, else a future grep hits a permanent contradiction. A new FB entry records the mechanism reversal (reserve the number first).
- **Validation = evals now; live health-tracker cold-run tracked as follow-up** (Fork 3 → A). Flow's own repo is `uiSurface:false`/`platform:library` → its own ship **correctly self-skips** the distill step. Per the realistic-demos rule, **no fabricated visual-history.html for flow's own non-visual repo.** Correctness is pinned by an eval fixture over a synthetic buffer (legitimate test data, not a user-facing demo); the FB-0016 live validation runs on the health-tracker iOS fixture as a tracked follow-up (exactly as #45's iOS cold run was).
- **Version bump → v1.8.0** (minor — new slot + new ship capability).

**Scope (in):**
1. **Schema slot.** Add `visualHistoryPath` (default e.g. `core-docs/visual-history.html` / flow's own would be `dev-docs/` but flow never writes it) to `flow.config.schema.json`, right after `verifyReportPath`. Description: companion to `historyPath`; uiSurface-gated; created-on-first-write by the ship distill step. **FB-0010 slot-count fan-out 22→23** across: `marketplace.json`, `plugin.json`, `README.md` (×2), `docs/workflow.md`, `doctor/SKILL.md` (Check 2.5 + the "all 22 slots" line), `template/base/CLAUDE.md.template`, `template/base/bootstrap.sh`.
2. **Skeleton lib asset** `skills/ship/lib/visual-history-skeleton.html` — neutral-theme, self-contained, empty-of-entries: `<h1>`, intro paragraph (what this doc is), an empty TOC `<nav>`, an entries container, reverse-chron + no-italic-heading conventions baked into structure. Zero project/brand tokens.
3. **Insert helper** `skills/ship/lib/insert-visual-history.py` (stdlib-only): args = target path, authored entry HTML (stdin or file), optional asset paths to register. Behavior: seed-from-skeleton-if-absent → prepend entry into the entries container → regenerate TOC from entry `<h2 id=...>` anchors → assert no `<em>`/`*...*` in headings (warn+strip or fail). Graceful on malformed target (warn, never crash). Pinned by an eval.
4. **`/flow:ship` distill step** (new **§ 5c**, after 5b, before Commit). Gated: run only when `uiSurface=true` AND verify-build ran at Step 2 AND the buffer has ≥1 load-bearing `grounding`/resolved `open_question`. Reads the buffer; the agent authors ONE curated, decision-centric entry (PR#/date/branch metadata, grounding = user-need + decision-test or design-language/craft rationale, a key before/after, questions carried forward); copies the cited frames into `visual-history-assets/`; calls the insert helper; stages the `.html` + assets. Skip path emits an explicit one-line reason (`no UI surface` / `no load-bearing visual decisions this run` / `verify-build skipped`), never a silent no-op. **Distill-then-discard:** the ephemeral report stays ephemeral.
5. **Step 4a extension** (small): also derive a candidate FB-XXXX from an `open_questions[this-iteration]` the human answered with a *correction* (canonical user-correction FB source) — blueprint § 4. Source-diversity bar still applies.
6. **Eval fixture** (`evals/`, stdlib): (a) insert helper seeds-from-skeleton, prepends reverse-chron, regenerates TOC, strips/flags italic headings; (b) distill output shape over a synthetic buffer; (c) gating — `uiSurface:false` ⇒ no file created; no-load-bearing-decision ⇒ skip. No new dependency.
7. **Doc-currency fix (folded in):** correct `roadmap.md` § Now header (`v1.7.0` → `v1.7.1`) + `plan.md` Current Focus (`v1.7.0` → `v1.7.1`). The Step 5a reconciliation will then carry both to **v1.8.0** at ship.
8. **Version bump 1.7.1 → 1.8.0** (both manifests + README + CHANGELOG).
9. **Docs:** `history.md` (**SAFETY** — new persistence path / commits assets / create-on-first-write fallback), `plan.md` (this block; swept at ship), `roadmap.md` (mark V3b shipped; V4 remains; the § Now currency fix; **+ the line-165 acceptance reversal** if Fork 2 approved), `feedback.md` (**one new FB** recording the FB-0042(e) mechanism reversal: bootstrap-scaffold → created-on-first-write — reserve the number first; otherwise FB-0042 governs). CLAUDE.md sync-table row for the new core living doc (per FB-0042 acceptance).

**Scope (out):** V4 consumer-side proactive-error loop → later. The live health-tracker cold-run validation → tracked follow-up (this PR ships capability + evals). Generalizing the record beyond visual scope to ADR-style decision history → explicitly out (FB-0042: visual-scoped). Auto-rendering entries from the buffer with no curation → out (FB-0042: curated, not a PR dump). Cross-run aggregation/analytics of the record → out (roadmap § Exploration).
